Ogus’s conjecture on F F -isocrystals
Haoyang Guo

Abstract
. In 1984, Ogus conjectured the existence of a canonical F F -isocrystal that enhances the Gauss–Manin connection, for a proper relative rigid space with analytically good reduction. We give a positive answer to this conjecture in full generality, through p p -adic local systems and prismatic methods. Along the way, we introduce a prismatic refinement of the p p -adic Riemann–Hilbert functor and prove a primitive purity theorem for Frobenius modules.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Ogus’s conjecture

Let
K
be a
p
-adic local field with perfect residue field
k
, and let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be the ring of integers.
Recall that a smooth rigid space over
K
has
good reduction
if it admits a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme model over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
More generally, we let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
X_{\eta}
be its generic fiber over
K
.
Then a smooth rigid space
Y_{\eta}
over
X_{\eta}
has
good reduction
(with respect to
X
) if it is the generic fiber of a smooth morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes
f:Y\to X
.
As a given rigid space can have many distinct integral models, which are
far from isomorphic to one another and can have different types of singularity,
it is in principle difficult to determine whether a given rigid space admits good reduction.

A fundamental insight of Grothendieck (
[
22
]
) and Berthelot (
[
3
]
) is that when a proper smooth rigid space
Y_{\eta}
over
X_{\eta}
has good reduction model
Y\to X
, its special fiber, which lives in characteristic
p
, could yield well-behaved algebraic invariants in characteristic
0
: the (relative) crystalline cohomology.
However, when the base space
X_{\eta}
has positive dimension, additional complexities arise
when considering the relative crystalline cohomology due to the possible singularity of the base formal scheme
X
.
Moreover, it is very demanding, in practice,
to ensure that both the integral morphism
f:Y\to X
and the target formal scheme
X
to be smooth simultaneously.
Relatedly, it is not clear whether the algebraic invariants over one integral model
X
can yield companion objects over a
different integral model
X^{\prime}
.

The above were some of the main focuses
of Ogus’s work in
[
38
]
, where he introduced the notion of convergent
F
-isocrystals to enhance crystalline cohomology in the relative setting and to connect it with the Gauss–Manin connections in characteristic

0
.
To this end, the following notion was introduced in
[
38
, §5.1]
.

Definition 1.1
.

A rigid space
Y_{\eta}
over
X_{\eta}
has
analytically good reduction
if
Y_{\eta}
is the generic fiber of a smooth morphism of
p
-adic formal schemes
f:Y\to X^{\prime}
for some integral model
X^{\prime}
of
X_{\eta}
.

In particular, Ogus formulated a conjecture regarding the existence of canonical
F
-isocrystals associated with a relative rigid space with analytically good reduction, which we recall it below.

Conjecture 1.2
.

[
38
, Conj. 5.1]

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
be a proper smooth rigid space with analytically good reduction over
X_{\eta}
.
For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a canonical
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},i}
on
X_{k}
that enhances the

i
-th Gauss–Manin connection of
Y_{\eta}/X_{\eta}
.

To illustrate the difficulty of
1.2
, we first note that in the statement,
the base
X^{\prime}
of the integral smooth morphism
f:Y\to X^{\prime}
may be different from the original integral model
X
.
Even
when the latter assumption is true, the anticipated
F
-isocrystal, which is the relative crystalline cohomology, should be both
independent
of the reduction
f

and
functorial
with respect to the generic fiber
f_{\eta}:Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
.
Here the Frobenius structure in the statement is crucial: by analyzing the geometric properties of convergent isocrystals, it was shown in
[
38
, Thm. 5.7]
that the underlying isocrystal always exists when
X
is smooth.
However, the existence of
F
-isocrystals as in
1.2
was only verified when
\dim X\leq 2
.
Its caninicity, on the other hand, was proved via the cycle
class map of the crystalline cohomology and the crystalline Riemann–Roch theorem, the main result of Gillet–Messing in
[
21
]
(cf.
[
38
, §5]
).
Crucially, both the existence and the caninicity made essential uses of the resolution of singularities for integral models of
X_{\eta}
, which is available if
\dim X\leq 2
.
We also mention that even though this conjecture was
proved in special cases, it found an immediate application: Ogus used it to study the crystalline realization
of the Kuga–Satake correspondence (
[
38
, Thm. 7.3]
), and the
latter result
played a central role in his joint work with Nygaard
[
37
]
, where they proved the Tate conjecture for K3
surfaces of finite heights over finite fields for
p\geq 5
.
The main result of this article settles
1.2
in full generality (see
B
).

1.2.
Prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor

To explain our strategy of approaching
1.2
, we give some background on the general ideas.
As the input of
1.2
are rigid spaces in characteristic
0
, it is natural to consider if the algebraic invariants of special fibers or of integral models can be determined solely by generic fibers.
In the 1970s, Grothendieck raised the question on
whether algebraic invariants in characteristic
0
can provide a necessary condition for determining when a rigid space
Y_{\eta}
over
K

has good reduction (
[
23
]
).
After much effort, this question was precisely formulated in terms of
p
-adic étale cohomology by Fontaine (
[
19
]
), known as the
\mathrm{C_{crys}}
-conjecture
.
Specifically, Fontaine introduced a functor

(1)

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(-):\Rep_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}({\mathrm{Gal}}_{K})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(k),

where
\Rep_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}({\mathrm{Gal}}_{K})
is the category of continuous representations of the absolute Galois group
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
on finite rank
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules, and
\mathrm{Isoc}^{\varphi}(k)
is the category of
F
-isocrystals over
k
.
It is then conjectured in
[
19
]
that the crystalline cohomology of the special fiber
Y_{k}
can be recovered from the étale cohomology of
Y_{\eta}
through the functor
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(-)
, where
Y
is a proper and smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
The
\mathrm{C_{crys}}
-conjecture, first proved by Faltings
[
18
]
in the algebraic setting and then by Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze
[
5
]
for non-algebraic
Y
, has been a seminal theme in
p
-adic Hodge theory from its early stages to modern development.

Along with the
\mathrm{C_{crys}}
-conjecture, Fontaine also introduced the abstract notion of
crystalline representations
, defined as those
p
-adic Galois representations
V
of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
such that
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
can recover the representation
V
.
So a major consequence of
\mathrm{C_{crys}}
-conjecture is that the
p
-adic étale cohomology of a proper rigid space
Y_{\eta}
is a crystalline representation whenever
Y_{\eta}
has good reduction.
In particular, the rational crystalline cohomology of the special fiber of
any
smooth integral model of
Y_{\eta}
depends only on
Y_{\eta}
itself.

On the other hand, a recent breakthrough in
p
-adic geometry is the introduction
of prismatic cohomology by Bhatt and Scholze
[
7
]
, building on their earlier work with
Morrow (
[
5
]
,
[
6
]
).
In
[
7
]
, they
attached to each
p
-adic formal scheme
X
a novel Grothendieck site
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, called the
prismatic
site
of
X
.
The objects of
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
are given by maps
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A)\to X
from
prisms

(A,I)
, that is,
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebras
A
which are
(p,I)
-complete for some invertible ideal
I\subset A
and are equipped with an
endomorphism on
A
lifting Frobenius on
A/p
.
The cohomology of the associated
structure sheaf is called the prismatic cohomology.
One of the key features of prismatic cohomology is its universality, as it encompasses
several important
p
-adic cohomology theories, including étale, crystalline, and de Rham cohomologies.
Moreover, the associated coefficient theory, known as the
prismatic
F
-crystal
, enhances both the Galois representation of the generic fiber and the
F
-isocrystal over the special fiber.
In addition, a fundamental observation of Bhatt and Scholze
[
8
]
is that crystalline
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
are in fact equivalent to prismatic
F
-crystals over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
As a consequence, the
p
-adic étale cohomology of a smooth proper rigid space
Y_{\eta}
over
K
naturally produces prismatic
F
-crystals over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
whenever
Y_{\eta}
admits good reduction.

To fit into the following more geometric discussion, for a finite field extension
K^{\prime}/K
, we recall that the category of
p
-adic Galois representations
\Rep_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}({\mathrm{Gal}}_{K^{\prime}})
is equivalent to the category of
p
-adic local systems over
\Spec(K^{\prime})
, denoted as
\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spec(K^{\prime}))
.
Then we note that for an analytically good reduction rigid space
f_{\eta}:Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}=X^{\prime}_{\eta}
, the fiber at any point
x\in X^{\prime}_{\eta}
has good reduction.
In particular, by the aforementioned results of Faltings and Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze, the stalk of the relative étale cohomology
R^{i}f_{\eta,*}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
at any point
x=\Spec(K^{\prime})
is a crystalline representation modulo torsion.
This motivates us to consider the following natural notion of the crystallinity in the higher dimensional setting through a fiberwise condition.

Definition 1.3
.

For a rigid space
X_{\eta}
, we let
\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})

1
1

1

For the cautious reader on the possible ambiguity of this terminology, see
D
.
be the full subcategory of
pointwise crystalline local systems
, consisting of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems
T
over
X_{\eta}
such that for each finite extension
K^{\prime}/K
and each point
x=\Spec(K^{\prime})\in X_{\eta}
, the stalk
T|_{x}
is a crystalline representation.

By rephrasing the above discussion, we then obtain the following observation, where the subscript
(-)_{\mathrm{tf}}
denotes the torsionfree quotient:

Theorem 1.4

(Faltings, Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze)
.

The local system
(R^{i}f_{\eta,*}\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{tf}}
belongs to
\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})
if
f_{\eta}:Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
has analytically good reduction.

The first main result of this article is that any
p
-adic local system
T
over the rigid space
X_{\eta}
gives rise to a canonical prismatic invariant over a regular integral model
X
, and this prismatic invariant becomes an
F
-crystal of appropriate size when
T
is pointwise crystalline.
To explain the construction, for a regular
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, we consider a full subcategory of
special prisms
in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, denoted as
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.
The category
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
consists of many regular objects (including Breuil–Kisin prisms,
q
-crystalline prisms, and their higher dimensional analogues), covers the original
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, and is closed under finite coproducts.
It in particular has the same crystal category as that of
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
(cf.
Definition

6.1
).
To each prism
(A,I)
, we consider the notion
weak Frobenius module
, defined as a pair
(M,\varphi_{M})
, where
M
is an
A
-module and
\varphi_{M}
is an
A[1/I]
-linear map
\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I]\to M[1/I]
.
We call
(M,\varphi_{M})
a
Frobenius module
if
\varphi_{M}
is an isomorphism, and denote the category of (weak) Frobenius modules over
A
as
\Mod^{(w)\varphi}(A)
.
We then define the category
{\mathrm{wCrys}}^{\varphi}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}})
of
weak prismatic
F
-crystals
over
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
such that its objects are the functors that assign to each
(A,I)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
a weak Frobenius module over
A
(cf.
Definition

6.2
).
Our main result can be stated as follows.

Theorem A

(Canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal)
.

Let
X
be a
p
-torsionfree regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
There is a canonical functor

\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{wCrys}}^{\varphi}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}),\quad T\longmapsto\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}.

It satisfies the following properties:

(i)

Finiteness (
Theorem

6.14
):
The
A
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(A,I)
is finitely presented if the ring
A
is regular and
A/I
is
p
-torsionfree.

(ii)

Crystalline realization (
Section

6.5
):
By evaluating at special crystalline prisms, the functor
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
induces a weak isocrystal with a weak Frobenius structure over
X_{k}
.

2
2

2

Here we recall from
[
29
]
that a
weak isocrystal
on the special fiber
X_{k}
is a functor that assigns to each affine open subvariety
U_{k}
an isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{U_{k}}
such that the pullback morphism
\mathcal{E}_{U_{k}}|_{V_{k}}\to\mathcal{E}_{V_{k}}
is an injection for each open immersion
V_{k}\to U_{k}
.

(iii)

Nygaard filtration (
Section

6.2
):
There is a natural descending and exhaustive
\mathbb{Z}
-filtration on
\varphi^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
which is compatible with the weak Frobenius structure and is eventually
\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
-adic.

(iv)

de Rham realization (
Section

6.4
):
The twisted reduction
\varphi^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
induces a presheaf of filtered
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
, and is equipped with a flat connection with Griffiths transversality either after inverting
p
.
Moreover, the flat connection can be defined integrally if
X
is smooth.

(v)

Crystallinity (
Theorem

6.25
):
Assume
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
The output
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is a coherent and reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal whose étale realization is canonically isomorphic to
T
, and the Nygaard filtration enhances it into a coherent
F
-gauge.

(vi)

Pullbacks (
Corollary

6.44
):
Let
f:Y\to X
be a map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes.
Assume either
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
or
f
is finite étale.
Then
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},-}
is compatible with pullbacks.

(vii)

Pushforwards (
Proposition

6.45
):
Let
g:X\to Z
be a proper smooth map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes, and let
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Then
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},-}
is compatible with the higher direct images up to bounded
p
-power torsions.

(viii)

Compatibility (
Proposition

6.31
,
Corollary

6.37
):
The crystalline realization
(ii)
recovers Guo–Yang’s crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}
in
[
29
]
, and the de Rham realization
(iv)
admits a natural injection into Liu–Zhu’s
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert functor
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
in
[
34
]
.

In light of
A
.
(viii)
, we also call the functor
T\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
the
prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor
.
By combining
A
.(
(ii)
,
(v)
,
(viii)
), the pointwise crystallinity of étale cohomology in
Theorem

1.4
, together with the de Rham comparison theorem of Scholze
[
40
]
, we give a positive answer to Ogus’s
1.2
in full generality.

Corollary B
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
be a proper smooth rigid space with analytically good reduction over
X_{\eta}
.
The crystalline realization of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},(R^{i}f_{\eta,*}\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{tf}}}
is an
F
-isocrystal on
X_{k}
that enhances the
i
-th Gauss–Manin connection of
Y_{\eta}/X_{\eta}
, is independent of the good reduction morphism
f:Y\to X^{\prime}
, and is functorial in the rigid space
Y_{\eta}
over
X_{\eta}
.

To explain the construction of
A
, for simplicity, we consider the special case when
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is a mixed characteristic point; the general construction for an arbitrary special prism
(A,I)
over regular
X
is similar (cf.
Section

6.1
).
Recall, it was first observed by Breuil
[
11
]
and then extended by Kisin
[
33
]
that a crystalline representation
T
gives rise to integral linear algebraic datum, the so-called
Breuil–Kisin module
.
In our terminology, a Breuil–Kisin module is a finitely generated Frobenius module over the prism
(\mathfrak{S},(E(u)))
, where
\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W(k)\llbracket u\rrbracket
such that the Frobenius structure sends
u
to
u^{p}
, and
E(u)
is an Eisenstein polynomial.
Despite being simple itself, the construction of the Breuil–Kisin module associated with
T
is however very technical and non-explicit.
This is in contrast to Fontaine’s original functor
\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(T)
of (
1
) in the 1980s, defined as the Galois invariant of the tensor product
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{B_{crys}}
, where
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
is Fontaine’s crystalline period ring.
So it is natural to ask if a Fontaine-style formula but for Breuil–Kisin modules exists.

A pivotal observation of Beilinson (
[
2
]
) and Bhatt (
[
9
]
) (which was extended to general perfectoid algebras by our joint work with Li in
[
26
]
) is that the period ring
\mathrm{B}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}
admits a geometric interpretation: it is a localization of the
p
-adic derived de Rham cohomology of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
, where the latter is the completion of the ring of
p
-adic algberaic integers.
It in particular suggests a new perspective on Fontaine’s functor, and leads to a much larger pool of period rings by considering other cohomologies of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
.
Our article then offers one such example that is new:
We let
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}
be the relative prismatic cohomology of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
over the Breuil–Kisin prism
(\mathfrak{S},E(u))
introduced by Bhatt and Scholze (
[
7
]
), which naturally admits a Frobenius structure, a
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
-action, and Nygaard filtration after the Frobenius twist.
Let
\mu=[\epsilon]-1\in{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}}}\subset{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}
be a canonical element.
Then the construction of the
A
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(A,I)
for
(A,I)=(\mathfrak{S},E(u))
in
A
is in fact a one-line formula, and yields the Breuil–Kisin module when
T
is crystalline.

Corollary C
.

Let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
of rank
n
.
The Galois invariant

(T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}[1/\mu])^{{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}}

is a finite free weak Frobenius module over
\mathfrak{S}
of rank
\leq n
, and is canonically isomorphic to the associated Breuil–Kisin module when
T\in\Rep^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}({\mathrm{Gal}}_{K})
.

Here we remind the reader that the relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}
lives in cohomological degree zero and can be identified with a coproduct of prisms (
Corollary

2.25
.)
Moreover, since the mod
u
reduction of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}[1/\mu]
is Fontaine’s period ring
\mathrm{B_{crys}}
, the above
\mathfrak{S}
-module has rank
n
if and only
T\in\Rep^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}({\mathrm{Gal}}_{K})
, which yields a new and equivalent definition of the crystalline representation that is of integral nature.
It also worth mentioning that by replacing
(A,I)
with other prisms, it leads to a formula of constructing the prismatic
F
-crystals
directly
out of crystalline
p
-adic local systems, answering a question from Esnault.
This is in contrast to the étale realization functor of prismatic
F
-crystals which is of opposite (or precisely downstream) direction.

To prove
C
and its higher dimensional generalization, the idea is to reduce the problem to the finiteness and the vanishing results of Tate–Sen (for
C
-linear representations) and more generally of Liu–Zhu
[
34
]
(cf.
Theorem

4.14
).
However, this reduction process is one of the most technical components of the article.
Indeed, by extending an observation of Bhatt–Morrow–Scholze in
[
5
]
, the period ring
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}[1/\mu]
can be identified with an (Frobenius, Galois, and filtered) equivariant colimit of double-twisted relative prismatic cohomology
R\Gamma((\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}\{n\})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(-n)
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then the essential work is to understand the Galois structure on the Nygaard filtration, where we make crucial use of a result from Bhatt–Lurie
[
4
]
on the relationship between the absolute and the relative de Rham realizations (cf.
Theorem

5.23
), together with a careful dévissage argument that refines the difference of
\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}
and
\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{S}}
through the Frobenius structure (
Theorem

5.27
).

Remark 1.5

(Pullback injectivity and the rank stratification)
.

As we discussed in
A
.
(vi)
, the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor interacts well with the pullbacks when the local system is crystalline.
In fact, the pullback compatibility extends to arbitrary local systems.
Specifically, given a map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes
f:X_{2}\to X_{1}
and a map of prisms
(B_{1},J_{1})\to(B_{2},J_{2})
such that
(B_{i},J_{i})\in(X_{i})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, there is an induced map of weak Frobenius modules over
B_{2}
:

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B_{1},J_{1})\otimes_{B_{1}}B_{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},f^{-1}T}(B_{2},J_{2}),

where
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{1,\eta})
.
Analogously to the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor in
[
29
]
, we show in
Theorem

6.41
that the map is generically injective when
(B_{i},J_{i})
are special and regular.
In particular, as was first discovered in our previous work
[
29
, Def. 5.11]
, there is a rank function on the Zariski site of
X
together with a canonical
rank stratification
, which measures how the reduction (in the sense of monodromy) varies within the family.
This will be studied in depth in a subsequent work with Ziquan Yang.

1.3.
A primitive purity theorem for Frobenius modules

In the above discussions, one of the major ingredients in the proof of Ogus’s
1.2
(i.e.
B
) is the behavior of the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor at the local system
T=(R^{i}f_{\eta,*}\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{tf}}
, where the latter by
Theorem

1.4
is pointwise crystalline.
In particular, our approach relies crucially on the understanding of the pointwise crystalline local systems.
We recall from our previous joint work with Yang
[
29
]
that when the rigid space
X_{\eta}
has a smooth integral model
X
, a pointwise crystalline local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
is crystalline
globally
: there is an
F
-isocrystal over the entire special fiber
X_{k}
that is associated to the local system
T
.
However, the smoothness of
X
is crucial in
[
29
]
, where we conducted a series of descending arguments for the
F
-isocrystal, and made essential use of its description in terms of a Frobenius module over a noetherian ring, a property that is only true when the special fiber
X_{k}
is non-singular.
In fact, even the notion of (global) crystalline local system was traditionally defined only for good reduction setting, until in
[
29
, Def. 4.4]
where we extended it to general reduction.
So as theoretic advancements, our
A
.
(v)
pushes the good behaviors of the pointwise crystalline local system to the regular reduction setting.
In fact, we show that several variants of the crystallinity for
p
-adic local systems are equivalent to each other.

Theorem D

(
Corollary

6.28
)
.

Let
X
be a
p
-torsionfree regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
The following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

The weak prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is a prismatic
F
-crystal whose étale realization is isomorphic to
T
.

(b)

The local system
T
is crystalline with respect to the integral model
X
: there is an
F
-isocrystal on the special fiber
X_{k}
that is associated to
T
, in the sense of
[
29
, Def. 4.4]
.

(c)

There is a dense open smooth subscheme
U\subseteq X
such that
T|_{U_{\eta}}
is a crystalline local system with respect to
U
.

(d)

The restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
is a crystalline representation for each Shilov point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
of
X_{\eta}
.

(e)

There is a dense open smooth subscheme
U\subseteq X
such that the restriction
T|_{x}
is a crystalline representation for each classical point
x\in U_{\eta}
.

In particular, the above conditions are independent of the regular integral model
X
.

Building on our previous work with Yang
[
29
]
, to obtain
D
, we prove that crystalline local systems with respect to regular integral models satisfy the
purity
, an observation first made by Tsuji in
[
45
]
, reproved by Moon in
[
36
]
for crystalline local systems and smooth integral models, and then extended to the semi-stable local systems by Du–Liu–Moon–Shimizu
[
16
]
.
To prove this property for local systems, the essential ingredient is a statement on Frobenius modules over regular prisms.
To explain, we recall that a given prism
(A,I)
is called transversal if
A/I
is
p
-torsionfree, and is called regular if
A
is a regular ring.
For an ideal
J\subset A
, we let
\mathrm{Ass}(J)
be the set of associated prime ideals of
J
in
A
.
When
A
is noetherian, for each associated prime
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(p,I)
, we let
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be the
p
-complete localization of
A
at
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(p,I)
with the induced Frobenius structure.

Theorem E

(Primitive Purity,
Theorem

3.29
,
Definition

3.22
)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal and regular prism.
Assume
(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(p,I)})
is a tuple of Frobenius modules, where
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\in\Vect^{\varphi}(A[1/I]^{\wedge}_{p})
,
M_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\Vect^{\varphi}(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, and they are isomorphic after base changing to
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]^{\wedge}_{p}
.
There is a canonical Frobenius module
M\in\Mod^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{sat}}(A)
such that

M[1/I]^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},~\text{and}~M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\simeq M_{\mathfrak{p}}~\text{up to bounded $I^{\infty}$-torsion}.

Here
\Mod_{\mathrm{sat}}^{\varphi}(A)
denotes the subcategory of Frobenius modules over
A
that are finite presented and saturated; cf.
Definition

3.3
.
The complete localization
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a two-dimensional regular
\delta
-ring with
(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}},IA_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
a transversal prism, and reduction
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/IA_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a
p
-adic discrete valuation ring with possibly imperfect residue field (
Proposition

2.16
).
We also note that the analogous statement holds true for Frobenius modules over the mod
p^{n}
-reductions, which we prove in
Theorem

3.23
.

The general strategy of proving
Theorem

3.29
is similar to that of Tsuji, Moon, and Du–Liu–Moon–Shimizu and is of elementary nature, in the sense that one constructs the Frobenius module over
A
via the intersections, and proves that it satisfies the expected structural results.
However, unlike in previous work where the reduction
A/I
is smooth or strictly semi-stable, several major properties of Breuil–Kisin modules fail dramatically.
These include the fully faithfulness of the étale realization functor
\Vect^{\varphi}(A)\to\Vect^{\varphi}(A[1/I]^{\wedge}_{p})
(proved by Kisin in
[
33
, Prop. 2.1.12]
and Bhatt–Scholze in
[
8
, Thm. 7.2]
), together with the projectivity of the
p
-inverted localizations which is implicitly pivotal in the purity of local systems.
Indeed, as we illustrate in
Example

3.10
, these pathological behaviors are essentially due to the vast range of possible Frobenius structures on an arbitrary regular ring.
It is in contrast to the case of Breuil–Kisin ring and its higher dimensional analogues in all the prior work, where the Frobenius structure sends a coordinate variable to its
p
-th power and in particular is much simpler.
We however mention that as we prove in
Theorem

3.9
, when the regular prism
A
is special (or precisely is
framed
), the
p
-inverted localizations of a Frobenius module over
A
are finite projective, provided an appropriate pointwise locally freeness condition holds.
Our proof of this locally freeness, on the other hand, is through a new strategy, which in spirit is closer to our previous work on the Frobenius isogeny of the relative prismatic cohomology in
[
28
, Thm. 8.1, Prop. 8.9]
.

Remark 1.6

(Primitivity)
.

We name
E
a
primitive
purity result, with two main reasons:
One is due to the elementary nature of its statement, in comparison to the purity of
p
-adic local systems or of prismatic
F
-crystals.
Another is the observation that the purity results of those more complicated objects are often the consequences of the purity for Frobenius modules.
The latter is illustrated in our proof of
Theorem

6.25
.
In addition, in an upcoming joint work with Inoue and Yang
[
24
]
, we will apply
E
to prove the pointwise criteria and the purity for the log-crystalline local systems that encompass both geometric and arithmetic monodromy.

Finally, we give another application to the extension problem for
p
-divisible groups.
Let
X_{0}
be a finite type regular and flat scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
Z_{0}\subset X_{0}
be a closed subscheme of codimension
\geq 2
(in
X_{0}
) that is supported in the special fiber, and let
G
be a
p
-divisible group over
U_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}X_{0}\backslash Z_{0}
.
It was known by examples of Raynaud–Ogus–Gabber (cf.
[
12
, §6]
) that
G
may not extend to a
p
-divisible group over entire
X_{0}
; in other words, the purity for
p
-divisible groups over
X_{0}
could fail.
This phenomenon was later analyzed by Vasiu–Zink
[
46
]
, where they studied the extension problem of
all
the
p
-divisible groups over a fixed base.
Yet it remains a question on whether a
given

p
-divisible group could extend or not.

Using the results in this article, we provide a method to answer the above question.
We let
X
be the
p
-adic completion of
X_{0}
, which is a regular and flat
p
-adic formal scheme and is geometrically the tubular neighborhood of the special fiber.
By the assumption of
G
and
D
, the relative Tate module
\mathrm{T}_{p}(G)
, regarded as a local system over
X_{\eta}
, is crystalline with respect to
X
.
So by
A
, it yields a canonical coherent prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{T}_{p}(G)}
over
X
of Hodge–Tate weight
[0,1]
whose étale realization is
\mathrm{T}_{p}(G)
.
Using the prismatic Dieudonne theory (
[
1
]
), we then obtain the following linear-algebraic criterion on the extension problem of
G
.

Corollary F

(Extension of
p
-divisible group)
.

In the above setting, the
p
-divisible group
G
extends to
X_{0}
(equivalently, to
X
) if and only if the coherent prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{T}_{p}(G)}
is locally free.

The latter condition admits a very explicit description: by choosing finitely many framed regular prisms
(A_{i},I_{i})
whose reductions
A_{i}/I_{i}
cover
X
jointly (which always exist by
Footnote

3
), it suffices to check that each Frobenius module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{T}_{p}(G)}(A_{i},I_{i})
is finite projective.

Remark 1.7
.

Finally, it worth mentioning that the prismatic site is indispensable in the proof of Ogus’s
1.2
.
An elementary yet fundamental reason is that for any given regular
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, its prismatic site admits regular, hence noetherian, covering objects (
Footnote

3
), which fails very often for the crystalline site of the special fiber.
Relatedly, unlike the special case when
X
is smooth, we are unaware of any method that can prove the purity result for
p
-adic local systems in the generality of
D
without using the prismatic theory.

1.4.
Convention

Unless otherwise mentioned, we will fix a discretely valued
p
-adic field
K
with the ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and the perfect residue field
k
.
We assume any prism used in the article is an object in
W(k)_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
For a prism
(A,I)
, we use
\overline{A}
to denote the reduction
A/I
.
We say that a map of prisms
(A_{1},I_{1})\to(A_{2},I_{2})
is completely (faithfully) flat if the map of the underlying rings
A_{1}\to A_{2}
is
(p,I_{1})
-completely (faithfully) flat.
We also consider a more general notion called the
\delta
-pair
(A,I)
, which consists of a
\delta
-ring
A
together with an ideal
I
.
Given a map of rings
A\to B
such that
A
underlies a
\delta
-pair
(A,I)
, for simplicity we may use
(B,(I))
to denote the induced
\delta
-pair
(B,IB)
.

We let
C
be a fixed complete algebraic closure of
K
, let
\mathcal{O}_{C}
be its ring of integers, so that the absolute Galois group
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
of the field
K
acts continuously on them.
We choose a compatible system
\zeta_{p^{n}}
of
p^{n}
-th roots of unity in
\mathcal{O}_{C}
and let
\epsilon=(1,\zeta_{p},\ldots)
be the corresponding element of
\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat}=\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathcal{O}_{C}
.
Let
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}={\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W(\mathcal{O}_{C}^{\flat})
and
q\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[\epsilon]\in{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}
be the Teichmüller lift.
Then the Galois group
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
acts on
q
via the cyclotomic character
\chi:{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{*}
, such that
g(q)=q^{\chi(g)}
for
g\in{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
.
We set
\mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}q-1
and
\tilde{\xi}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[p]_{q}=\frac{q^{p}-1}{q-1}
.
So by the above explicit formula, the invertible ideal
\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}
is invariant under the action of
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
.

Given a ring
R
and an ideal
I
, unless otherwise mentioned, we use
R^{\wedge}_{I}
to denote the derived
I
-adic completion of
R
.
If
R
is complete under the
I
-adic topology, we use
R\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
to denote the complete polynomial ring over
R
, namely the
I
-adic completion of
R[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
.
If
R
is in addition equipped with a
\delta
-structure, we use
R\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\}
to denote the complete
\delta
-polynomial ring over
R
(i.e. the complete free
\delta
-ring of
n
variables over
R
, in the sense of
[
7
, Lem. 2.11]
).
We also use
R\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\}^{{\mathrm{pd}}}
to denote the
p
-complete divided power polynomial ring over
R
.
To simplify the notation, we sometimes use
R\langle t_{i}\rangle
to abbreviate
R\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
when the context of variables is clear, and similarly for the other variants.
In the special case when
(A,I)
is a bounded prism, we use
A\langle 1/I\rangle
to denote the
p
-complete localization
(A[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}
.

For a characteristic
p
point
x
of a
p
-adic formal scheme, we use
k(x)
to denote the residue field of
x
.
On the other hand, for a characteristic
0
point
x_{\eta}
of a rigid space, we use
K(x_{\eta})
to denote its characteristic zero complete residue field in the sense of rigid analytic geometry.

1.5.
Acknowledgement

1.2
possible is the pointwise criteria of
p
-adic local systems, which was first discovered jointly with Ziquan Yang in
[
29
]
.

F
-crystal directly from the local system was first mentioned to me by Hélène Esnault, during my visit to the University of Copenhagen in January 2023. I am grateful to Hélène for the question and encouragement at that time.

1.2
in his article
[
38
]
, which naturally connects with many of my interests and expertises, and motivates the results in the prismatic theory developed in this article.

2.
Regular prisms

In this section, we analyze the prisms whose underlying rings are regular.
As we will see, such objects cover the prismatic site of a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, and play a crucial role throughout the article.
In addition, we study the localizations and the coproducts of regular prisms in the prismatic site.

We assume the basics of the
\delta
-ring and the prism as in
[
7
, §2, §3]
.

2.1.
Regular prism and framed regular prism

In this subsection, we introduce the notion of
regular prism
, together with a better-behaved notion called the
framed regular prism
.
We show that up to étale localization, there always exists a framed regular prism that lifts the given regular
p
-adic formal scheme.

We first setup a convention.

Definition 2.1
.

We say a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is
regular
if it is
p
-torsionfree and topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, such that for each affine open subscheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subset X
, the ring
R
is regular.

The main construction of this subsection is the following.

Theorem 2.2
.
3
3

3

We thank Bhargav Bhatt for suggesting to us the construction.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme of relative dimension
n-1
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
For each closed point
x\in X
, there is a transversal and orientable prism
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, such that:

(a)

The
p
-adic formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})
is a
p
-completely étale neighborhood of
x\in X
.

(b)

We equip the ring
W(k)\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
with the trivial
\delta
-structure on
t_{i}
. There is a map of
p
-completely étale map of
\delta
-rings
W(k)\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle\to A_{0}
together with a principal invertible ideal
I_{0}\subset A_{0}
such that
(A,I)=((A_{0})^{\wedge}_{I_{0}},I_{0}(A_{0})^{\wedge}_{I_{0}})
.

Before we discuss the proof, we mention that
Footnote

3
in particular implies that any regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is a local complete intersection, a folklore statement that we cannot find in literature.

Corollary 2.3
.

Any regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is also quasi-syntomic over
W(k)
.

In the proof below, by
shrinking
a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
(around a point
x\in X
), we mean replacing
X
by an open dense
p
-adic formal scheme of
X
(that contains
x\in X
).

Proof of
Footnote

3
.

We may assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine and the point
x
corresponds to a maximal ideal
m\subset R
.
By assumption, since the ring
R
is regular, the cotangent space
m/m^{2}
is a
k(x)
-vector space of dimension
n
, where
k(x)
is the residue field of
X
at
x
and is in particular a finite extension of
k
.
So by taking the finite étale base change along
W(k)\to W(k(x))
if necessary, we may assume
k(x)=k
and the map
W(k)\to R\to k(x)
induces an isomorphism on residue fields.
By lifting elements from
m/m^{2}
to
R
, we can then produce a map of
p
-complete
W=W(k)
-algebras

\psi:W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle\longrightarrow R,

where the image of
\{t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\}
forms a
k
-basis of
m/m^{2}
.
Moreover, by taking the base change along the complete localization map
W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle\to W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
and by Cohen’s structure theorem
[
43
,
Tag 00NO
]
, the above induces a surjection

\widehat{\psi}:W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket\longrightarrow R^{\wedge}_{m},

such that the ideal
\ker(\widehat{\psi})
is principally generated by a regular element
f\in(p,t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)\subset W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
(
[
43
,
Tag 00NR
]
,
[
43
,
Tag 00NQ
]
).
Here we note that the ring
W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
naturally admits a
\delta
-structure, defined by
\delta(t_{i})=0
(or equivalently, its Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket}
sends
t_{i}
onto
t_{i}^{p}
and extends Witt vector Frobenius of
W
).

Claim 2.4
.

The
\delta
-pair
(W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket,\ker(\widehat{\psi}))
is a prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

Proof of
2.4
.

Since any pseudo-uniformizer of the ring
R
is contained in the maximal ideal
m\subset R
, the choice of
t_{i}
implies that there is an element
g\in(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)\subset W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
such that
\widehat{\psi}(g)=p
.
This shows that the element
p-g\in\ker(\widehat{\psi})
.
Note that since
g\in(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
, the element
\delta(p-g)=\frac{(p-\varphi(g))-(p-g)^{p}}{p}
, regarded as a function in
t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}
, satisfies the condition that its evaluation at
(t_{1}=1,\ldots,t_{n}=1)
is the number
(1-p^{p-1})
.
In particular, the element
\delta(p-g)
is a unit in
W\llbracket t_{1}-1\,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
, and
p-g\in\ker(\widehat{\psi})
is a distinguished element (in the sense of
[
7
, Def. 2.19]
) in the ring
W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
.
As a consequence, since the generator
f
is contained in
\mathrm{rad}(W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket)
, by
[
7
, Lem. 2.24]
the element
f
is a distinguished element as well, and the pair
(W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket,(f)=\ker(\widehat{\psi}))
is a prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
∎

We then claim that
\psi
is a surjection after restricted onto a
p
-complete open neighborhood of
x\in X
.
To see this, by applying Zariski’s Main Theorem (
[
43
,
Tag 05K0
]
) at the mod
p
reduction of the map
\psi
, there is an open neighborhood of the point
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
, which is a
p
-adic formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)\subseteq{\mathrm{Spf}}(W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
, such that the restriction
\psi|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)}
is a finite morphism.
We let
C
be the cokernel of the map
\psi|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)}\colon S\to(S\otimes_{W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle}R)^{\wedge}_{p}
.
Then by the finiteness of
\psi|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)}
, we see
C
is a finite (and thus
p
-complete)
S
-module.
Notice that by the first paragraph, the flat base change
C\otimes_{S}W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
vanishes.
As a consequence, since
W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
is faithfully flat over the localization
S_{(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)}
, we see there is a Zariski open neighborhood
V
of the point
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
in
\Spec(S)
such that
C|_{V}=0
and the map
\psi|_{V}
is a surjection.
By taking the
p
-completion of the scheme
V
, we get a
p
-complete open neighborhood
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})
of
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
inside
{\mathrm{Spf}}(W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
such that the restriction
\psi|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})}:A_{0}\to(R\otimes_{W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
is a surjection.
Moreover, its complete localization at
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
is a regular surjection of codimension one onto the complete local ring of
R^{\wedge}_{m}
.

Next we show that by shrinking
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})
around the point
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
in
{\mathrm{Spf}}(W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle)
if necessary,
the kernel ideal
I_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ker(\psi|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})})
is an invertible sheaf generated by a regular element.
For this, we first note that up to shrinking
A_{0}
by a
p
-complete open neighborhood of
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
if necessary, the ideal
I_{0}
is invertible: this is because its base change along the complete localization map
A_{0}\to W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
is generated by one regular element and is in particular invertible, so by the similar approximation mentioned in the first paragraph above,
4
4

4

More concretely, both the kernel and the cokernel of the map
I_{0}\otimes\Hom_{A_{0}}(I_{0},A_{0})\to A_{0}
vanish after the complete localization, so they vanish after shrinking around the point
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
.

there is a
p
-complete open neighborhood of
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
in
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})
such that
I_{0}
is invertible.
As the ideal
I_{0}
is Zariski locally principal, we may further assume the invertible sheaf
I_{0}
is generated by one element, which we assume for now.

Now, by inheriting the
\delta
-structure along the
p
-completely étale map from
W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
(
[
7
, Lem. 2.18]
), we obtain an orientable
\delta
-pair
(A_{0},I_{0})
together with a
p
-completely étale map of
\delta
-rings
W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle\to A_{0}
.
We let
(A,I\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}I_{0}A)
be the
\delta
-pair defined by the
I_{0}
-adic completion of
(A_{0},I_{0})
.
Since the completion preserves the regularity, we know the ring
A
is regular.
So to show that
(A,I)
is in fact a prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, by
[
7
, Lem. 3.1, Def. 3.2]
, it suffices to show that the ideal
p\cdot A/(I,\varphi(I))
vanishes up to shrinking around the point
(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1)
.
This again follows from the observation that its flat base change along the complete localization
A\to W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket
vanishes by
2.4
. ∎

Argued verbatically as the proof of
Footnote

3
, we obtain the following analogue of framed regular prism over a complete regular local ring of mixed characteristic.

Corollary 2.5

(Regular prism of complete regular local ring)
.

Let
R
be a
p
-torsionfree complete regular local ring with residue field
k
.
There is a prism
(W\llbracket t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{n}-1\rrbracket,(f))
with
\delta(t_{i})=0
, such that
\overline{A}
is a finite étale extension of
R
.

We give some names for the prisms whose underlying rings are regular.

Definition 2.6
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(A,I)
be a bounded prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

(i)

We say
(A,I)
is a
regular prism
if
A
is a regular ring.

(ii)

We say
(A,I)
is a
framed regular prism
if there is a map of
\delta
-pairs
(A_{0},I_{0})\to(A,I)
together with a finite set of elements
\Sigma\subset A
, such that

•

I_{0}
is a principal invertible ideal, with
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0}/I_{0})
being
p
-completely étale over
X
.

•

A_{0}
is
p
-completely étale over
W(k)\langle t_{a}|~a\in\Sigma\rangle
and is equipped with a
\delta
-structure that is compatible with
W(k)
with
\delta(t_{a})=0
.

•

The prism
(A,I)
is the
I_{0}
-completion of
(A_{0},I_{0})
.

Under the assumption, we call the data
((A_{0},I_{0}),\Sigma)
a
framing
of
(A,I)
.

By definition, we know the prism
(A,I)
in
Footnote

3
is a framed regular prism and admits a framing from
(A_{0},I_{0})
with
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
.

Here we notice that the regularity of a prism puts a very strong constrain on its Frobenius structure.

Proposition 2.7

(Kunz’s theorem for prisms)
.
5
5

5

The statement was proved independently in a recent work of Ishizuka–Nakazato
[
32
, Prop. 3.4]
, and we refer the reader to
[
32
, §3]
for related discussions.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal prism such that
A
is noetherian.
The ring
A
is regular if and only if the map
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
is flat.

Proof.

We first assume
A
is regular.
The assumptions implies that the ring
A
is noetherian,
p
-torsionfree, and classically
p
-complete.
So by
[
43
,
Tag 0912
]
, it is enough to prove that the mod
p^{n}
reduction of the map
\varphi_{A}
is faithfully flat.
Moreover, as observed in
[
6
, Rmk. 4.2]
, it suffices to consider the case for
n=1
.
Note that the mod
p
reduction of
\varphi_{A}
is the Frobenius endomorphism of the noetherian
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
A/pA
.
So by Kunz’ theorem (
[
43
,
Tag 0EC0
]
), it remains to show that
A/pA
is a regular ring.

As the regularity is a local property and can be checked at maximal ideals (
[
43
,
Tag 00OD
,
Tag 0AFS
]
), it suffices to check the regularity of
(A/pA)_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}
, where
\overline{\mathfrak{m}}
is any maximal ideal of
A/pA
.
We let
\mathfrak{m}
be the unique maximal ideal of
A
that lifts
\overline{m}
.
Then by
[
43
,
Tag 00NQ
]
, since
A_{\mathfrak{m}}
is a regular local ring, it suffice to prove that the element
p
, which is contained in
\mathfrak{m}A_{\mathfrak{m}}
by the
p
-completeness of
A
, is not in
\mathfrak{m}^{2}A_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Moreover, by the faithful flatness of the complete localization
A_{\mathfrak{m}}\to A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, it suffice to check the above by replacing
A_{\mathfrak{m}}
by the ring
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

To continue, by
[
7
, Lem. 2.18]
, the complete local ring
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
admits a unique
\delta
-structure compatible with that of
A
.
If
p
is contained in
\mathfrak{m}^{2}A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, then since the
\delta
-structure sends the subset
\mathfrak{m}^{2}A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
into the subset
\mathfrak{m}A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
(
[
7
, Def. 2.1]
), we know
\delta(p)
is an element in
\mathfrak{m}A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
However, as
A
is
p
-torsionfree, we also know that
\delta(p)=\frac{\varphi_{A}(p)-p^{p}}{p}=1-p^{p-1}
, which is a unit in
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and is contradicting to the above.
Hence
p
cannot be contained in the ideal
\mathfrak{m}^{2}A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Now assume that
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
is flat, which, by the surjectivity of the Frobenius endomorphism of
\Spec(A/pA)
, is automatically faithfully flat.
Then by Kunz’s theorem (
[
43
,
Tag 0EC0
]
) we know
A/pA
is regular.
So the claim follows from
[
43
,
Tag 00NU
]
, and the fact that
A
is
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree.
∎

The following statement summarizes the properties of the Frobenius structure of framed regular prisms.

Proposition 2.8

(Frobenius structure of framed regular prism)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

(i)

The Frobenius morphism
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
is finite and faithfully flat.

(ii)

The mod
I
reduction of the perfection map
(A,I)\to(A_{\mathrm{perf}},I_{\mathrm{perf}})
is a quasi-syntomic cover with the shifted
p
-complete cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}/\overline{A}}[-1]
isomorphic to a finite projective module over
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
.

In particular, a framed regular prism is a regular prism.

Proof.

Let
(A_{0},I_{0})
be a framing of
(A,I)
, where
A_{0}
is a
p
-completely étale
\delta
-algebra over
W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
.
We first note that by the assumption, the mod
p
reduction of
A_{0}
is a smooth
k
-algebra.
In particular, the Frobenius endomorphism on
A_{0}/p
is finite surjective and is flat, where the latter follows from Kunz’s theorem (
[
43
,
Tag 0EC0
]
).
So by the
p
-completeness and
p
-torsionfreeness of
A_{0}
, since the endomorphism
\varphi_{A_{0}}
lifts the Frobenius endomorphism of
A_{0}/p
, we see
\varphi_{A_{0}}
is finite and
p
-completely faithfully flat.
Here we note that by the noetherianity of the ring
A_{0}
and
[
43
,
Tag 0912
]
, the map
\varphi_{A_{0}}
is in fact faithfully flat.

We then note that as the ring
A
is the
I_{0}
-adic completion of
A_{0}
, the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{A}
is defined by the completion of
\varphi_{A_{0}}
.
By the finiteness of the homomorphism
\varphi_{A_{0}}
and the noetherianity of the ring
A_{0}
, we know
\varphi_{A}
is isomorphic to the base change morphism
\varphi_{A_{0}}\otimes_{A_{0}}A
.
Hence the map
\varphi_{A}
is finite and faithfully flat as well.

Finally, to calculate the
p
-complete cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}/\overline{A}}
, we recall that the perfection
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},I_{\mathrm{perf}})
is defined as the
(p,I)
-completion of
\colim_{\varphi_{A}}A
.
By the paragraph above, since the Frobenius map
\varphi_{A}
is the
I_{0}
-completion of
\varphi_{A_{0}}
, the perfection map
A\to A_{\mathrm{perf}}
can be identified with the
(p,I_{0})
-completion of the map

A_{0}\longrightarrow\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0}.

In particular, the map of
p
-complete algebras
\overline{A}\to\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
can be identified with the mod
I_{0}
reduction map
\overline{A}_{0}\to\overline{(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}}
, and we get

\mathbb{L}_{\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}/\overline{A}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\overline{(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}}/\overline{A}_{0}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}/A_{0}}\otimes_{A_{0}}^{L}\overline{A}_{0}.

So to finish the proof, it suffice to show that
\mathbb{L}_{(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}/A_{0}}
has Tor amplitude
[-1,-1]
, which follows from the distinguished triangle

\mathbb{L}_{(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}/W}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}/A_{0}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}/W}[1]\otimes^{L}_{A_{0}}(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}.

Here the first term vanishes thanks to the relative perfectness of
(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
over
W
, and the last term is a finite projective module over
(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
that lives in cohomological degree
(-1)
, thanks to the smoothness of
A_{0}
over
W
.
∎

A quick consequence of
Proposition

2.8
and André’s lemma is that framed regular prisms cover the absolute prismatic site.

Corollary 2.9

(Weak initiality)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism such that
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})
is an étale cover of
X
.
Then the prism
(A,I)
covers the absolute prismatic site
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

Proof.

Let
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
be the perfection of
(A,I)
, and let
(B,J)
be any bounded prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
By
Proposition

2.8
, we know the reduction
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
is a quasi-syntomic cover over
X
, so the
p
-complete base change
(\overline{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})^{\wedge}_{p}
is also a quasi-syntomic cover.
Thus by
[
7
, Prop. 7.11]
, we know there is a map of the prisms
(B,J)\to(C,L)
such that
\overline{C}
is a
p
-complete flat cover over
(\overline{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})^{\wedge}_{p}
and hence over
\overline{B}
.
Moreover, by the initial property of the perfect prism
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
, the prism
(C,L)
admits a map from
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
and hence from
(A,I)
.
So in summary, a flat cover of the prism
(B,J)
admits a map from
(A,I)
, which finishes the proof.
∎

By taking the
p
-adic divided power envelope for the surjection
A\to\overline{A}
, one obtains a crystalline prism of the mod
p
fiber
X_{p=0}
that is also a covering object, as we shall explain.

Example 2.10

(Framed crystalline prism)
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be an affine regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism, such that
\overline{A}=R
.
We let
D=D_{A}(I)
be the
p
-complete
p
-adic divided power envelope for the surjection
A\to\overline{A}
, and let
i:A\to D
be the canonical map.
So we get a
\delta
-pair
(D,(p))
, equipped with the induced
\delta
-structure from
A
.
In addition, by precomposing with the faithfully flat Frobenius structure
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
(
Proposition

2.8
) and by applying the mod
p
reduction, we get the following diagram of solid arrows:

(2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R/p.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{mod~}I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Notice that for any generator
x\in I
, we have
\varphi_{A}(x)=x^{p}+p\delta(x)
, which implies that the image of the ideal
I
along the composition
i\circ\varphi_{A}
in (
2
) is zero.
Hence the diagram in (
2
) induces a natural dashed arrow
f:R/p\to D/p
.
Here we note that the above in particular produces a crystalline prism
(D,pD)
with the structure map
f\colon R/p\to D/pD)
in
(X_{p=0})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

Moreover, we claim that the map of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras
f:R/p\to D/p
is faithfully flat.
To see this, we fix a generator
x
of the invertible ideal
I\subset A
, whose mod
p
reduction is a non-zero-divisor in
A/p
(by the assumption of
A
).
Then by taking the pushout of the left corner, the diagram (
2
) can be enlarged into the following

(3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D/p,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R/p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/(p,I^{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{mod~}I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

and by the faithful flatness of
\varphi_{A}
in
Proposition

2.8
, it is left to check that the map
g:A/(p,I^{p})\to D_{A}(I)/p
is faithful flat.

To proceed, by the regularity of the element
x
in
A/p
, we notice that the map
g
is equal to the base change of
g_{0}:\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]/T^{p}\to D_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]}(T)
along the map
\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]\xrightarrow{T\mapsto x}A/p
.
The latter in particular allows us to reduce the question to checking the faithful flatness of the map
g_{0}
.
Notice that since
T
is a non-zero-divisor in
\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]
, by
[
9
, Lem. 3.42]
, there is an increasing exhaustive
\mathbb{N}
-indexed filtration on
D_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]}(T)
, where the
i
-th graded piece is
\varphi_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]}^{*}(\Gamma^{i}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p})
and is in particular finite projective over the ring
\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]/T^{p}=\varphi_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]}^{*}(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
This shows that
D_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]}(T)
is a faithfully flat
\mathbb{F}_{p}[T]/T^{p}
-module, which finishes the proof.

Recall that for a given uniformizer
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
, there is a natural
Breuil–Kisin
prism
(\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W(k)\llbracket u\rrbracket,E(u))\in(\mathcal{O}_{K})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
Its
\delta
-structure extends that of
W(k)
and vanishes at
u
, and its reduction map sends the element
u
onto
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
So a Breuil–Kisin prism is in particular framed.
As we shall recall from
[
15
]
and
[
16
]
, the construction naturally extends to the case when
X
is strictly semi-stable or even smooth, with concrete presentations.

Example 2.11

(Strictly semi-stable prism)
.

Let
R
be the ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rangle/(\prod_{i}x_{i}-\pi)
, so that
R
is strictly semi-stable and thus regular.
Under the assumption, the tangent space of
R
at the origin is generated by the image of
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
in
m/m^{2}
.
We can then form a natural map

\psi:W\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle\longrightarrow R,

sending
\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\}
onto the elements
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.
By rewriting the ring
R
as

W\langle u,s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle/(E(u),\prod_{i}s_{i}-u),

the map
\psi
identifies the ring
R
as the quotient of
W\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle
by the regular element
E(\prod_{i}s_{i})
.
Moreover, we can equip
W\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle
with the
\delta
-structure extending that of
W
such that
\delta(s_{i})=0
, thus yielding the
\delta
-pair
(W\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle,(E(\prod_{i}s_{i})))
over
(\mathfrak{S},E(u))
whose reduction is
R
.
Since
E(u)
is distinguished and has image
E(\prod_{i}s_{i})
in
W\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle
, we in particular know that
E(\prod_{i}s_{i})
is a distinguished element as well.
Hence the
E(\prod_{i}s_{i})
-completion of
(W\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle,E(\prod_{i}s_{i}))
is a framed regular prism with
\Sigma=\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\}
.
Here we note that the construction naturally extends to
p
-complete étale
R
-algebras by
[
7
, Lem. 2.18]
.

At the end of this subsection, we prove that regular transversal prisms of Krull dimension two admits a uniform and explicit description.

Proposition 2.12
.

Let
(A,I)
be a regular and transversal prism such that
A
has Krull dimension two.
There is an isomorphism of prisms
(V\llbracket t\rrbracket,(E(t)))\xrightarrow{\sim}(A,I)
, where

•

the element
E(t)
is an Eisenstein polynomial in
t
;

•

the ring
V
is a mixed characteristic Cohen ring (
[
43
,
Tag 0327
]
) and is a Frobenius equivariant subring in
A
;

•

the element
\varphi_{V\llbracket t\rrbracket}(t)
is divisible by
t
.

Remark 2.13

(Difference with the Breuil–Kisin prism)
.

It is tempting to claim that the prism
(A,I)
in
Proposition

2.12
is more or less a special case of the Breuil–Kisin prism (with possibly imperfect residue field).
For the underlying rings, this is indeed the case by
loc. cit.
.
However, one cannot always arrange
(A,I)
to the case such that
\varphi_{V\llbracket t\rrbracket}(t)=t^{p}
, either via a change of variable or up to a finite extension.
The latter can be seen for example by taking (the reduction of) the perfection of the
q
-de Rham prism
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\llbracket q-1\rrbracket,[p]_{q})
.

Proof of
Proposition

2.12
.

We first notice that the flatness of
\varphi_{A}
implies the flatness of the Frobenius endomorphism of
A/pA
.
So by Kunz’s theorem
[
43
,
Tag 0EC0
]
, the ring
A/pA
is a complete regular local ring of dimensino one in characteristic
p
.
Thus by Cohen’s structural theorem
[
43
,
Tag 0C0S
]
, we have
A/pA\simeq k^{\prime}\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket
for a field
k^{\prime}
in characteristic
p
.
Hence by taking the power series ring over any Cohen ring
V^{\prime}
of the residue field
k^{\prime}
, we get an isomorphism of
p
-complete rings
A\simeq V^{\prime}\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
Here we note that the latter isomorphism may not be Frobenius equivariant.

To proceed, since the ring
A
is of dimension two, the transversal assumption implies that
A
is a complete regular local ring.
So
A
is in particular a unique factorization domain (
[
43
,
Tag 0AG0
]
), and thus the invertible ideal
I
is principal.
Moreover, by the transversal condition again, the quotient
\overline{A}
is a mixed characteristic regular ring in dimension one, and hence a mixed characteristic complete discrete valuation ring.
As the residue field of
\overline{A}
is
k^{\prime}
, the induced composition
V^{\prime}\to V^{\prime}\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket\simeq A\to\overline{A}
is a finite totally ramified extension.
So the image of
t^{\prime}
in
\overline{A}
, which generates
\overline{A}
as a
V^{\prime}
-algebra, is a uniformizer.
In particular, the kernel of the surjection
V^{\prime}\llbracket t\rrbracket\to\overline{A}
is generated by a Eisenstein polynomial
E(t^{\prime})
.

At this moment, we note that for each
n\geq 1
, the pair
(A,\varphi^{n}(I))
with the same delta structure is also a regular prism of dimension two.
In particular, the sequence
\{A/\varphi^{n}(I)\}
is a sequence of extensions of discrete valuation rings.
We let
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
be the perfection of
(A,I)
.
Then the reduction
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
is the
p
-adic completion of the colimit
\colim_{n\geq 0}A/\varphi^{n}(I)
.
Hence the perfectoid ring
S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
is the ring of integers of a perfectoid field extension of
\overline{A}[1/p]
,
and the
\delta
-ring
A_{\mathrm{perf}}
is isomorphic to
W(S^{\flat})
.

We now let
\mathfrak{f}
be the residue field of
S
and thus of
S^{\flat}
, which is perfect and naturally contains both
k^{\prime}
and thus the perfection
k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}}
.
Here by the perfectness of
k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}}
, the inclusion
k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}}\to S^{\flat}
induces a Frobenius equivariant injection
W(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}})\to W(S^{\flat})=A_{\mathrm{perf}}
.
Moreover, there is a Frobenius equivariant surjection
A_{\mathrm{perf}}=W(S^{\flat})\to W(\mathfrak{f})
.
Now we let
V
be the intersection
W(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}})\cap A
inside
A_{\mathrm{perf}}
, which by construction is preserved by the endomorphism
\varphi_{A}
.
Let
\iota:V\to A
be the inclusion map.
Then we can fit
A
into the Frobenius equivariant commutative diagram

(4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(S^{\flat})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(\mathfrak{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

On the other hand, by the explicit presentation
A/pA\simeq k^{\prime}\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket
, we know the intersection
k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}}\cap A/pA
inside
S^{\flat}=A_{\mathrm{perf}}/pA_{\mathrm{perf}}\simeq k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}}\llbracket t^{1/p^{\infty}}\rrbracket
is naturally isomorphic to the subfield
k^{\prime}
.
As a consequence, by the limit presentation
V\simeq\lim_{n\geq 1}\bigl(W(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}})/p^{n}W(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{perf}})\cap A/p^{n}A\bigr)
, we know
V
is also a Cohen ring of the field
k^{\prime}
.
Therefore, via the inclusion map
\iota:V\to A
, we can now replace the previous presentation
V^{\prime}\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket\simeq A
by
V\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket\simeq A
, so that the subring
V\subset A
is preserved by
\varphi_{A}
.

Finally, let
A^{\prime}
be the image of the Frobenius equivariant composition
A\to A_{\mathrm{perf}}\simeq W(S^{\flat})\to W(\mathfrak{f})
in (
4
).
Then by taking the mod
p
reduction, we know the composition
V\to A\to A^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
We let
f
be the image of
t^{\prime}\in V\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket\simeq A
in
V\simeq A^{\prime}
.
By construction, the element
(t^{\prime}-\iota(f))
in
A
generates the kernel of the Frobenius equivariant surjection
A\to A^{\prime}
.
Hence by assigning
t\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}t^{\prime}-f
, we see the ring
A
is isomorphic to
V\llbracket t\rrbracket
, with the induced Frobenius structure sending
t
onto a
t
-divisible element. ∎

2.2.
Localizations and completions

In this subsection, we consider some localizations and completions of a regular prism that will be used later.

We start with an observation on the localization of the reduction of a regular ring.
Below for a finite module
M
over a ring
R
, we let
\mathrm{Ass}_{R}(M)
be the set of associated prime ideals of
M
, and we drop the subscript of the notation when the meaning is clear.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
(A,I)
be a regular prism.

(i)

The reduction
\overline{A}/p\overline{A}=A/(p,I)A
has no embedded prime ideals, and hence the localization
\overline{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}/p\overline{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an artinian local ring for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
.

(ii)

The localization map
\overline{A}/p\overline{A}\to\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\overline{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}/p\overline{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an injection.

Proof.

Part
(ii)
follows by applying
[
43
,
Tag 0311
]
to
M=\overline{A}/p\overline{A}
.
It is left to show that every prime ideal in
\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
is minimal.
The statement is local and can be checked around each closed point of
V(p,I)\subset\Spec(A)
, hence we may assume
A
is a
p
-torsionfree complete regular local ring with a flat Frobenius structure
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
(
Footnote

5
).
Moreover, since
\varphi_{A}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear, by taking the base change along the mod
p
reduction, we know the Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{A/pA}:A/pA\to A/pA
is finite flat as well.
So by Kunz’s theorem
[
43
,
Tag 0EC0
]
, the ring
A/pA
is a complete regular ring in characteristic
p
.
Thus by Cohen’s structural theorem
[
43
,
Tag 0C0S
]
, we have
A/pA\simeq k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
for a field
k
in characteristic
p
.
If the ideal
I\cdot A/pA
is the zero ideal (so that
I=pA
and
(A,I)
is a crystalline prism), then the claim follows from the above structural result.
Otherwise, note that since the power series ring is a unique factorization domain, the non-zero invertible ideal
I\cdot A/pA
is principal, and its generator
\overline{f}
is a finite product of prime elements
\overline{g}_{j}
in
k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
.
We let
g_{j}\in\overline{A}
be any lift of
\overline{g}_{j}
along the surjection
\overline{A}\to\overline{A}/p=k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket/(\overline{f})
.
Then by
[
43
,
Tag 00LB
]
, we have
\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})\subset\{(p,g_{j})_{j}\}
.
Notice that each prime factor
\overline{g}_{j}
generates a height
1
prime ideal in
k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
.
So for each
j
, the Krull dimension of the quotient ring
\overline{A}/(p,g_{j})\overline{A}=k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket/\overline{g}_{j}
is equal to
n-1
.
Hence by the dimensional reason, every element in
\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
is minimal.
∎

For the convenience of our discussion, we introduce the following notations on the complete localizations.

Construction 2.15
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism.

(1)

For each prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
, we let
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be the
p
-completion of the localization
\overline{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and let
L_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the ring
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/p]
.

(2)

By
[
43
,
Tag 05DZ
]
and the equality
\overline{A}/p\overline{A}=A/(p,I)A
, the closed immersion
\Spec(\overline{A})\to\Spec(A)
induces a natural bijection between the two sets of primes ideals

\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ass}_{A}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A}),~\mathfrak{p}\longmapsto\mathfrak{P}.

We let
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be the
(p,I)
-completion of the localization
A_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Proposition 2.16
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

(i)

The natural map
\overline{A}/p^{n}\overline{A}\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is an injection.
When
n=\infty
the target is a finite product of complete discrete valuation rings
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
.

(ii)

For each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
, the pair
(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}},IA_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
naturally admits a structure of a regular prism over
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
that is compatible with
(A,I)
.
Moreover, the canonical map
A/p^{n}A\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is an injection.

Remark 2.17
.

Here we note that by
Proposition

2.16
.
(i)
, each
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is in particular a complete regular local ring of dimension
2
.

Proof.

We start with
(i)
.
By assumption and
Lemma

2.14
.
(ii)
, each localization
\overline{A}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a
p
-torsionfree regular local ring of Krull dimension one such that
p
is contained in the maximal ideal.
So by taking the
p
-adic completion, the ring
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
.
By the
p
-completeness and
p
-torsionfreeness of
\overline{A}
, the injectivity of the map
\overline{A}/p^{n}\overline{A}\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
follows from
Lemma

2.14
.
(ii)
.

For
(ii)
, as the map
A\to A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is a
(p,I)
-completion of a Zariski localization,
the ring
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is regular.
Moreover, by
[
7
, Lem. 2.18]
, the ring
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
admits a unique
\delta
-structure that is compatible with
A
.
The induced
\delta
-pair
(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}},IA_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is then a transversal prism, thanks to
[
7
, Lem. 3.1]
.
In particular, the injectivity of the map
A/p^{n}A\longrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
follows from that in
(i)
.
∎

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal orientable regular prism, with
d
a generator of
I
.
By assumption, the sequence
(d,p)
is regular in the ring
A
.
In particular, by
[
43
,
Tag 062F
]
, since
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathrm{Kos}_{A}(d,p))=0
, we know there is no element
x\in A\backslash pA
such that
dx\in pA
.
Hence the ring
A/pA
has no
I
-torsion and the canonical map
A/pA\to A/pA[1/I]
is injective; similarly for
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, where
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
.
So by combining this with
Proposition

2.16
, we get the following natural commutative diagram of injections into various (complete) localizations

(5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/p^{n}A[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/p^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}},}

which naturally extends to general transversal regular prisms by flat descent.
As we shall see below, the diagram induces an intersection formula of the prism
A
.

Corollary 2.18
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
The canonical maps in diagram (
5
) induce an isomorphism

(6)

A\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigl(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bigr)\bigcap(A/p^{n}A[1/I]).

Proof.

By induction and by taking the limits, it suffices to assume
n=1
.
By taking a flat cover if necessary, we may assume
I=(d)
is principal.
We denote the right hand side as
N
.
Then we notice that the injection (
6
) becomes an isomorphism after inverting the ideal
I
.
If the map (
6
) is not surjective, then there is a non-zero element
x\in N
such that
x\notin A
but
dx\in A
.
We let
\overline{dx}
be the image of
dx
in
\overline{A}=A/IA
, which is nonzero by assumption.
Then the image of
\overline{dx}
in
N/IN
is

d\cdot(\text{image of}~x~\text{in}~N/IN),

which in particular vanishes.
So we conclude the proof by noticing that from
Lemma

2.14
.
(ii)
, we know the map

\overline{A}/p\overline{A}\to N/IN=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}(A)}\overline{A}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p\overline{A}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}

is an injection, which contradicts the existence of the element
x
.
∎

To prepare for the analysis of Frobenius modules, it is useful to equip ourselves with the explicit formula of the complete localization of the framed regular prism.

Proposition 2.19
.

Let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism, let
\mathfrak{m}
be a maximal ideal of
A
with its residue field
k^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}k(\mathfrak{m})=A/\mathfrak{m}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}
be the maximal ideal of
A^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A\otimes_{W(k)}W(k^{\prime})
defined by its canonical surjection onto
k^{\prime}
.
There exists an isomorphism of regular prisms

(W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket,I^{\prime})\longrightarrow((A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}},I(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}),

with the following properties:

•

for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, the endomorphism
\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket}
on
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
sends
x_{i}
to a monic polynomial in
x_{i}
of degree
p
such that

x_{i}|\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket}(x_{i}),~\text{and}~\delta\bigl(\frac{\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket}(x_{i})}{x_{i}}\bigr)~\text{is zero or a unit};

•

the ideal
I^{\prime}
is generated by an element
d\in W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
whose constant term is
p
.

Proof.

We let
(A_{0},I_{0})
be the framing of
(A,I)
, with a
p
-completely étale map of
\delta
-rings
W(k)\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\to A_{0}
, where the
\delta
-structure vanishes on
t_{i}
.
As the ring
A
is
(p,I)
-complete, the ideal
(p,I)
is contained in the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
.
Moreover, since the mod
(p,I)
reduction of
A
is a finite type
k
-algebra, the residue field
k^{\prime}
is a finite extension of
k
.
So we get a commutative diagram of Frobenius equivariant maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(k)\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(k^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k^{\prime}=A/\mathfrak{m},}

which naturally extends to the Frobenius equivariant maps of
W(k^{\prime})
-algebras

(7)

W(k^{\prime})\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\longrightarrow A^{\prime}=A\otimes_{W(k)}W(k^{\prime})\longrightarrow k^{\prime}.

We let
a_{i}
be the image of
t_{i}
in
k^{\prime}
.
Then by taking the formal completion for the maps in (
7
) with respect to the surjections onto
k^{\prime}
, we obtain the Frobenius equivariant maps as below

(8)

W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\longrightarrow(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow k^{\prime},

where each
x_{i}
is the image of the element
t_{i}-[a_{i}]\in W(k^{\prime})\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
.
Note that since the map
W(k)\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\to A_{0}
is
p
-completely étale, the associated map
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\to A^{\prime\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
is formally étale and induces an isomorphism of cotangent spaces at the maximal ideals.
Hence by checking the graded pieces, the map
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\to A^{\prime\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
is a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism.
Here we also note that the Frobenius structure sends each
x_{i}
onto
(x_{i}+[a_{i}])^{p}-\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})}([a_{i}])
, where the latter is a degree
p
monic polynomial in
x_{i}
whose constant term is zero (thanks to the equality
\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})}([a_{i}])=[a_{i}]^{p}
).
The latter in particular implies that Frobenius endomorphism on
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
is a finite flat cover.
Moreover, by expanding the polynomial and the binomial formula, the element
\frac{(x_{i}+[a_{i}])^{p}-\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})}([a_{i}])}{x_{i}}
is of the form
x_{i}h(x_{i})+p[a]^{p-1}
.
