In particular, the element
\delta(\frac{(x_{i}+[a_{i}])^{p}-\varphi_{W(k^{\prime})}([a_{i}])}{x_{i}})
is of the form
x_{i}g(x_{i})+(1-p^{p-1})[a_{i}]^{p(p-1)}
, which is a unit if
a_{i}\neq 0
.
If
a_{i}=0
, then we have
x_{i}=t_{i}
, and thus the
\delta
-stricture of
\frac{\varphi(x_{i})}{x_{i}}=x_{i}^{p-1}
is zero.

The above produces a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism between two regular rings
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\longrightarrow(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
, and we then claim that this map naturally underlies a morphism of prisms.
First of all, since both rings are
p
-torsionfree, their Frobenius endomorphisms uniquely determine
\delta
-structures on them respectively, which are compatible under the map.
Moreover, since
(A,I)
is a prism, we know the element
p
is contained in the ideal
I+\varphi(I)
, and hence in
I(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}+\varphi(I)(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
.
Thus the
\delta
-pair
((A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}},I(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}})
is a prism as well (
[
7
, Def. 3.2.(1)]
).

We let
I^{\prime}
be the unique invertible ideal of
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\longrightarrow(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
that is induced by
I(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
via the isomorphism
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\longrightarrow(A^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m^{\prime}}}
.
Then the
\delta
-equivariant isomorphism makes
(W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket,I^{\prime})
a prism.
Here by the orientability of the prism
(A,I)
(
Footnote

3
), the ideal
I^{\prime}
is principal and can be generated by an element
d
.
We write the element
d
as
up^{n}+g
, with
u\in W(k^{\prime})^{\times}
,
n\geq 0,
and
g\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
.
If
n=0
, then by construction the element
d
is a unit in
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
, which is impossible since the ring
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
is also
d
-adically complete.
On the other hand, by taking the surjection along the equivariant map
W(k^{\prime})\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket\xrightarrow{x_{i}\mapsto 0}W(k^{\prime})
, the containment
p\in(I^{\prime},\varphi(I^{\prime}))
implies that
p\in(up^{n},\varphi(u)p^{n})\subset W(k^{\prime})
, which is possible only when
n=1
.
Hence by multiplying with the unit
u^{-1}
, we see the ideal
I^{\prime}
can be generated by a power series in
x_{i}
whose constant term is the element
p
.
∎

2.3.
Coproduct of prisms and relative prismatic cohomology

In this subsection, we show that the coproduct exists for a large collection of prisms over a regular
p
-adic formal scheme.
In addition, we consider the special case when one of the factors is perfect, where we prove that the relative prismatic cohomology often coincides with the coproduct.

For the convenience of our discussion, we sort out a subcategory of bounded prisms that satisfy some flatness conditions.

Definition 2.20

(Flatness assumption)
.

Let
X
be a
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)
be a bounded prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

•

We say
(B,J)
is a
(faithfully) flat prism
if
\overline{B}
is
p
-completely (faithfully) flat over
X
.

•

We say
(B,J)
is a
flat crystalline prism
if
J=pB
and
\overline{B}=B/pB
is flat over
X_{p=0}
.

Note that a flat prism over
X
is in particular transversal if
X
is
p
-torsionfree.

Theorem 2.21

(Existence of coproducts)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B_{i},J_{i})
for
i=1,2
be two bounded prisms such that each of them admit a map from a flat prism.

(i)

The coproduct
(B_{1},J_{1})\coprod(B_{2},J_{2})
exists in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

(ii)

If
(B_{1},J_{1})
is a (faithfully) flat prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, then the coproduct is completely (faithfully) flat over
(B_{2},J_{2})
.

Assume
(B_{2},J_{2})
is a framed regular prism
(A,I)
for a framing
\Sigma=\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\}
, and
(B_{1},J_{1})
admits a map of prisms
f:(A,I)\to(B_{1},J_{1})
.

(iii)

The underlying
\delta
-ring of
(B_{1},J_{1})\coprod(A,I)
is isomorphic to the complete
\delta
-envelope

B_{1}\{\frac{f(t_{1})\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{1}}{J_{1}},\ldots,\frac{f(t_{n})\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{n}}{J_{1}}\},

and its mod
I
reduction is isomorphic to the complete divided power polynomial
\overline{B}_{1}\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\}^{\mathrm{pd}}
, where
u_{i}
is the image of
\frac{f(t_{n})\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{n}}{d}
and
d
is a generator of
I
.
In particular, the coproduct is completely free over
(B_{1},J_{1})
(cf.
Definition

4.8
).

Proof.

By the categorical description of the coproduct and the fiber product, to prove
(i)
and
(ii)
, it suffices to assume that both prisms are flat prisms.
In addition, by the uniqueness of the coproduct (if exists), it suffices to prove its existence up to Zariski localization.
So we assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine, and the maps to the reductions
R\to\overline{B}_{i}
is (
p
-completely) faithfully flat.
In addition, by the weak initiality of the framed regular prism and by further shrinking
X
Zariski locally if necessary, we may assume that

•

there is a framed regular prism
(A,I)
with
\overline{A}
finite étale over
X
, and

•

there are completely faithfully flat covers
(B_{i},J_{i})\to(C_{i},L_{i})
for
i=1,2
, such that
(C_{i},L_{i})
admit maps from
(A,I)
.

Here we note that by assumption, since
\overline{A}
is finite étale over
X
, and since the composition
R\to\overline{B}_{i}\to\overline{C}_{i}
is
p
-completely faithfully flat and factors through the finite flat map
R\to\overline{A}
, the map
\overline{A}\to\overline{C}_{i}
is also
p
-completely faithfully flat for each
i
.

To continue, we consider the following commutative diagram:

(9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{1}\otimes_{W}B_{2})^{\wedge}_{(p,1\otimes J_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(C_{1},\otimes_{W}C_{2})^{\wedge}_{(p,1\otimes L_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{(p,1\otimes I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{B}_{1}\otimes_{R}\overline{B}_{2})^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(C_{1}\otimes_{R}C_{2})^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

where the horizontal arrows are completely faithfully flat, each square is a complete (derived) pushout diagram, and the arrows in the top row are compatible with the
\delta
-structures.
Moreover, we notice that the kernel of the map
\gamma
is the ideal
(I\otimes 1,t_{1}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{n})
and is generated by a
(p,1\otimes I)
-regular sequence in
(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{(p,1\otimes I)}
.
Thus by the complete faithful flatness of the top row in (
9
), the ideal
\ker(\alpha)
is also generated by a
(p,1\otimes J_{2})
-regular sequence as well.
Hence by
[
7
, Prop. 3.13]
, the prismatic envelope
\bigl((B_{1}\otimes_{W}B_{2})^{\wedge}_{(p,1\otimes J_{2})}\{\frac{\ker(\alpha)}{1\otimes J_{2}}\},(1\otimes J_{2})\bigr)
for the surjection
\alpha
exists, which by its categorical description is the coproduct of
(B_{i},J_{i})
over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
In addition, under the complete faithful flatness assumption of
(B_{i},J_{i})
and by
loc. cit.
, we know the coproduct is completely faithfully flat over each of
(B_{i},J_{i})
.

Finally we prove
(iii)
, for which we may assume
(B_{1},J_{1})=(A,I)
as well.
We let
J_{0}
be the kernel ideal for the diagonal surjection
(A_{0}\otimes_{W}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}\to R
.
So the image of
J_{0}
in
(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{(p,I\otimes 1,1\otimes I)}
generates the kernel ideal
J
for the surjection
(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{(p,I\otimes 1,1\otimes I)}\to R
.
To see the explicit formula of the coproduct in
(iii)
, we consider an auxiliary construction.
As in the previous paragraphs, since the
\delta
-ring
A_{0}
admits a map from the
\delta
-ring
W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
, there is a natural map of
\delta
-pairs

(10)

\left(\bigl(A_{0}\otimes_{W}W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p},(t_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i})\right)\longrightarrow\bigl((A_{0}\otimes_{W}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p},J_{0}\bigr).

So by taking the induced map of the
(p,I_{0}\otimes 1)
-complete prismatic envelopes as in the proof of
(i)
, we get a map of prisms

(11)

\alpha\colon\left(A\{\frac{t_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}}{I_{0}\otimes 1}\},(I_{0}\otimes 1)\right)\longrightarrow\left(\bigl((A_{0}\otimes_{W}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}\{\frac{J_{0}}{I_{0}\otimes 1}\}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{(p,I_{0}\otimes 1)},(I_{0}\otimes 1)\right).

To proceed, we let
v_{i}=t_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}
.
Then we notice that the images of
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}
in
W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\otimes_{W}W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
form a basis of the relative differential
\Omega^{1}_{W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle/W}
.
In addition, since
W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\to A_{0}
is
p
-completely étale, the natural map of coherent
R
-modules
A_{0}\otimes_{W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle}(\Omega^{1}_{W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle/W})^{\wedge}_{p}\to(\Omega^{1}_{A_{0}/W})^{\wedge}_{p}
is an isomorphism.
As a consequence, by checking it via the mod
(I_{0}\otimes 1)
reduction, we see the map
\overline{\alpha}
is an isomorphism of
p
-complete rings, and so is the map
\alpha
.

Now consider the explicit presentation of the mod
I=(d)
reduction.
By unwinding the construction of the prismatic envelope in
[
7
, Prop. 3.13]
, the coproduct
A^{1}
is obtained from the free
\delta
-ring over
(A_{0}\otimes_{W}W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle)^{\wedge}_{p}
on symbols
u_{i}
by imposing the
\delta
-relations

du_{i}=v_{i}.

The higher
\delta
-relations are therefore generated by the identities

\delta^{m}(du_{i})=\delta^{m}(v_{i})\qquad(m\geq 0).

We claim that
\delta^{m}(v_{i})\in(v_{i})
for all
m\geq 0
.
Indeed, since the framing map
W(k)\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\to A_{0}
is a map of
\delta
-rings and we have
\delta(t_{i})=0
, the
\delta
-structure vanishes on both
t_{i}\otimes 1
and
1\otimes t_{i}
.
Therefore

p\delta(v_{i})=\varphi(v_{i})-v_{i}^{p}=(t_{i}^{p}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}^{p})-(t_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i})^{p}.

On the other hand, the polynomial

\frac{X^{p}-Y^{p}-(X-Y)^{p}}{p}\in\mathbb{Z}[X,Y]

vanishes after setting
X=Y
, hence is divisible by
X-Y
.
So by evaluating at
X=t_{i}\otimes 1
and
Y=1\otimes t_{i}
, we obtain
\delta(v_{i})\in(v_{i})
.
Now given an element
r=av_{i}\in(v_{i})
, the identity

\delta(av_{i})=a^{p}\delta(v_{i})+v_{i}^{p}\delta(a)+p\delta(a)\delta(v_{i})

shows that
\delta(r)\in(v_{i})
as well.
Hence the ideal
(v_{i})
is preserved by the
\delta
-structure, and the claim follows by induction.
Since
v_{i}=du_{i}
, we conclude that

\delta^{m}(du_{i})\equiv 0\pmod{d}\qquad\text{for all }m\geq 0.

In addition, notice that
\delta(d)\in A^{\times}
.
At this point the argument of
[
17
, Lem. 2.2.5, Prop. 2.2.8(1)]
applies verbatim: its proof only uses the relations
du_{i}=v_{i}
, the congruences
\delta^{m}(du_{i})\equiv 0\pmod{d}
, and the invertibility of
\delta(d)
.
Concretely, modulo
d
one gets a recursive system of congruences of the form

p\mu_{i,0}\,\delta(u_{i})\equiv u_{i}^{p}\pmod{d},

and for
m\geq 1
, there exists
\mu_{i,m}\in\overline{A}^{\times}
such that

p\mu_{i,m}\,\delta^{m+1}(u_{i})\equiv(\delta^{m}(u_{i}))^{p}+\text{(lower divided-power terms)}\pmod{d}.

In particular the subring generated by the higher
\delta^{m}(u_{i})
is the same as the one generated by the
p
-adic divided powers of the
u_{i}
.
Hence the quotient modulo
d
is exactly the ordinary divided power polynomial algebra on the classes of the
u_{i}
.
∎

Here we note that we can in particular obtain an explicit presentation of the Čech nerve for a given framed regular prism.

Corollary 2.22

(Čech nerve of a regular prism)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme of relative dimension
d-1
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism.
Then the Čech nerve of the
(A,I)
is the cosimplicial object of the prisms

\Delta\ni[n]\longmapsto(A^{n},IA^{n})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(A\{\frac{\delta_{ij}}{I}\}_{0\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq d},(I)\bigr).

Here
\delta_{ij}
is the image of the element
t_{j}\otimes 1\otimes\cdots\otimes 1-1\otimes\cdots\otimes t_{j}\otimes\cdots\otimes 1
, where
t_{j}
in the second term is at the
i
-th tensor factor.

For the later applications, we consider the coproduct of prisms such that one of the factor is perfect.
Our first observation is a reinterpretation of the relative prismatic cohomology using coproducts.

Proposition 2.23

(Coproduct = Relative prismatic cohomology)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid algebra over
X
, and let
(B,J)
be a bounded prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
We let
S_{\overline{B}}
be the
p
-completion of
S\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B}
.
The following two categories are naturally equivalent to each other

•

\{((C,L),~f,~g)~|~(C,L)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},~f\in\Map_{X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\bigl(({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\tilde{\theta})),(C,L)\bigr),~g\in\Map_{X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\bigl((B,J),(C,L)\bigr)\}
;

•

\bigl(S_{\overline{B}}/(B,J)\bigr)_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

In particular, the coproduct
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\tilde{\theta}))\coprod(B,J)
exists in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
if and only if the prismatic site
\bigl(S_{\overline{B}}/(B,J)\bigr)_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
admits an initial prism.

Proof.

We let
(C,L)
be any prism in
\bigl(S_{\overline{B}}/(B,J)\bigr)_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, which by definition is equivalent to a commutative diagram of rings

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\overline{B}}=(S\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

such that the left vertical map is compatible with the
\delta
-structures and the right square is the prescribed pushout diagram.
Then since
S
is a perfectoid ring, by the initiality of the perfect prism
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
in
S_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
(
[
7
, Thm. 3.10, Prop. 7.2]
),
there is an unique map of prisms
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))\to(C,L)
that enlarges the diagram (
12
) to the following

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A_{\inf}(S)}=S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\overline{B}}=(S\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

In this way, in the category
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, we obtain the maps of prisms from
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
and
(B,J)
to
(C,L)
respectively, which are determined uniquely by the diagram in (
12
).
Conversely, given maps of prisms from
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
and
(B,J)
to
(C,L)
respectively in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, we obtain equivalently the following commutative diagram

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A_{\inf}(S)}=S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X},}

where the left vertical maps are compatible with the
\delta
-structures.
The diagram in (
14
) uniquely extends to a diagram as in (
13
), hence produce a prism
(C,L)\in(S_{\overline{B}}/(B,J))_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
∎

Next, we recall the Faltings extension, its splitting, and its relation to the relative prismatic cohomology.

Proposition 2.24
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and let
S
be a perfectoid algebra over
X
.

(i)

The
p
-complete cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{S/X}
fits into a canonical fiber sequence

(15)

S\{1\}[1]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{S/X}\longrightarrow\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{X/W}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}S\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[1].

Assume there is a map of prisms
f:(A,I)\to({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, where
(A,I)
is a framed regular prism with a framing
(A_{0},I_{0})
.

(ii)

The map
f
induces an isomorphism between the shifted
p
-complete cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{S/X}[-1]
and the tensor product
\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}/W}\otimes_{A_{0}}S[-1]
.
In particular,
\mathbb{L}_{S/X}[-1]
is a finite projective
S
-module.

(iii)

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism or a flat crystalline prism.
The derived relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
coincides with the site theoretic relative prismatic cohomology of
S_{\overline{B}}
over
(B,J)
and lives in cohomologically degree zero, with its mod
I
reduction being of Tor amplitude
[0,0]
over
S_{\overline{B}}
.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from the canonical fiber sequence of the cotangent complexes with respect to the maps
W\to\mathcal{O}_{X}\to S
, together with the natural isomorphisms

\mathbb{L}_{S/W}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{S/{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}\simeq S\{1\}[1].

For
(ii)
, we first recall that
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})
is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
W
.
The maps of rings
W\to A_{0}\to{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
then produces a fiber sequence of
p
-complete cotangent complexes

\mathbb{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/W}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/A_{0}}\longrightarrow\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}/W}\otimes_{A_{0}}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[1].

Notice that since
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
is
p
-completely relative perfect over
W
, the first term in the sequence vanishes.
On the other hand, by the
p
-complete smoothness of
A_{0}
over
W
, we know the
\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}/W}
is a finite projective
A_{0}
-module.
Thus by taking the derived base change of the above sequence along the surjection
A_{0}\to\overline{A}_{0}=\overline{A}
and by the base change formula of the cotangent complex, we get

\mathbb{L}_{S/\overline{A}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/A_{0}}\otimes^{L}_{A_{0}}\overline{A}_{0}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}/W}\otimes_{A_{0}}S[1].

Furthermore, we notice that the assumption on the map
f
implies that the structure map
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)\to X
factors through
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{0})
, where the latter is étale over
X
.
So we have
\mathbb{L}_{S/\overline{A}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{S/X}
, which concludes the proof of
(ii)
.

For
(iii)
, we notice that the flatness assumption implies that
S_{\overline{B}}\simeq(S\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}
.
Moreover, there are natural isomorphisms of
p
-complete cotangent complexes

\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\simeq\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/X}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/X}\otimes^{L}_{S}S_{\overline{B}}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p},

where the latter by
(ii)
is isomorphic to the shifted finite projective
S_{\overline{B}}
-module
\mathbb{L}_{S/X}\otimes_{S}S_{\overline{B}}
.
So the statement follows from
[
4
, Var. 4.1.19, Thm. 4.3.6]
(see also
[
4
, Rmk. 4.3.9]
for a related discussion), where the condition
(\ast^{+})
of loc. cit. is satisfied thanks to the aforementioned finite projectivity.
∎

Summarizing the properties of the coproduct where one of the factors being perfect, we obtain the following consequence.

Corollary 2.25

(Coproduct with a perfect prism)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
S
be a perfectoid ring over
X
, and let
(B,J)
be a flat or a flat crystalline prism.

(i)

There is a natural isomorphism of prisms

({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))\coprod(B,J)\simeq\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},I{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr),

which lives in cohomological degree zero with its mod
I
reduction completely (faithfully) flat over
S_{\overline{B}}
. In addition, it is completely (faithfully) flat over
\overline{B}
if
S
is
p
-completely (faithfully) flat over
X
.

(ii)

Assume there is a map of prisms
f:(A,I)\to({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
, where
(A,I)
is framed regular.
Then
f
induces an isomorphism of prisms

({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))\coprod(A,I)\simeq\bigl({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S_{\overline{A}})\{\frac{f(t_{i})\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}}{I}\},(I)\bigr),

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from the discreteness of the cotangent complex in
Proposition

2.24
.
(iii)
and the categorical description of the relative prismatic cohomology in
Proposition

2.23
, together with the flatness in
Theorem

2.21
.
(ii)
.
Part
(ii)
follows from the explicit formula in
Theorem

2.21
.
(iii)
.
∎

Finally, to assist the calculation of the global section for the rational prismatic period sheaf, we record the following formulae on intersections and Frobenius structures.

Lemma 2.26
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism.
Assume
S
is a perfectoid ring that is
p
-completely faithfully flat over
X
.
Then the canonical map below is an equality

(16)

B\longrightarrow(B[1/p])^{\wedge}_{J}\cap{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}.

Proof.

By assumption and
Corollary

2.25
.
(i)
, the sequence
(J,p)
is regular in both
B
and
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, and the both rings are
p
-torsionfree.
So by induction and by taking the limit, the claim follows if the mod
J
reduction

(17)

\overline{B}\longrightarrow\overline{B}[1/p]\cap\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}

is an equality.
We claim that the cokernel
C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\coker(\overline{B}\to\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})
is
p
-torsionfree.
Granting the claim, if the map in (
17
) is not surjective, there exists an element
x\in\overline{B}\backslash p\overline{B}
such that
\frac{x}{p}\in\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
The image of
\frac{x}{p}
in
C
is then a non-zero
p
-torsion element, leading to a contradiction.

To see the claim, we first notice that since
\overline{B}
and
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
are derived
p
-complete, so is the cokernel
C
.
We consider the complete base change of the fiber sequence

\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\longrightarrow(\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow(C\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}.

By
Corollary

2.25
.
(i)
, we know map
\overline{B}\to\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
is
p
-completely faithfully flat.
So to show the
p
-torsionfreeness of
C
, it is equivalent to showing that the complete base change
(C\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}
is
p
-torsionfree.
Note that the first arrow in the sequence above (namely
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\to(\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}
) is split injective, since the arrow admits a canonical section by the multiplication.
Thus
(C\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}
is a direct summand of
(\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}
.
Hence the
p
-torsionfree of
(C\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}
follows from that of
(\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})^{\wedge}_{p}
, where the latter follows from the
p
-torsionfreeness of
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
together with the complete flatness of the morphism
\overline{B}\to\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
∎

Proposition 2.27
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, let
S
be a
p
-completely flat perfectoid algebra over
X
, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
Suppose
(B,J)
is a coproduct of finitely many framed regular prisms.

(i)

The mod
J
-reduction of the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{B}
is injective.

(ii)

For each
a\geq 1
, the mod
\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-a-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})
reductions of the absolute Frobenius maps
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
and
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
are injective.

(iii)

The following complete pushout diagram is also a pullback diagram of injections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{B}}

Proof.

We first assume that
(B,J)=(A,I)
is a framed regular prism.
In this case,
(i)
follows from the fact that the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{A}
is finite flat (
Proposition

2.8
).
For
(ii)
, we let
A_{0}=W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle
be a framing, and let
d_{S}
be a generator of
I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
in
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
.
We first show that both the source and the target are isomorphic to the complete divided power polynomial rings over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a-1}(d_{S})
and over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
respectively.
Recall from
Theorem

2.21
and its proof that the relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}
is isomorphic to the prismatic envelope
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\{\frac{t_{1}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}}{d_{S}}\}
, which can represented as the
\delta
-quotient

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\}/(u_{i}d-(t_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}))_{\delta}.

Now by repeating the arguments for
Theorem

2.21
.
(iii)
, we have

\displaystyle{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}/\varphi^{-a-1}(d_{S})

\displaystyle\simeq{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a-1}(d_{S})\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\}^{{\mathrm{pd}}},

\displaystyle{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})

\displaystyle\simeq{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\}^{{\mathrm{pd}}},

and the map in the statement extends the isomorphism
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a-1}(d_{S})\xrightarrow{\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
and sends
u_{i}
onto
\varphi(u_{i})
.
Notice that in the ring
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}
, by applying the Frobenius at the relation
1\otimes t_{i}=t_{i}\otimes 1-u_{i}d_{S}
and using the assumption that the Frobenius sends both
1\otimes t_{i}
and
t_{i}\otimes 1
to their
p
-th powers, we have

\varphi(d_{S})\varphi(u_{i})=-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\binom{p}{j}(-u_{i}d_{S})^{j}(t_{i}\otimes 1)^{p-j}.

Moreover, the images of both
d_{S}
and
\varphi(d_{S})
in
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
are equal to products of
p
by units (since
a\geq 1
).
So the image of
\varphi(u_{i})
in the mod
\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
reduction
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\}^{{\mathrm{pd}}}
is a degree
p
polynomial of
u_{i}
over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
and is in particular non-zero.
In particular, the induced map
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}/\varphi^{-a-1}(d_{S})\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
identifies the source as the complete divided power polynomial subring generated by the variables
\varphi(u_{i})
over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
.
As a consequence, by considering the mod
\varphi^{-a}(d_{S})
reduction of the image, the absolute Frobenius map
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}
is non-zero on each free divided power variable
u_{i}
and its divided powers, and there are no algebraic relations for different
i
, which implies the injectivity of the map.
The same extends to the Frobenius twisted version by raising
1\otimes t_{i}
to its
p
-th power.

For
(iii)
, we notice that the diagram for
(B,J)=(A,I)
naturally admits a compatible injection into the diagram but for
(B,J)=(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
, where the claim for the latter is automatic since
\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}
is an isomorphism.
So we reduce the question into showing the following being cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{perf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{perf}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}}

which is clear thanks to the explicit coordinate from the framing
W\langle t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\rangle\to A_{0}\to A
.

Now we consider the case for general
(B,J)
.
We then notice that the claims can be fitted into a natural induction: if
(B,J)
is a multiple coproduct of framed regular prisms, Part
(i)
for
(B,J)
follows from Part
(ii)
for
S=\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
and
(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})
, where
(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})
is the other factor such that
(B,J)=(A,I)\coprod(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})
, thanks to the complete projectivity of
A\to A_{\mathrm{perf}}
together with the injectivity of the linearization maps in
Lemma

4.9
.
For
(ii)
, the arguments in the first paragraph above work the same, since the coproduct
(B,J)
admits a similar presentation as a prismatic envelope (cf.
Corollary

2.22
).
For
(iii)
, we notice that the diagram for
(B,J)=(A,I)\coprod(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})
admits a compatible injection into the diagram for
(B,J)=(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})\coprod(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})
.
So it reduces to check that the following diagram is cartesian (where we slightly abuse the notations for coproducts)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\coprod B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{perf}}\coprod B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\coprod B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{perf}}\coprod B^{\prime},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A\coprod B^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}\coprod B^{\prime}}}

which follows from the explicit formula for the reduction of
\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}\coprod B^{\prime}}=\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\overline{B}^{\prime}}/B^{\prime}}}
in the first paragraph above.
∎

3.
Frobenius modules over regular prisms

In this section, we analyze the structure of a Frobenius module over a regular prism.
We prove that certain torsionfree Frobenius modules over a framed regular prism are locally free after restricted onto the analytic locus.
In addition, we prove that Frobenius modules over regular prisms (or their reductions) admit a primitive purity result.

3.1.
Frobenius modules, and generalities on the reflexivity

To facilitate the discussion, we introduce a natural extension of Breuil–Kisin(–Fargues) modules, and discuss some general results on the reflexivity.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
(A,I)
be a prism, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
a\leq b
be two integers.
Let
A^{\prime}
be any of the rings in the collection
\{A,A\langle 1/I\rangle,A/p^{n}A,A/p^{n}A[1/I]\}
.

(1)

A
weak Frobenius module over
A^{\prime}
is a pair
(M,\varphi_{M})
, where
M
is an
A^{\prime}
-module, and
\varphi_{M}
is an
A^{\prime}[1/I]
-linear map
\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I]\to M[1/I]
.
We also require
M
to be finitely generated if
A
is a noetherian ring.

(2)

A weak Frobenius module
(M,\varphi_{M})
over
A^{\prime}
is called a
Frobenius module over
A^{\prime}
if the map
\varphi_{M}
is an isomorphism.

(3)

An
I
-torsionfree Frobenius module
(M,\varphi_{M})
over
A^{\prime}
has
Frobenius height in
[a,b]
if there are containments of
A^{\prime}
-submodules in
M[1/I]
6
6

6

Note that the condition is trivially satisfied for any
a,b
if
A^{\prime}
is either
A\langle 1/I\rangle
of
A/p^{n}A[1/I]
.

(18)

I^{b}\cdot M\subseteq\varphi_{M}(\varphi_{A}^{*}M))\subseteq I^{a}\cdot M.

We use
\Coh^{w\varphi}(A^{\prime})
to denote the category of weak Frobenius modules over
A^{\prime}
, use
\Coh^{\varphi}(A^{\prime})
to denote the subcategory of Frobenius modules over
A^{\prime}
, and use
\Coh^{\varphi,[a,b]}(A^{\prime})
to denote the subcategory of
I
-torsionfree Frobenius modules of Frobenius height
[a,b]
.

Remark 3.2
.

The category of Frobenius modules naturally embeds fully faithfully into the category of weak Frobenius modules.

We sort out a subcategory of (weak Frobenius) modules as below.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal prism, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(1)

A finitely generated module
M
over
A
is called

•

analytically locally free
if the restriction of
M
onto
\Spec(A)\backslash V(p,I)
is locally free;

•

saturated
if it is
p
-torsionfree,
I
-torsionfree, and satisfies the equality
M=M[1/p]\cap M[1/I]
;

•

reflexive
if it is analytically locally free, and the Koszul complex
\Kos_{M}(p,d)
is discrete, where
d
is any generator of
I
up to passing to a Zariski cover.

(2)

A finitely generated module
M
over
A/p^{n}A
is called
reflexive
if
M
is
I
-torsionfree, the localization
M[1/I]
is finite projective over
A/p^{n}A[1/I]
, and the reduction
M/IM
is generically locally free over
A/(p^{n},I)A
.

For
A^{\prime}\in\{A,A/p^{n}A\}
and
*\in\{{\mathrm{refl}},{\mathrm{sat}}\}
, we use
\Coh^{w\varphi}_{*}(A^{\prime})
to denote the full subcategory of reflexive/saturated weak Frobenius modules over
A^{\prime}
, and similarly use
\Coh^{w\varphi}_{*}(A^{\prime})
and
\Coh^{\varphi,[a,b]}_{*}(A^{\prime})
for the full subcategories of weak Frobenius modules or Frobenius modules with prescribed heights.

As we see below, the reflexivity defined here is a special case of the reflexivity in commutative algebra.
Roughly speaking, the condition on the Koszul complex in the reflexivity is equivalent to the torsionfreeness and the saturatedness, and can be detected using the reduction.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
(x,y)
be a regular sequence in a noetherian ring
A
.
Let
M
be a finitely genereated
A
-module.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

the Koszul complex
\Kos_{M}(x,y)
is discrete;

(ii)

M
is both
x
-torsionfree and
y
-torsionfree, and the natural injection
M\to M[1/x]\cap M[1/y]
is an isomorphism.

(iii)

M
is both
x
-torsionfree and
y
-torsionfree, the reduction
M/xM
is
y
-torsionfree, and the reduction
M/yM
is
x
-torsionfree.

Proof.

We first prove the equivalence between
(i)
and
(ii)
.
Assume
(i)
holds for
M
, namely the homology of the following chain complex vanishes for positive homological degrees

\Kos_{M}(x,y)=M\xrightarrow{m\mapsto(xm,ym)}M\oplus M\xrightarrow{(m_{1},m_{2})\mapsto ym_{1}-xm_{2}}M.

If
M[x^{\infty}]\neq 0
, by the finiteness assumptions, there is an element
m\in M
such that
xm=0
and
y\nmid m
.
In particular, the pair
(0,m)
produces a non-zero element in
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Kos_{M}(x,y))
, a contradiction.
By symmetry, the torsion submodule
M[y^{\infty}]
vanishes as well.
If the map
M\to M[1/x]\cap M[1/y]
is not surjective, then there are elements
m_{1},m_{2}\in M
such that
x\nmid m_{1}
and
y\nmid m_{2}
, together with the equality that
\frac{m_{1}}{x^{r}}=\frac{m_{2}}{y^{s}}\notin M
for some pair of positive integers
(r,s)
.
So we get an equality
m_{1}y^{s}=m_{2}x^{r}
of elements in
M
, and the image of
m_{1}
in
M/xM
is a non-zero and
y^{s}
-torsion element.
In particular, the subset
(M/xM)[y]\subset(M/xM)[y^{s}]
is non-empty

7
7

7

If not, the multiplication-by-
y
map
M/xM[y^{i+1}]\to M/xM[y^{i}]
is injective, and by induction we know
M/xM[y^{s}]=0
, a contradiction.
.
Hence by picking an element
m_{2}^{\prime}\in M
whose image in
M/xM
is a non-zero element in
M/xM[y]
, we know there is another element
m_{1}^{\prime}\in M
such that
ym_{2}^{\prime}=xm_{1}^{\prime}
in
M
.
Note that the element
m_{2}^{\prime}\in M
is not divisible by
x
.
As a consequence, we see the pair
(m_{1}^{\prime},m_{2}^{\prime})
represents a non-zero class in
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Kos_{M}(x,y))
, contradicting to our assumption.

Conversely, assume
(ii)
holds for
M
.
The torsionfreeness assumption of
M
implies that the vanishing of
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\Kos_{M}(x,y))
.
In addition, if there is a non-zero class in
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\Kos_{M}(x,y))
, then by definition there are elements
m_{1},m_{2}\in M
such that
ym_{1}=xm_{2}
, yet the element
m\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{m_{1}}{x}=\frac{m_{2}}{y}\in M[1/xy]
is not contained in the submodule
M
.
Notice that the element
m
in contained in both
M[1/x]
and
M[1/y]
.
Thus we have
m\in(M[1/x]\cap M[1/y])\backslash M
, a contradiction.

Finally we prove the equivalence between
(ii)
and
(iii)
, and assume
M
is
x
-torsionfree and
y
-torsionfree.
If
M/xM
has non-trivial
y
-torsion, then there is an element
m_{1}\in M
such that
x\nmid m_{1}
but
ym_{1}=xm_{2}
for some
m_{2}\in M
.
This implies that the element
\frac{m_{1}}{x}=\frac{m_{2}}{y}
is contained in the intersection
(M[1/x]\cap M[1/y])
but not in
M
.
Conversely, if
M\to M[1/x]\cap M[1/y]
is not surjective, then we may find an element
\frac{m_{1}}{x}=\frac{m_{2}}{y}
, for some
m_{1},m_{2}\in M
such that
x\nmid m_{1}
and
y\nmid m_{2}
.
This implies that the image of
m_{1}
in
M/xM
is non-zero, yet the image of
ym_{1}=xm_{2}
is zero.
∎

It worth to mentioning that the proof of the implication from
Lemma

3.4
.
(i)
to
Lemma

3.4
.
(ii)
applies to more general settings.

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
(x,y)
be two elements in a ring
A
, and let
M
be an
A
-module.
Assume
M
is
x
-torsionfree and
y
-torsionfree, and the Koszul complex
\Kos_{M}(x,y)
is discrete.
Then
M\to M[1/x]\cap M[1/y]
is an isomorphism.

We also notice that by
[
43
,
Tag 0AY6
]
, the reflexivity in
Definition

3.3
often coincides with the usual notion in commutative algebra.

Corollary 3.6
.

Let
(A,I)
be a regular and transversal prism, and let
M
be a finitely generated analytically locally free
A
-module.
Then
M
is reflexive in the sense of
Definition

3.3
if and only if it is reflexive in the sense of commutative algebra (
[
43
,
Tag 0AVT
]
).
Moreover, the reflexive hull of
M
is equal to the intersection
M[1/p]\cap M[1/I]
.

By specializing the above results to modules over a regular prism, we obtain the following observations.

Corollary 3.7
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a prime ideal in
{\mathrm{Ass}}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
.
Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, an object in
\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is finite projective over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Proof.

The statement for
n\in\mathbb{N}
is trivial by
Definition

3.3
, and it is left to consider the case when
n=\infty
.
In the latter case, by
Definition

3.3
and
Lemma

3.4
, we know an object
M\in\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is in particular reflexive in the sense of
[
43
,
Tag 0AY6
]
.
Hence we can use
[
43
,
Tag 0B3N
]
to conclude the finite projectivity of
M
over
A
.
∎

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism.
Then the inclusion map
A\to A[1/p]\cap A[1/I]
is an isomorphism, and the completion map
A[1/I]\to A\langle 1/I\rangle
is an injection.

Proof.

By the assumption of
(A,I)
, we know the Koszul complex
\Kos_{A}(d,p)
is discrete, where
d
is a local generator of the ideal
I
.
So the first claim follows from the equivalence of
(i)
and
(ii)
in
Lemma

3.4
.
For the second claim, it suffices to notice that by the equivalence of
(ii)
and
(i)
in
Lemma

3.4
, we know for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
that
A/p^{n}A
is
d
-torsionfree and hence the map
A/p^{n}A\to A/p^{n}A[1/I]
is injective.
So by taking the limit, we get an injection
A\to A\langle 1/I\rangle
, where the source is
I
-torsionfree.
Hence the map
A[1/I]\to A\langle 1/I\rangle
is injective as well.
∎

3.2.
Analytic locally freeness

We prove that a
p
-torsionfree Frobenius module over a framed regular prism is in fact analytically locally free, as long as it satisfies an appropriate pointwise assumption.

Theorem 3.9

(Analytic locally freeness)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism, and let
(M,\varphi_{M})
be a Frobenius module over
(A,I)
.
Assume that for any surjection
(A,I)\to(A^{\prime},IA^{\prime})
onto a transversal regular prism of Krull dimension two, the base change
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}[1/p]
is locally free over
A^{\prime}[1/p]
.
Then the restriction of
M/M[p^{\infty}]
at the open subscheme
\Spec(A)\backslash V(p,I)
is locally free.

Before the proof, it worth to mention that the analytic locally freeness in
Theorem

3.9
does not hold for general torsionfree Frobenius modules over a regular prism or even a framed regular prism.
In the following, we give an example of a framed regular prism
(A,I)
that shares the same underlying ring/ideal with that of the Breuil–Kisin prism, yet the structural results of the Breuil–Kisin modules as in
[
5
, Prop. 4.3]
fail for those over
(A,I)
.
Relatedly, in contrast to the result of Kisin
[
33
, Prop. 2.1.12]
and Bhatt–Scholze (
[
8
, Thm. 7.2]
) on Breuil–Kisin modules, the étale realization functor
\Vect^{\varphi}(A)\to\Vect^{\varphi}(A\langle 1/I\rangle)
is not full.

Example 3.10
.

We let
A
be the ring
W\llbracket t\rrbracket
, and let
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
be the unique continuous extension of
\varphi_{W}
that sends the element
t
onto the polynomial
t(t-p)^{p-1}
.
Since the mod
p
reduction of the map
\varphi_{A}
is finite flat, and since the ring
A
is both
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree, we know
\varphi_{A}
is finite flat as well.
We let
E(t)=t-p
.
Then as the constant term of the element
\frac{(\varphi_{A}(t)-p)-(t-p)^{p}}{p}\in A
is
\frac{-p-(-p)^{p}}{p}
and in particular is a unit in
W
, we know
E(t)
is distinguished in the
\delta
-ring
A
.
Thus the pair
(A,(E(t)))
is a prism (
[
7
, Lem. 3.1]
). Now consider the
p
-torsionfree module
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A/tA\simeq W
.
The Frobenius pullback
\varphi_{A}^{*}M
is naturally isomorphic to the
A
-module
A/\varphi_{A}(t)A=W\llbracket t\rrbracket/tE(t)^{p-1}W\llbracket t\rrbracket
.
In particular, there is a natural isomorphism between the localizations

\varphi_{M}:\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/E(t)]=\bigl(A/tE(t)^{p-1}A\bigr)[1/E(t)]\simeq\bigl(A/tA\bigr)[1/E(t)]=M[1/E(t)].

Hence pair
(M,\varphi_{M})
forms a
p
-torsionfree Frobenius module that is not analytically locally free.

Proof of
Theorem

3.9
.

We let
J
be a non-zero fitting ideal of
M
.
We first claim that
M[1/p]
is finite projective over
A[1/p]
.
By
[
43
,
Tag 07ZD
]
, it suffices to show that the inclusion of
A
-modules
J\subset A
becomes an equality after inverting by
p
.
Here we implicitly use the fact that the formation of the fitting ideal commutes with arbitrary base changes of the ring (
[
43
,
Tag 07ZA
.(3)]
).
Moreover, by the local property of quasi-coherent sheaves and the faithfully flatness of the complete localization, to check
J[1/p]=A[1/p]
, it suffices to do so by taking the base change along the map
A\to A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
for a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset A
.
In addition, by taking the finite étale base change along
W(k)\to W(k(\mathfrak{m}))
and apply the further faithfully flat base change along
A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to A^{\prime\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}
as in
Proposition

2.19
, we may assume that there is a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of regular prisms

(W\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket,I^{\prime})\simeq(A^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}},IA^{\wedge}_{\mathfrak{m}}),

where
(W\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket,I^{\prime})
satisfies the condition as in
Proposition

2.19
.
So the claim follows from
Theorem

3.13
, which will be proved in the rest of the subsection.

We now grant the above claim and assume
M
is
p
-torsionfree.
By the Beauville–Laszlo gluing, to finish the proof, it suffices to show that
M\otimes_{A}A\langle 1/I\rangle
is finite projective over
A\langle 1/I\rangle
.
By
[
43
,
Tag 0F7M
]
, it suffices to show that the fitting ideal
J\otimes_{A}A\langle 1/I\rangle
is equal to
p^{n}A\langle 1/I\rangle
for some
n
.
The latter follows from the more general observation on Frobenius equivariant ideals in a noetherian
\delta
-ring
Proposition

3.11
, applied at
A\langle 1/I\rangle
.
∎

Proposition 3.11

(Local freeness of étale realization)
.

Let
A
be a
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree noetherian
\delta
-ring, and let
J
be an ideal in
A
such that
\varphi_{A}^{*}(J)=J
.
Then
J=p^{n}A
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

By assumption, the ideal
J
is a
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree finitely presented
A
-module.
We let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be the largest integer such that
J\subseteq p^{n}A
.
Then by the
p
-torsionfree assumption and by replacing
J
with the ideal
\frac{J}{p^{n}}
, we may assume
J
is not contained in the ideal
pA
and it suffices to check that the ideal
J
is equal to
A
.

We assume that
J
is not equal to
A
, and let
\overline{J}
be the image of
J
in
A/pA
, so that
\overline{J}
is a non-zero ideal in
\overline{A}
.
By assumption, we have the equality
\overline{J}=\varphi_{A}^{*}(\overline{J})
, as ideals in
A/pA
.
We let
\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}\}
be a set of generators of
\overline{J}
, which is guaranteed by the noetherian assumption.
Then the condition on Frobenius twist of
\overline{J}
implies the equality of ideals

(f_{1},\ldots,f_{m})A/pA=(f_{1}^{p},\ldots,f_{m}^{p})A/pA,

and thus
\{f_{1}^{p},\ldots,f_{m}^{p}\}
is another set of generators of
\overline{J}
.
So by repeating this process, and by noticing that the systems of ideals
\{\overline{J}^{n}\}_{n}
and
\{(f_{1}^{p^{n}},\ldots,f_{m}^{p^{n}})A/pA\}_{n}
are cofinal to each other,
we get the equality of ideals

\overline{J}=\overline{J}^{n},~\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

Hence by Krull’s intersection theorem (
[
43
,
Tag 00IP
]
), we know
\overline{J}=(0)
, a contradiction. ∎

For the convenience of the discussion, we sort out the following explicit conditions on the prism as in
Proposition

2.19
.

Convention 3.12
.

We let
(A,I)
be the regular prism
(V\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket,(d))
, where

•

the ring
V
is an unramified Cohen ring of mixed characteristic that is preserved by
\varphi_{A}
;

•

for each
1\leq i\leq n
, the element
\varphi_{A}(x_{i})
is a monic polynomial in
x_{i}
of degree
p
such that

x_{i}~\text{divides}~\varphi_{A}(x_{i}),~\text{and}~\delta(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})~\text{is either zero or a unit};

•

the ideal
I
is generated by a non-constant power series
d\in W\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
whose constant term is
p
.

Theorem 3.13
.

Let
(A,I)
be the regular prism in
3.12
, and let
(M,\varphi_{M})
be a Frobenius module over
A
.
Let
J
be a non-zero ideal of
A
satisfying the following two conditions:

•

There is an equality of ideals in
A[1/d]
:

(19)

J[1/d]=\varphi_{A}^{*}(J)[1/d];

•

for any surjection
(A,I)\to(A^{\prime},IA^{\prime})
onto a regular prism of Krull dimension two, the base change
JA^{\prime}[1/p]
is either the zero ideal or the entire ring
A^{\prime}[1/p]
.

Then
J[1/p]=A[1/p]
.

The rest of the subsection will be devoted to the proof of
Theorem

3.13
, during which we explore various algebraic properties of the
\delta
-ring
A
and the ideal
J
.
We start with the following simple calculations will be used frequently so we record them here.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
A
be the
\delta
-ring
A
in
3.12
, and let
g\in A
be any element.

(i)

For
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, and
h\in A
, the element
(p^{s}\cdot h+g)^{p^{r}}
is of the form
p^{sp^{r}}\cdot h^{p^{r}}+g^{p^{r}}+p^{s+r^{\prime}}\cdot g\cdot h\cdot g_{1}
, where
g_{1}\in A
, and
r^{\prime}=\begin{cases}r,~\text{if}~s\geq 1;\\
1,~\text{if}~s=0.\end{cases}

(ii)

The element
\varphi_{A}(g)-g^{p}
is of the form
p\cdot g_{2}
for some
g_{2}\in A
, and
g_{2}
is contained in the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
if
g
is so.

Proof of
Lemma

3.14
.

For the first claim, we use the binomial formula and consider each
\binom{p^{r}}{i}\cdot(p^{s}\cdot h)^{i}\cdot g^{p^{r}-i}
, which is divided by
g\cdot p^{s}\cdot h
for any
1\leq i\leq p^{r}-1
.
So the statement for
s=0
follows from the divisibility
p|\binom{p^{r}}{i}
.
If
s\geq 1
, we recall that the
p
-adic valuation of
\binom{p^{r}}{i}=\frac{p^{r}}{i}\binom{p^{r}-1}{i-1}
is
r-v
, where
v
is the
p
-adic valuation of
i
.
So it is left to check that for each
1\leq i\leq p^{r}-1
, we have
si+r-v\geq s+r
, or equivalently
s(i-1)\geq v
.
The latter follows from the inequalities of real functions
v\leq\log_{p}(i)\leq\log_{2}(i)\leq i-1
for
i\geq 0
.

For the first half of the second claim, it follows by the definition of
\varphi_{A}
.
If
g\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
, since the setup of
3.12
implies that the Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
sends the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
into itself, we see the element
\varphi_{A}^{r}(g)-g^{p^{r}}
belongs to
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
as well.
∎

As quick consequences, we have the following results on certain ideals being the entire ring.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
A
be the
\delta
-ring in
3.12
, and let
f=p^{r}+f_{1}
be an element in
A
, where
r\geq 1
and
f_{1}\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
.
Then the ideal
(f,\varphi_{A}(f),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{r}(f))[1/p]
is equal to
A[1/p]
.

Proof.

We prove this by induction on
r
.
If
r=1
, then the element
\frac{\varphi_{A}(f)-f^{p}}{p}
is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{p+\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-(p+f_{1})^{p}}{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{p+\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-(p^{p}+f_{1}^{p}+p\cdot f_{1}\cdot f_{2})}{p}

\displaystyle=(1-p^{p-1})+\frac{\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-f_{1}^{p}}{p}-f_{1}\cdot f_{2},

where
f_{2}
is an element in
A
and the first equality follows from
Lemma

3.14
.
In particular, both
\frac{\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-f_{1}^{p}}{p}
and
f_{1}\cdot f_{2}
belong to the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
.
Hence the element
\frac{\varphi_{A}(f)-f^{p}}{p}
is a unit in
A[1/p]
.

Now let
r>1
.
We claim that the ideal
(f,\varphi_{A}(f))[1/p]
contains an element
g\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p^{r-1}+g_{1}
in
A
for some
g_{1}\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
.
Granting the claim, by taking Frobenius twists, we know
(f,\varphi_{A}(f),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{r}(f))[1/p]
contains
(g,\varphi_{A}(g),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{r-1}(g))[1/p]
, where the latter is equal to
A[1/p]
by the induction hypothesis.
To see the claim, we consider the element
\delta(f)=\frac{\varphi_{A}(f)-f^{p}}{p}
, which is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{p^{r}+\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-(p^{r}+f_{1})^{p}}{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{r}+\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-(p^{rp}+f_{1}^{p}+p^{r}\cdot f_{1}\cdot f_{2})}{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{(p^{r}-p^{rp})+(\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-f_{1}^{p})-p^{r}\cdot f_{1}\cdot f_{2}}{f}

\displaystyle=p^{r-1}\cdot(1-p^{rp-1})+\frac{\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-f_{1}^{p}}{p}-p^{r-1}\cdot f_{1}\cdot f_{2},

where
f_{2}
is an element in
A
and the first equality follows from
Lemma

3.14
.
In particular, we know the element
\bigl(\frac{\varphi_{A}(f_{1})-f_{1}^{p}}{p}-p^{r-1}\cdot f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\bigr)
belong to the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
.
Hence the required element
g
can be obtained by dividing
\delta(f)
by the unit
(1-p^{rp-1})
, which finishes the proof of the claim.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
A
be the
\delta
-ring in
3.12
.
For each
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, the ideal
(\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(d)^{N},d^{N})A[1/p]
is equal to
A[1/p]
.

Proof.

We first notice that by the assumption of
3.12
, the element
d
is of the form
p+g
, where
g\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\subset A
.
So by
Lemma

3.14
, the element
\frac{\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(d)-d^{p^{s+1}}}{p}
is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{p+\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(g)-(p+g)^{p^{s+1}}}{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{p-p^{p^{s+1}}+\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(g)-g^{p^{s+1}}-p\cdot g\cdot g_{1}}{p}

\displaystyle=(1-p^{p^{s+1}-1})+p^{s}\cdot g_{2}-g\cdot g_{1},

where
g_{1}\in A
and
g_{2}\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
So the element
p^{s}\cdot g_{2}-g\cdot g_{1}
is contained in the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
, and the element
\frac{p+\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(g)-(p+g)^{p^{s+1}}}{p}
is invertible in
A[1/p]
.
Hence the ideal
(\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(d),d)A[1/p]
is equal to the ring
A[1/p]
.
Finally, the claim for general
N
follows by noticing that the ideal
(\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(d)^{N},d^{N})A[1/p]
contains a large power of the ideal
(\varphi_{A}^{s+1}(d),d)A[1/p]
.
∎

The following observation narrows down the possibility of the ideal
J[1/p]
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
(A,I)
be the prism in
3.12
, and let
J
be an ideal in
A
satisfying (
19
).
Then either
J[1/p]=A[1/p]
, or
J[1/p]
is contained in
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})A[1/p]
.

Proof.

We assume
J[1/p]
is not the entire ring
A[1/p]
, and prove the other condition by contradiction: namely we assume that there exists an element
f=p^{r}+f_{1}\in J
, where
r\geq 1
and
f_{1}\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})A
.
We first notice that by (
19
), we know
\varphi_{A}(f)\in J[1/d]
.
By taking its Frobenius twist, we get

\varphi_{A}^{2}(f)\in\varphi_{A}^{*}(J)[1/\varphi_{A}(d)]\subset\varphi_{A}^{*}(J)[1/\varphi_{A}(d)d]=J[1/\varphi_{A}(d)d].

Continuing this process, we see the Frobenius twists of the equality (
19
) imply that for each
i\geq 1
, we have the containment

(20)

\{\varphi_{A}^{i}(f),\varphi_{A}^{i-1}(f),\ldots,f\}\subset J[\frac{1}{\varphi_{A}^{i-1}(d)\cdots d}].

So combining
Lemma

3.15
with (
20
), we get the following equality of ideals:

(21)

J[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{r-1}(d)\cdots d}]=A[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{r-1}(d)\cdots d}].

We now let
s\in\mathbb{N}
be the least non-negative integer such that the equality below holds true.

(22)

J[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d}]=A[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d}].

Then by (
22
) and (
19
) we have

(23)

\varphi^{*}(J)[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d}]=A[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d}].

Hence by representing the identity
1_{A}
as elements in the left hand side, there exists a large positive integer
N
such that

(\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d)^{N}\in J[1/p],~\text{and}~(\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d)^{N}\in\varphi_{A}^{*}(J)[1/p].

Next we let
(A_{{\mathrm{perf}}},IA_{{\mathrm{perf}}})
be the perfection of the prism
(A,I)
, where
A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}
by assumption is faithfully flat over
A
.
Since
\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}
is an isomorphism, we get

(24)

(\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d)^{N}\in J[1/p]\otimes_{A}A_{{\mathrm{perf}}},~\text{and}~(\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}^{s-1}(d)\cdots\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}^{-1}(d))^{N}\in J[1/p]\otimes_{A}A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}.

Now we discuss the possible values of the integer
s
.
If
s=0
, then we know from (
24
) that both
d^{N}
and
\varphi_{A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}}^{-1}(d)^{N}
belong to the ideal
J[1/p]\otimes_{A}A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}
.
However,
Lemma

3.16
implies that the ideal of
A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}[1/p]
that is generated by
(d^{N},\varphi^{-1}(d)^{N})
is the entire ring
A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}[1/p]
.
Thus the inclusion
J[1/p]\subset A[1/p]
becomes an isomorphism after taking the base change along the faithfully flat cover
A\to A_{\mathrm{perf}}
, a contradiction to the assumption from the beginning that
J[1/p]\neq A[1/p]
.

So let us assume
s\geq 1
from now.
Under the assumption,
Lemma

3.16
implies that the ideal of
A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}[1/p]
generated by
(\varphi^{s}(d)^{N},\varphi^{-1}(d)^{N})
is the ring
A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}[1/p]
.
On the other hand, by (
24
), both
(\varphi_{A}^{s}(d)\cdots d)^{N}
and
(\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}^{s-1}(d)\cdots\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}^{-1}(d))^{N}
belong to the ideal
J[1/p]\otimes_{A}A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}
.
Hence by taking a linear combination, we know the element
(\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}^{s-1}(d)\cdots d)^{N}=(\varphi_{A}^{s-1}(d)\cdots d)^{N}
belong to
J[1/p]\otimes_{A}A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}
.
The latter, by the faithful flatness of
A\to A_{{\mathrm{perf}}}
again, implies the equality
J[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{s-1}(d)\cdots d}]=A[\frac{1}{p\varphi_{A}^{s-1}(d)\cdots d}]
, contradicting to the minimality of the positive integer
s
.
As a consequence, we see the ideal
J[1/p]
has to be contained in the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})A[1/p]
.
∎

To prepare for the induction procedure later, we notice that the equation (
19
) puts a very strong relationship between the divisibility of
J
by
x_{i}
, and the formation of the distinguished element
d
.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
(A,I)
be the prism in
3.12
, and let
J\subset A
be a non-zero ideal satisfying (
19
).
Assume the ideal
J[1/p]
is contained in the ideal
x_{i}\cdot A[1/p]
for some integer
i
.
Then
\delta(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
is a unit, and there exists an integer
m\in\mathbb{N}
and a unit
u\in A
such that
d=u\cdot\varphi_{A}^{m}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
.
In particular, the element
du^{-1}
is contained in the subring
V\llbracket x_{i}\rrbracket\subset V\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

We first note that the ideal
J
is finitely generated.
So for any fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
, by taking the successive
m
-th Frobenius twists of the equality (
19
), we have

(25)

(\varphi^{m}_{A}(d)\cdots d)^{N}J[1/p]\subset\varphi_{A}^{m+1}(x_{i})\cdot A[1/p],~\text{for}~N\gg 0.

Moreover, the element
\varphi_{A}^{m+1}(x_{i})
can be written as the product

\varphi_{A}^{m+1}(x_{i})=x_{i}\cdot\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}}\cdots\varphi_{A}^{m}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}}).

We then discuss the value of
\delta(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
.
If
\delta(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})=0
, then
\varphi_{A}^{m+1}(x_{i})=x_{i}^{p^{m+1}}
, which is not divisible by any of
\varphi^{i}_{A}(d)
.
So by the fact that the ring
A
is a unique factorization domain, the containment in (
25
) implies that
J[1/p]
is contained in
x_{i}^{p^{m+1}}\cdot A[1/p]
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence
J[1/p]
is in

\bigcap_{m>0}x_{i}^{p^{m}}\cdot A[1/p],

where the latter is zero and contradicts to the assumption of
J[1/p]
.
Thus
\delta(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
can only be a unit.

To continue, we assume the element
d
(which is irreducible) is not divisible by any of the element
\varphi_{A}^{m}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
.
By the assumption of
3.12
, we know each
\varphi_{A}^{j}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
is an Eisinstein polynomial of degree
p^{j}(p-1)
and is in particular irreducible.
Moreover, the non-divisibility assumption on
d
implies that the ideal
J[1/p]
is contained in the ideal
\varphi_{A}^{j}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})\cdot A[1/p]
for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
In addition, for the degree reason, the Eisenstein polynomials
\varphi_{A}^{j}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
are pairwise non-divisible by each other.
Hence the ideal
J[1/p]
is contained in the ideal

\bigcap_{m>0}\varphi_{A}^{m}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})\cdot A[1/p],

and each non-zero element in
J[1/p]
can be divided by infinitely many irreducible elements
\varphi_{A}^{m}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
, contradicting to the noetherianity of
A
.
Thus
d
has to be divided by one of
\varphi_{A}^{m}(\frac{\varphi_{A}(x_{i})}{x_{i}})
.
∎

The following crystalline analogue of
Theorem

3.13
will be used later.

Proposition 3.19

(Frobenius modules over regular crystalline prism)
.

Let
A
be the
\delta
ring in
3.12
, and let
J
be a non-zero ideal in
A
such that

\varphi^{*}_{A}(J)[1/p]=J[1/p].

Then
J[1/p]=A[1/p]
.

Proof.

We first notice that the ideal
J[1/p]
is not contained in the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})[1/p]
.
Otherwise, by taking the Frobenius twists, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

J\subset J[1/p]=\varphi^{m,*}_{A}(J)[1/p]\subset(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))[1/p].

On the other hand, since
J
is contained in
A
, we get

J\subset(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))[1/p]\cap A.

We then claim that the intersection on the right hand side is the ideal
(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))
in
A
.
Granting the claim, since
(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))
is contained in
(p,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})^{m}
, and intersection
\bigcap_{m>0}(p,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})^{m}
in
A
is zero, we see
J=(0)
, contradicting to the non-zero assumption of this ideal.
To see the claim, we prove by contradiction and assume that there is a non-zero element
f\in A
and an integer
i\geq 1
, such that
p^{i}f\in(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))
and
p^{i-1}f\notin(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))
.
Then the image of
f
in the quotient
A/(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))
is a non-zero
p^{i}
-torsion element.
Yet by the assumption in
3.12
that each
\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{j})
is a monic polynomial in
x_{j}
, the quotient
A/(\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{1}),\ldots,\varphi_{A}^{m}(x_{n}))
is finite free over
V
and is in particular
p
-torsionfree, a contradiction.

To proceed, we notice that there exists an element
f=p^{r}+f_{1}
in
J[1/p]
, where
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
f_{1}\in(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
Moreover, by taking Frobenius twists, we know the element
\varphi_{A}^{m}(f)
belongs to
J[1/p]
for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
If
r=0
, then since the element
f_{1}
is topologically nilpotent, the element
f
is a unit in
A
, which implies that
J[1/p]=A[1/p]
.
If
r>0
, the equality
J[1/p]=A[1/p]
follows from
Lemma

3.15
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Now assembling all the ingredients above, we are ready to prove
Theorem

3.13
.

Proof of
Theorem

3.13
.

We prove the statement by induction on
n
.
If
n=1
, as
V\llbracket x_{1}\rrbracket
itself is a transversal regular prism of Krull dimension two, there is nothing to prove.
So we may fix an integer
n>1
, and assume that
Theorem

3.13
is true for the setup of
(n-1)
variables.
We assume that the non-zero ideal
J[1/p]
is not the entire ring
A[1/p]
.
By
Proposition

3.17
, we know the ideal
J[1/p]
is contained in
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})[1/p]
.
We will show that the assumptions on
J[1/p]
would eventually contradict the inductive hypothesis.

To proceed, we first notice that by the setup in
Theorem

3.13
, for each
1\leq i\leq n
, the surjection
(A,(d))\to(A/x_{i}A,(d))
is Frobenius equivariant and induces a natural prism structure on the target.
Then we discuss the following possibilities:

(1)

If the ideal
J[1/p]
is contained in the ideal
x_{n}A[1/p]
, then by
Lemma

3.18
we know both of the following two conditions are true:

•

the ideal
J[1/p]
is not contained in the ideal
x_{1}A[1/p]
;

•

the element
d-p
is not divided by
x_{1}
.

We let
\overline{J}
be the image of the ideal
J
in
A/x_{1}A
, and let
\overline{d}
be the reduction of the element
d
.
Then the
\delta
-pair
(A/x_{1}A,(\overline{d}))
satisfies the same assumptions of
3.12
but with
(n-1)
variables.
In addition, by the above two conditions, we know the ideal
\overline{J}[1/p]
is non-zero, is contained in
(x_{2},\ldots,x_{n})A/x_{1}A[1/p]
(hence a proper ideal in
A/x_{1}A[1/p]
), and satisfies the two assumptions in
Theorem

3.13
.
The latter is impossible, thanks to the inductive hypothesis of
Theorem

3.13
as in the first paragraph.

(2)

If the ideal
J[1/p]
is not contained in
x_{n}A[1/p]
.
We let
\overline{J}
be the image of
J
in
A/x_{n}A
, which is then non-zero, is contained in
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1})A/x_{n}A[1/p]
, and satisfies the two assumptions in
Theorem

3.13
.
At this moment, the mod
x_{n}
reduction of the distinguished element (which we denote as
\overline{d}
) is of the form
p+h
, where
h
is an element in the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1})A/x_{n}A
.
If the element
h
is equal to zero, then
(A/x_{n}A,IA/x_{n}A)
is a regular crystalline prism.
So by the crystalline analogue of the locally freeness in
Proposition

3.19
, the ideal
\overline{J}[1/p]
has to be the entire ring
A/x_{n}A[1/p]
, which contradicts the previous sentence that
\overline{J}[1/p]
is a proper ideal.
Otherwise, the element
h
is non-zero in the ideal
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n-1})A/x_{n}A
, so the prism
(A/x_{n}A,IA/x_{n}A)
satisfies the same assumptions of
3.12
but with
(n-1)
variables.
Thus we reduce the problem to
Theorem

3.13
but for
(n-1)
-variables, under which there cannot exist an ideal
\overline{J}[1/p]
that satisfies the aforementioned properties.
Hence we again obtain a contradiction, which finishes the proof.

∎

Finally, we provide an alternative assumption of the regular prism that prevents the defect we saw in
Example

3.10
.
Roughly speaking, it says that the aforementioned example would never happen if the reduction
\overline{A}
is sufficiently ramified.

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
(A,I)
be a regular prism of Krull dimension two, and assume the absolute ramification degree of the discrete valuation ring
\overline{A}
is not dividing
p-1
.
Then for any Frobenius module
M
over
A
, we have
M[1/p]
is free.

Corollary 3.21
.

Assume the absolute ramification degree of the
p
-adic local field
K
is not dividing
(p-1)
.
Let
(A,I)
be a framed regular prism such that the reduction
A/I
is an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra.
For any Frobenius module
M
over
A
, the quotient
M/M[p^{\infty}]
is analytically locally free.

Proof of
Proposition

3.20
.

We recall the structural result of the regular prism of Krull dimension two in
Proposition

2.12
, and identify
(A,I)
with the pair
(V\llbracket t\rrbracket,(d))
, where the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{V\llbracket t\rrbracket}
preserves the subring
V
.
Then the argument is similar to that of
[
5
, Prop. 4.3]
, which we now explain.
Let
J
be a fitting ideal of
M
, which under our assumption satisfies the equation in (
19
).
By Weierstrass preparation theorem
[
31
, Page 17, Corollary]
, we know the ideal
J[1/p]
is generated by an element
f(t)=\prod_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}(t)^{a_{i}}
, where each
f_{i}
is a monic irreducible polynomial in
t
.
Then by considering the
p
-adic valuation of the solution of each
f_{i}
, we know the only possible irreducible factor of
f(t)
is
t
.
Now we assume
J[1/p]
is not equal to
A[1/p]
.
Then the equality (
19
) implies that
\varphi_{A}(t)^{a_{1}}=t^{a_{1}}\cdot d^{s}\cdot u
for some integer
s\in\mathbb{N}
and some unit
u\in A^{\times}
.
In particular, the irreducible element
d
divides the element
\frac{\varphi_{A}(t)}{t}
.
However, by
Proposition

2.12
, since
d
is an Eisinstein polynomial in
t
and since the mod
p
reduction of
\frac{\varphi_{A}(t)}{t}
is
t^{p-1}
, the divisibility implies that
\deg(d)|(p-1)
.
Hence the ramification degree of the field extension
\overline{A}[1/p]
over
V[1/p]
is dividing
(p-1)
, contradicting to the assumption on the ramification index of
\overline{A}
in
Theorem

3.13
. ∎

3.3.
Primitive Purity theorem: mod
p^{n}
reductions

In this subsection, we show that Frobenius modules over the reduction of a transversal regular prism enjoy a similar purity result as that of crystalline or semi-stable local systems.
The proof strategy is inspired by the work of Du–Liu–Moon–Shimizu as in
[
15
]
,
[
16
]
.

We start by introducing the category of tuples that consist of compatible Frobenius modules over various complete localizations.
Below we refer the reader to
Definition

3.1
for the precise definition of Frobenius modules.
For the simplicity of the notations, we use
\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}
to denote either the category
\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
or the category
\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I),n}
.
We also remind the reader of a convention that for a (derived)
p
-complete ring
A
, the quotient ring
A/p^{n}A
is defined to be the ring
A
itself when
n=\infty
.

Definition 3.22
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
We define the category
\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}
consisting of the tuples as below

\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\bigr),

where

•

M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is a finite projective (Frobenius) module over
A\langle 1/I\rangle/p^{n}A\langle 1/I\rangle
;

•

M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a finite projective (Frobenius) module over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
;

•

\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}:M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
is an isomorphism of (Frobenius) modules over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
.

The morphisms are defined as maps of tuples that are compatible with the linear structures (and the Frobenius structures).
A tuple
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\bigr)
in
\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I),n}
has
Frobenius height in
[a,b]
if each
M_{\mathfrak{p}}
has Frobenius height in
[a,b]
for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
.
When
n=\infty
, we use
\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I)}
to abbreviate the notation
\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),\infty}
.

Now we can present a primitive version of the purity result, for reflexive Frobenius modules over the mod
p^{n}
-reduction of an arbitrary transversal regular prism.

Theorem 3.23

(Primitive Purity at the finite level)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
There is a natural fully faithful functor

(26)

\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}\longrightarrow\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A),

The functor preserves the Frobenius heights, and its composition with the tensor product functor
\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A)\to\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}
is equal to the identity functor of
\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}
.

Proof.

We first give the construction of the functor.
Let
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\bigr)\in\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
.
By the assumption on the finite projectivity and the injectivity of the localization maps in
Proposition

2.16
.
(ii)
, both
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
are contained in
\widetilde{M}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A/p^{n}A[1/I]}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]\simeq\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]
, and it makes sense to consider the intersection
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, as a sub
A/p^{n}A
-module in
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.
It is then proved in
Lemma

3.24
and
Lemma

3.25
below that
M
is finitely generated over
A/p^{n}A
and is reflexive, hence is an object in
\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A)
.
In addition, since
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
is flat (
Footnote

5
), by the fact that the flat base change commutes with the finite limit, we get

\displaystyle\varphi_{A}^{*}(M)[1/I]

\displaystyle=\varphi_{A}^{*}(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}})[1/I]

\displaystyle\simeq(\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/I])\cap(\prod\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]).

So if we assume
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)\in\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I),n}
, then the Frobenius structure of
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)
naturally equips the last term of the equation above with an isomorphism to
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/I]\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]=M[1/I]
.
Hence we obtain the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{M}
on
M
.
If we further assume the tuple has Frobenius height in
[a,b]
, then we have the inclusions

I^{b}\cdot M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\subseteq\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\subseteq I^{a}\cdot M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},~\text{and}~I^{b}\cdot M_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq I^{a}\cdot M_{\mathfrak{p}},~\forall\mathfrak{p}.

Notice that since
I
is invertible and
\widetilde{M}
is
I
-torsionfree, as
A/p^{n}A
-submodules in
\widetilde{M}
we have
I^{i}\cdot M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap I^{i}\cdot\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}=I^{i}\cdot M
for each integer
i
.
Thus by taking the intersection we have that
(M,\varphi_{M})
has Frobenius height in
[a,b]
as well.

We then show that the following composition with the tensor product functor is the identity functor

\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}\longrightarrow\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}.

As the claim is Zariski local on
A
, we assume that the ideal
I=(d)
is principal.
Let
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)
be an object in
\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
, and let
M
be the intersection
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which by the last paragraph is an object in
\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A)
.
As the intersection commutes with the flat base change, the natural map
M\to M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
becomes an isomorphism after inverting by
I
.
In addition, by
Lemma

3.25
we know the canonical linearlization
M\otimes_{A}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an isomorphism.
Hence the tensor product functor naturally sends
M
onto
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)
.
If the input admits a Frobenius structure, then from the last paragraph, the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{M}
on
M
is defined via intersection.
Noticing that since the images of
M
in both
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
are generating, hence the base changes
\varphi_{M}\otimes_{A}A[1/I]
and
\varphi_{M}\otimes_{A}\prod A_{\mathfrak{p}}
coincide with
\varphi_{M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
and
\varphi_{\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}}
respectively.

Finally, since the composition above is an equivalence of categories, the intersection functor
\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n}\longrightarrow\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A)
is faithful.
To show the functor is full, we let
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)
and
\bigl(N_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(N_{\mathfrak{p}},\beta_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)
be two objects in the source, and let
M
and
N
be their corresponding intersection modules over
A/p^{n}A
.
Then given a morphism
f:M\to N
in
\Coh^{(\varphi)}(A/p^{n}A)
, we may define the natural base changes morphisms
g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f[1/I]
and
g_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
Then we obtain a natural arrow

g\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(g_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(g_{\mathfrak{p}}))\colon\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigl(N_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(N_{\mathfrak{p}},\beta_{\mathfrak{p}})\bigr).

It is then left to check that under the functor (
26
), the image of the arrow
g
, which by definition is the restriction of
g
on
M=M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, is equal to the arrow
f
.
The latter follows quickly from the fact that
M
is contained in
M[1/I]
, and by noticing that the two maps from
M
to
N
coincide after inverting by
I
.
∎

The following algebraic observations on the intersection module were used in the proof above.

Lemma 3.24

(Finiteness)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and
let
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\bigr)\in\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
The intersection
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is finitely generated over
A
.

Proof.

As the statement is Zariski local with respect to
A
, by passing to an open subset of
\Spec(A)
if necessary, we may assume the ideal
I=(d)
is principal and
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(and hence
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]\simeq M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
) is finite free.
We first notice that since
M[1/I]=M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is finite free over
A/p^{n}[1/I]
, there are finitely many elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in M
whose images in
M[1/I]
form a basis.
In addition, using the isomorphisms
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the images of
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
in
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A/p^{n}A[1/I]}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]\simeq\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]
form a basis of the latter over
\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]
as well.
Thus by the finitely generatedness of
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, there is an integer
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is contained in the
\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-submodule of
\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]
generated by
(\frac{x_{1}}{d^{N}},\ldots,\frac{x_{n}}{d^{N}})\in M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

We now claim that, within
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, the
A/p^{n}A
-submodule
M
is contained in the
A/p^{n}A
-submodule of
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
generated by
\frac{x_{1}}{d^{N}},\ldots,\frac{x_{n}}{d^{N}}
, which by the assumption that
A
is noetherian would conclude the proof.
To see the claim, we let
x=\sum_{i}a_{i}\cdot\frac{x_{i}}{d^{N}}
be any element in
M
for some
a_{i}\in A/p^{n}A[1/I]
.
By construction, the image of the element
x
in
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]
is contained in
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and is in particular in contained in the free submodule
\bigoplus_{i}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\frac{x_{i}}{d^{N}}\subset\bigoplus_{i}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]\cdot\frac{x_{i}}{d^{N}}=\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]
.
Thus the coefficients
a_{i}
, regarded as elements in
\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]
, are also within the subring
\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
As a consequence, we get
a_{i}\in(A/p^{n}A[1/I])\cap(\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, which finishes the proof since the intersection ring by
Corollary

2.18
is equal to
A/p^{n}A
.
∎

Lemma 3.25

(Reflexivity and base change)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism, and let
\bigl(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathrm{Ass}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}\bigr)\in\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
The intersection
