M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is reflexive over
A
(in the sense of
Definition

3.3
), and the canonical map
M\otimes_{A}\prod A_{\mathfrak{p}}\to\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

As the finite limit commutes with the flat base change, the localization
M[1/I]
is naturally isomorphic to
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, which by assumption is finite projective over
A/p^{n}A[1/I]
.
On the other hand, notice that by
Proposition

2.16
.
(i)
, the localization of
A/(p^{n},I)A
at a generic point is equal to one of the ring
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/(p^{n},I)A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
Thus to check the generic locally freeness of
M/IM=M\otimes_{A/p^{n}A}A/(p^{n},I)A
(hence the reflexivity of
(M,\varphi_{M})
), it suffices to show that the linearlization map
M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an isomorphism, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in{\mathrm{Ass}}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
.
Notice that using again the exactness of the flat base change along
A\to A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, we have

\displaystyle M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Ass}}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})}(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap M_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}})\bigr)\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle\simeq\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\bigl((M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})\cap(M_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigcap_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\bigl(M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]\cap(M_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})\bigr).

Moreover, if
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\neq\mathfrak{p}
, then since the mod
p^{n}
reduction of them are distinct minimal prime ideals of the ideal
I\cdot A/(p^{n}A
(
Proposition

2.16
.
(i)
), we know the ideal
I
is invertible in the mod
p^{n}
reduction of the ring
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
Hence
M_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}=M_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\otimes_{A}(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is naturally isomorphic to
M_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}[1/I]
, which is further isomorphic to
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}}[1/I]\simeq M_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}}[1/I]
.
As a consequence, continuing with the previous calculations, we get

\displaystyle M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle\simeq M_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A/p^{n}A}\bigl(A/p^{n}A[1/I]\cap(\prod_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p^{\prime}}}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq M_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A}A=M_{\mathfrak{p}},

where the second isomorphism is
Corollary

2.18
.
∎

As we saw from the proof of
Theorem

3.23
, the essential image of the functor (
26
) consists of the objects
(M,\varphi_{M})\in\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A)
such that the natural injection
M\to(M[1/I])\cap(M\otimes_{A/p^{n}A}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/p^{n}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is an isomorphism.
In general, the Frobenius module
(M,\varphi_{M})
is likely to be a proper submodule in the intersection, with the cokernel killed by a power of the ideal
I
.
The last result in this subsection says that the difference of the above two Frobenius modules depends only on their Frobenius heights and the natural number
n
.

Lemma 3.26
.

Let
(A,I)
be a regular prism, and let
f:(M_{1},\varphi_{M_{1}})\to(M_{2},\varphi_{M_{2}})
be an injection of
I
-torsionfree Frobenius modules in
\Coh^{\varphi,[a,b]}(A/p^{n}A)
such that
M_{1}[1/I]=M_{2}[1/I]
.
Then there is an integer
r=r(n;a,b)
, depending only on
n,a,b
, such that
\mathrm{Coker}(f)
is
I^{r}
-torsion.

Proof.

We first assume the ideal
I
admits a generator
d
.
As the element
\varphi_{A}(d)-d^{p}
is divided by
p
, by the binomial formula, we can find a large enough integer
s
(of
p
-power) that depends only on
n
such that
\varphi_{A}(d^{s})-d^{ps}
is divided by
p^{n}
(
Lemma

3.14
).
On the other hand, since both
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
have Frobenius height in
[a,b]
, their Frobenius structures induce
A/p^{n}A
-linear maps
\widetilde{\varphi}_{M_{i}}:\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{i}\to d^{a}\otimes M_{i}
.
As the image of
\widetilde{\varphi}_{M_{i}}
contains
d^{b}\otimes M_{i}
, the inverse isomorphism
\varphi_{M_{i}}^{-1}
can be extended to an
A/p^{n}A
-linear map
\widetilde{\psi}_{M_{i}}\colon d^{a}\otimes M_{i}\to\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{i}
, such that the composition
\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{i}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\varphi}_{M_{i}}}d^{a}\otimes M_{i}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\psi}_{M_{i}}}\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{i}
is the multiplication by
d^{b-a}
, for
i=1,2
.

We now assume the map
f
is not an isomorphism, and let
M_{3}
be
\mathrm{Coker}(f)
, which is a non-zero
I
-power torsion finitely generated module, and is equipped with the induced
A/p^{n}A
-linear morphisms
\widetilde{\varphi}_{M_{3}}:\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3}\to d^{a}\otimes M_{3}
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{M_{3}}:d^{a}\otimes M_{3}\to\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3}
, with the composition
\widetilde{\psi}_{M_{3}}\circ\widetilde{\varphi}_{M_{3}}=d^{b-a}
.
We let
r
be the smallest positive integer such that
M_{3}
is equal to the torsion submodule
M_{3}[d^{sr}]
, so that the multiplication by
d^{s(r-1)}
is non-zero on
M_{3}
.
Then by taking the Frobenius twist, we have the equalities of torsion modules

\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3}=\varphi_{A}^{*}(M_{3}[d^{sr}])=(\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3})[\varphi_{A}(d)^{sr}],

which (by the choice of
s
) is further equal to
(\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3})[d^{psr}]
.
Moreover, since
\varphi_{A}
is faithfully flat (
Footnote

5
), we know the multiplication by the element
\varphi_{A}(d)^{s(r-1)}=d^{ps(r-1)}
on
(\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3})
is non-zero.
On the other hand, as the module
d^{a}\otimes M_{3}
is killed by
d^{sr}
and the composition
\widetilde{\psi}_{M_{3}}\circ\widetilde{\varphi}_{M_{3}}
is equal to the multiplication by
d^{b-a}
on
\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3}
, we know the element
d^{sr}\cdot d^{b-a}=d^{sr+b-a}
kills the module
\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{3}
as well.
Hence combining the above analysis, we get the inequality
sr+b-a>ps(r-1)
, which implies that
r\leq\frac{b-a+ps}{(p-1)s}
.
Notice that the positive integer
s
can be chosen to be only depending on
n
.
Hence the claim follows by taking the roof function of the above positive rational number.
∎

3.4.
Primitive Purity theorem: infinite level

In this subsection, we extend the purity result for mod
p^{n}
modules to the infinite level, for Frobenius modules over regular prisms.

We start with the following limit presentation.

Proposition 3.27

(Limit presentation of Frobenius modules)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism.
Then the mod
p^{n}
reduction functors induce a fully faithful functor as below

(27)

\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A)\longrightarrow\lim_{n}\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A/p^{n}A).

In addition, a Frobenius module
(M,\varphi_{M})\in\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A)
has Frobenius height
[a,b]
if and only if its each mod
p^{n}
reduction does so.

Proof.

To start, we claim that the image of the tensor product functor
\Coh_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(A)\to\Coh(A/p^{n}A)
is contained in the subcategory
\Coh_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(A/p^{n}A)
.
Namely for
M\in\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}(A)
, we want to check that
M/p^{n}M
is
I
-torsionfree, the localization
M/p^{n}M[1/I]
is finite projective over
A/p^{n}A[1/I]
, and the reduction
M/(p^{n},I)M
is generically locally free over
A/(p^{n},I)A
:
By
Definition

3.3
, since
M[1/I]
is finite projective over
A[1/I]
, its mod
p^{n}
-reduction is finite projective over
A/p^{n}A[1/I]
as well.
Moreover, by
Lemma

3.4
.
(iii)
, the reflexivity assumption of
M
implies that
M/p^{n}M
is
I
-torsionfree.
Furthermore, since
M
is reflexive and the map
A\to A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is flat for each
\mathfrak{p}\in{\mathrm{Ass}}_{(}\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
, the base change
M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is reflexive and hence finite projective (
Corollary

3.7
) over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
In particular, the mod
(I,p^{n})
reduction of
M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is finite projective over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}/(p^{n},I)A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
Hence
M/(p^{n},I)M
is generically locally free.
As a consequence, the limit of the base change functors naturally induce a functor as in (
27
).
Notice that since
A
is
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree, we naturally have
M=\lim_{n}M/p^{n}M
.
In particular, the induced functor on the underlying modules
\Coh_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(A)\longrightarrow\lim_{n}\Coh_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(A/p^{n}A)
is fully faithful.

Now we prove that the functor (
27
) for Frobenius modules is fully faithful.
Let
(M,\varphi_{M})
and
(N,\varphi_{N})
be two objects in
\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A)
, and let
f:M\to N
be a map of
A
-modules, such that
f_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f\otimes_{A}A/p^{n}A
is Frobenius equivariant for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
By the first paragraph above, it suffices to show that
f
is Frobenius equivariant itself.
Note that by assumption, each
f_{n}
fits into the commutative diagram

(28)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}^{*}M/p^{n}M[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}^{*}N/p^{n}N[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M/p^{n}M[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N/p^{n}N[1/I].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}^{*}(f_{n}){[1/I]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{M}\otimes A/p^{n}A{[1/I]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{N}\otimes A/p^{n}A{[1/I]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}{[1/I]}}

By taking the limit, we get

(29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\langle 1/I\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\langle 1/I\rangle.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim\varphi_{A}^{*}(f_{n}){[1/I]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{M}\otimes A\langle 1/I\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{N}\otimes(A{[1/I]})^{\wedge}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim(f_{n}[1/I])}

Notice that the first vertical map in (
29
) is the base change of the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{M}
.
So by precomposing with the Frobenius structure of
M
, we get the following enlarged commutative diagram

(30)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\langle 1/I\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\langle 1/I\rangle.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim\varphi_{A}^{*}(f_{n}){[1/I]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{M}\otimes(A{[1/I]})^{\wedge}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{N}\otimes(A{[1/I]})^{\wedge}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim f_{n}{[1/I]}}

On the other hand, the composition of top row in (
30
), which has nothing to do with the Frobenius structures, can be also factored as the composition
\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I]\xrightarrow{\varphi_{A}^{*}f{[1/I]}}\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I]\to(\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}
.
Hence by combining the aforementioned factorization and the commutativity in (
30
), we get a commutative diagram

(31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[1/I]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\langle 1/I\rangle,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{N}\otimes(A{[1/I]})^{\wedge}_{p}}

where the right vertical map is an isomorphism, and the top map is injective, thanks to the analytic locally freeness of
N
and
Corollary

3.8
.
Notice that since the isomorphism
\varphi_{N}\otimes A\langle 1/I\rangle:(\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}N\langle 1/I\rangle
is equal to to the base change of the isomorphism
\varphi_{N}:\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I]\to N[1/I]
, the intersection
N\langle 1/I\rangle\cap\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I]
inside
N\langle 1/I\rangle\simeq(\varphi_{A}^{*}N[1/I])^{\wedge}_{p}
is exactly the submodule
N[1/I]
.
Hence the commutativity of the diagram (
31
) implies that the
M[1/I]
maps into
N[1/I]
, which finishes the proof.

Finally, we check the claim on Frobenius height.
If
(M,\varphi_{M})\in\Coh^{\varphi,[a,b]}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A)
, then by definition we have the containments of the submodules in
M[1/I]
as in (
18
).
Thus by taking the image of them into
M/p^{n}M
, each its mod
p^{n}
reduction has the same Frobenius height as well.
Conversely, if each
(M/p^{n}M,\varphi_{M/p^{n}M})
has Frobenius height
[a,b]
, then by taking the limit for the sequences (
18
) of
M/p^{n}M
, we see
(M,\varphi_{M})
admits the same type of sequence as well.
∎

Corollary 3.28
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism.
The base change functor induces a fully faithful functor

(32)

\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I)}\longrightarrow\lim_{n}\mathcal{G}^{(\varphi)}_{(A,I),n},

which preserves the Frobenius heights.

Proof.

We notice that the each ring appeared in the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{(A,I)}
is noetherian.
Thus for the underlying categories of modules
\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
, the fully faithfulness of the functor (
32
) follows from the locally freeness assumption of the reductions, together with the flatness of the limit in
[
43
,
Tag 0912
]
.
For the finite projective Frobenius modules over the ring
A\langle 1/I\rangle
, the fully faithfulness of the limit functor (
32
) follows by the same proof as in the last paragraph of the proof for
Proposition

3.27
.
As a consequence, the fully faithfulness for the functor
\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I)}\longrightarrow\lim_{n}\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I),n}
follows by applying
Proposition

3.27
at the regular prism
(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}},IA_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, together with the aforementioned fully faithfulness for Frobenius modules over
A\langle 1/I\rangle
.
∎

Now we present the primitive purity theorem for Frobenius modules over
A
.

Theorem 3.29

(Primitive Purity at the infinite level)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal regular prism.
There is a natural faithful functor

(33)

\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I)}\longrightarrow\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{sat}}(A),\quad(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}))\longmapsto M.

It satisfies the following properties:

•

The functor preserves the Frobenius heights.

•

The composition with the tensor product functor
\Coh^{\varphi}(A)\to\Coh^{\varphi}(A\langle 1/I\rangle)
naturally sends
M
onto
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

•

For each
\mathfrak{p}\in{\mathrm{Ass}}_{\overline{A}}(\overline{A}/p\overline{A})
, there is a natural injection of Frobenius modules
M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\hookrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which becomes isomorphic after base changing to
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
.

Remark 3.30

(Refined versions)
.

Before the proof, we comment on some special cases where the result admits automatic improvements.

(1)

If the étale realization functors
\Vect^{\varphi}(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})\to\Vect^{\varphi}(A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle)
are fully faithful for each associated prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
, then the composition of the functor (
33
) with the tensor product functor
\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{sat}}(A)\to\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I)}
is naturally equivalent to the identity functor on
\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I)}
.

(2)

If the analytic locally freeness in
Theorem

3.9
holds true for all the
p
-torsionfree Frobenius modules over
A
(for example when
(A,I)
is Breuil–Kisin or satisfies the assumption on ramification in
Corollary

3.21
), then the essential image of (
33
) is contained in the subcategory
\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}^{\varphi}(A)
.
In particular, each
M
is analytically locally free.

Proof of
Theorem

3.29
.

Step 1: The underlying
A
-module.

Let
(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}))
be an object in
\mathcal{G}_{(A,I)}
.
By the finite projectivity assumption of objects in
\mathcal{G}_{(A,I)}
and by the injectivity in
Proposition

2.16
.
(ii)
, the isomorphisms
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}
induce injections
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\widetilde{M}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle\simeq\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
, and in particular, it makes sense to consider the intersection
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
within
\widetilde{M}
.
Note that since both
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
are classical (and thus derived)
p
-complete, by
[
5
, Lem. 6.15]
, we know
M
is derived
p
-complete as well.
Moreover, the finite projectivity assumption implies that
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
M_{\mathfrak{p}}
are
p
-torsionfree.
Thus the submodule
M
is also
p
-torsionfree and is in particular classical
p
-complete, and we get the isomorphism
M=\lim M/p^{n}M
.

Step 2: The finiteness and the limit presentation.

We let
(M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n};(M_{\mathfrak{p},n},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}))\in\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}
be the mod
p^{n}
reduction of
(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}};(M_{\mathfrak{p}},\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}))
, and as in the proof of
Theorem

3.23
it makes sense to consider the intersection
M_{n}
, which by
loc. cit.
is a reflexive finitely presented module over
A/p^{n}A
.
Then we claim that the natural map
M/p^{n}M\to M_{n}
is injective.
Granting the claim, by the finiteness of
M_{n}
and the
p
-completeness of
M
, we know
M
is finitely generated over
A
.
To see the claim, if the map is not injective, then there is an element
m\in M
whose image in
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\oplus\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is contained in
\ker\bigl((M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\oplus\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}})\to(M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n}\oplus\prod M_{\mathfrak{p},n})\bigr)
.
The finite projectivity assumption then implies that the image of
m
in
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\oplus\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is contained in
p^{n}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\oplus\prod p^{n}M_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which we denote as
p^{n}\cdot(m^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},(m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}})
for some elements
m^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\in M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}\in M_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Then we get
p^{n}\cdot m^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}-p^{n}(m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}=0
in
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle\simeq\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}\langle 1/I\rangle
.
But notice that the latter is
p
-torsionfree.
Hence we have
m^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}=(m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which then comes from an element
m^{\prime}
in
M
such that
p^{n}\cdot m^{\prime}=m
.
This shows that
m\in p^{n}M
, and thus the injectivity of
M/p^{n}M\to M_{n}
.

In addition, notice that the finite projectivity assumption on
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
M_{\mathfrak{p}}
) implies its limit presentation
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}=\lim_{n}M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n}
(resp.
M_{\mathfrak{p}}=\lim_{n}M_{\mathfrak{p},n}
).
Hence by commuting the limit with the intersection, we have

(34)

M=M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}=(\lim M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n})\cap(\lim\prod M_{\mathfrak{p},n})=\lim(M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p},n})=\lim M_{n}.

Step 3: The Frobenius structure
\varphi_{M}
.

We claim that the Frobenius structures
\varphi_{M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
and
\varphi_{M_{\mathfrak{p}}}
naturally induce a Frobenius structure over
M
.
By assumption that
(A,I)
is regular, we know
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
is flat (
Footnote

5
).
In particular, by the fact that flat base change commutes with the finite intersection, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{A}^{*}M[1/I]

\displaystyle=\varphi_{A}^{*}(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}})[1/I]

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{A}^{*}M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/I])\cap(\varphi_{A}^{*}\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/I])\cap(\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I])

\displaystyle=M[1/I],

where the isomorphism in the third line above is given by the isomorphisms
\varphi_{M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}
and
\varphi_{M_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, which by assumption are compatible within their common ambient module
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes\prod A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle\simeq\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}\langle 1/I\rangle
.
Thus we obtain a natural Frobenius structure on
M
, which we denote as
\varphi_{M}
.

Here we note that as we saw from the proof of
Theorem

3.23
, each individual
A/p^{n}A
-module
M_{n}
admits a Frobenius structure as well, defined by the same formula as above (but for
M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n}
and
M_{\mathfrak{p},n}
).
So by their constructions, the mod
p^{n}
reduction of
\varphi_{M}
is naturally compatible with the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{M_{n}}
of
M_{n}
along the injection
M/p^{n}M\to M_{n}
.

Moreover, notice that for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, as
A
-submodules of
M[1/I]
we have
I^{i}\cdot M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap I^{i}\cdot\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}=I^{i}\cdot(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}})=I^{i}\cdot M
.
Thus if each
M_{\mathfrak{p}}
(and trivially for
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
has Frobenius height
[a,b]
, then by taking the intersection of (
18
), we see
(M,\varphi_{M})
has Frobenius height
[a,b]
as well.

Step 4: The saturatedness.

By assumption, since
M
is a submodule in the finite projective
A\langle 1/I\rangle
-module
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, we know
M
is torsionfree.
we want to show that
M
satisfies the condition in
Lemma

3.4
.
(ii)
with respect to
p
and (local generators of)
I
, namely the injection
M\to M[1/p]\cap M[1/I]
is an isomorphism.
Notice that by commuting the flat base change with the finite intersection, we get

\displaystyle M[1/p]\cap M[1/I]

\displaystyle=(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}})[1/p]\cap(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}})[1/I]

\displaystyle=(M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/p]\cap M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/I])\cap\prod(M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/p]\cap M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I]).

Since each
M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is locally free over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and in particular is reflexive, we have
(M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/p]\cap M_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/I])=M_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
On the other hand, since
A\langle 1/I\rangle=A\langle 1/I\rangle[1/p]\cap A\langle 1/I\rangle[1/I]
, by the finite projectivity of
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
over
A\langle 1/I\rangle
, we get
M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/p]\cap M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[1/I]=M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.
Hence continuing with the calculation above, we get
M[1/p]\cap M[1/I]=M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\cap\prod M_{\mathfrak{p}}=M
.

Step 5: Composition with the tensor product functor
\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{refl}}(A)\to\mathcal{G}^{\varphi}_{(A,I)}
.

We now consider the composition of
33
with the tensor product functor
\Coh_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(A)\to\mathcal{G}_{(A,I)}
.
We first claim that the linearization map
M\otimes_{A}A\langle 1/I\rangle\to M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is an Frobenius equivariant isomorphism.
As both objects are classically
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree, it suffices to show the isomoprhims for the mod
p
reductions, namely

M/pM[1/I]\to M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},1},

where the latter by
Theorem

3.23
is naturally identified with
M_{1}[1/I]
.
From Step 1, we know
M/pM\to M_{1}
is injective, which implies the injectivity of the localization
M/pM[1/I]\to M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},1}
.
Moreover, by the limit presentation
M=\lim M_{n}
in Step 2, we know
M/pM
is equal to the intersection of Frobenius modules
\cap_{n>0}\mathrm{Im}(M_{n}\to M_{1})
.
Notice that
Theorem

3.23
implies the equality
M_{n}[1/I]=M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n}
for each
n
, and by our assumption the mod
p
reduction of
M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},n}
is equal to
M_{{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},1}
.
Hence each
\mathrm{Im}(M_{n}\to M_{1})
is a Frobenius submodule of
M_{1}
with same Frobenius heights such that
\mathrm{Im}(M_{n}\to M_{1})[1/I]=M_{1}[1/I]
.
As a consequence, by
Lemma

3.26
we know there is a positive integer
r=r(1;a,b)
such that the cokernel of the map
\mathrm{Im}(M_{n}\to M_{1})\to M_{1}
is killed by
I^{r}
, for each
n>0
.
The latter implies that the submodule
I^{r}M_{1}
is contained in the intersection
\cap_{n>0}\mathrm{Im}(M_{n}\to M_{1})=M/pM
, and hence
M/pM[1/I]=M_{1}[1/I]
.

We then check that each
M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an injection and induces an isomorphism after base changing to
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
.
We first notice that since the mod
p
reduction of the map is
M/pM\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to M_{\mathfrak{p}}/pM_{\mathfrak{p}}
, where the target by
Theorem

3.23
is equal to
M_{1}\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, its injectivity follows from that of
M/pM\to M_{1}
, which was proved in Step 4.
So by the completeness and torsionfreeness, we know
M\otimes_{A}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\to M_{\mathfrak{p}}
is injective as well.
The isomorphism over
A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
follows from the last paragraph together with the input isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}:M_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle\simeq M_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
.
∎

Remark 3.31
.

For the underlying
A
-modules without the Frobenius structures, it follows from the proof of
Theorem

3.29
that the functor (
33
) can be factorized as below:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{(A,I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Coh_{\mathrm{sat}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{n}\mathcal{G}_{(A,I),n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{n}\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}(A),}

where the bottom functor is (
26
).

4.
Integral pro-étale cohomology and its finiteness

In this section, we setup the basics of the pro-étale geometry needed in the article, give a projection formula for integral continuous group cohomology, and recollect various finiteness results of pro-étale cohomology of integral and rational coefficients.

We setup the convention of the pro-étale site used in the article.
Fix a
p
-adic complete discrete valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with perfect residue field
k
.
Let
{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
be the absolute Galois group of a fixed algebraic closure
\overline{K}
over
K
, and let
C
be the completion of
\overline{K}
, with
\mathcal{O}_{C}
its ring of integers.
Here we will implicitly fix a cardinality and assume all the constructions are
small
(cf.
[
41
, § 4]
).

Definition 4.1
.

(1)

We let
{\mathrm{Perfd}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
be the
big pro-étale site
on the category of perfectoid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, together with the pro-étale topology, as in
[
41
, Def. 8.1]
.

(2)

For a locally spatial diamond
Z
, we let
Z_{{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
be the
pro-étale site of
Z
of perfectoid spaces over
Z
, defined as the sliced site
{\mathrm{Perfd}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}|_{Z}
.

Remark 4.2
.

The cautious reader may notice that the notion of the pro-étale site used in this article is different from the original construction in
[
40
]
, where various period sheaves were introduced.
We remark that the only properties that are relevant to the discussion of the period sheaves are the sheafifiness of the (tilted) pro-étale structure sheaves and the acyclicity of their cohomology on affinoid perfectoid spaces, which are guaranteed by
[
41
, Prop. 8.5]
.

We also setup the convention on the generic fiber.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
X
be bounded
p
-adic formal scheme.
We let
X_{\eta}
be the
generic fiber of
X
, regarded as a locally special diamond in
\Shv({\mathrm{Perfd}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
as in
[
41
, Def. 15.5]
.

Remark 4.4
.

Assume
X
is a topologically of finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, so that its Raynaud’s generic fiber is a rigid space over
K
.
Then thanks to
[
7
, Lem. 15.6]
and
[
39
, Prop. 10.2.3]
, the (finite) étale site of this rigid space is equivalent to the (finite) étale site of the diamond
X_{\eta}
.
For this reason, we will identify the Raynaud’s generic fiber of
X
with its associated diamond
X_{\eta}
, and call both of them the
generic fiber of
X
.

To calculate the pro-étale cohomology, it is often useful to translate it into certain continuous group cohomology.
As a preparation, we recall the following construction of the algebraic fundamental group.

Construction 4.5

(Algebraic fundamental group)
.

Let
X
be a connected normal
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
X_{\eta}
be its generic fiber.
Let
\bar{x}_{\eta}
be a geometric point of
X_{\eta}
.
We let
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}
be a fixed maximal connected pro-finite-étale cover of
X_{\eta}
, which is a perfectoid space in
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
Then the canonical map
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is a Galois cover, and its associated Galois group is the
algebraic fundamental group

G_{X_{\eta}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X_{\eta},\bar{x}_{\eta})
. In addition, the map
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
factors through the base extension
X_{\eta,C}\to X_{\eta}
, which corresponds to the surjection
G_{X_{\eta}}\to{\mathrm{Gal}}_{K}
and we use
G_{X_{\eta,C}}
to denote the kernel.
In the special case when
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine,
the adic space
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}
is affinoid perfectoid and is of the form
{\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
, where
S
is the
p
-completion of the integral closure of
R
in a fixed connected maximal pro-finite-étale extension of
R[1/p]
, and admits a map from the ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{C}
.

4.1.
Projection formula

We prove a projection formula for continuous group cohomology in this subsection.

To prepare, we start with a discreteness result of the completely flat tensor product.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
R
be a ring, let
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
be a Koszul regular sequence in
R
, and assume
R
is complete under the
I=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
-adic topology.
Assume
M
and
N
are two derived
I
-complete
R
-modules, such that

•

M
is
I
-completely flat over
R
;

•

(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
is a Koszul regular sequence for
N
.

Then the derived
I
-adic complete tensor product
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{I}
is naturally isomorphic to the classical completion of the classical tensor product, namely
\lim_{n}(M\otimes_{R}N)/I^{n}
.
In particular, it lives in cohomological degree zero.

Proof.

By the first assumption, the derived
I
-completion
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{I}
is
I
-completely flat over
N
.
This implies that the Koszul complex
\mathrm{Kos}(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N;f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{d}^{n})
is a flat complex over
\mathrm{Kos}(N;f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{d}^{n})
.
On the other hand by the second assumption, the Koszul complex
\mathrm{Kos}(N;f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{d}^{n})
is a discrete module.
Note that by definition, the derived completion
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{I}
is
R\lim_{n}\mathrm{Kos}(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N;f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{d}^{n})
.
As a consequence, since
\{\mathrm{Kos}(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N;f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{d}^{n})\}_{n}
is a pro-system of discrete modules with surjective transition maps, we see the derived complete tensor product
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{I}
lives in cohomological degree zero.

Moreover, since
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
is Koszul regular in
R
, using the noetherian approximation and
[
43
,
Tag 0925
,
Tag 0921
]
, we see the pro-systems
\{\mathrm{Kos}(R,f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{d}^{n})\}_{n}
and
\{R/I^{n}\}
are pro-isomorphic to each other,
and the derived completion
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{I}
is isomorphic to
R\lim_{n}\bigl((M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)\otimes^{L}_{R}R/I^{n}\bigr)
.
Finally, since the derived tensor product
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)\otimes^{L}_{R}R/I
by assumptions is isomorphic to
M/IM\otimes_{R/IR}N/IN
, the discreteness of the derived completion
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{I}
implies that
R\lim_{n}\bigl((M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)\otimes^{L}_{R}R/I^{n}\bigr)\simeq\lim_{n}(M\otimes_{R}N)/I^{n}
.
∎

Another preparation is the following vanishing result on the limit of the first continuous group cohomology.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
R
be a
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree ring, let
G
be a pro-finite group.
Assume
N
is a
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree
R
-module with a continuous
G
-action.
Then we have

R^{1}\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)/p^{n})=\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}])=0.

Proof.

By assumption, we know
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)\subseteq N
is also a
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree module.
So we have
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)\simeq R\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)/p^{n}
, which implies the vanishing of
R^{1}\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)/p^{n})
.
In addition, by the isomorphism
N=R\lim_{n}N/p^{n}N\simeq\lim_{n}N/p^{n}N
and the fact that limit commutes with continuous group invariant, we have

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,\lim_{n}N)\simeq\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n}N).

So by the universal coefficient theorem (with respect to
p
), we get the vanishing of
\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}])
.
∎

We also introduce a stronger flatness assumption.

Definition 4.8
.

Let
R
be a ring, let
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
be a Koszul regular sequence in
R
, and assume
R
is complete under the
I=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
-adic topology.

(1)

We say an
R
-module
M
is
completely projective (resp. completely finite projective, resp. completely free)
over
R
if there is a projective (resp. finite projective, resp. free) module
M_{0}
over
R
, such that
M\simeq(M_{0})^{\wedge}_{I}
.

(2)

We say a map of
R
-modules
M\to N
is
completely injective
if the derived mod
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
reduction of the map is an injection of
R/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})R
-modules.

Note that the complete injectivity in particular implies the injectivity.
We also say an
R
-algebra
S
(equivalently a homomorphism
R\to S
) is
completely projective
if
S
is completely projective over
R
, and similarly for the others.
So a completely projective homomorphism of complete rings is in particular completely faithfully flat.

The following lemmas on the complete projective base change of an injection will be used later.

Lemma 4.9

(Injectivity of complete base change, two variables)
.

Let
R
be a ring and assume
R
is complete under a Koszul regular sequence
(f_{1},f_{2})
in
R
.
Let
R\to S
be a completely injective map of
(f_{1},f_{2})
-complete rings.
Let
M\to N
be a map of
(f_{1},f_{2})
-regular and
(f_{1},f_{2})
-complete
R
-modules.

(1)

Assume either
M
is completely flat, or
S
is completely faithfully flat over
R
. The complete linearization map
M\to(M\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{(f_{1},f_{2})}
is injective.

(2)

Assume
S
is completely projective over
R
and the mod
f_{1}
reduction of
M\to N
is injective. The complete base change
(M\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{(f_{1},f_{2})}\to(N\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{(f_{1},f_{2})}
is injective.

Proof.

For the first part, it suffices to check that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the linearization map

M/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}M\to M/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}M\otimes_{R/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}R}S/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}S

is injective:
this is clear if
M
is completely flat since
R\to S
is completely injective, so we assume
S/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}S
is faithfully flat over
R/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}R
for now.
Then to check its injectivity, by the faithfully flatness it suffices to check it for the base change along
R/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}R\to S/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}S
, which admits a canonical section via the multiplication map of the two identical factors of
S/(f_{1},f_{2})^{n}S
.
Hence by taking the inverse limit, we get the injectivity of
M\to(M\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{(f_{1},f_{2})}
.

For the second part, we let
\overline{R}
denote the mod
f_{1}
reduction of
R
and similarly for other objects.
We let
C
be the cokernel of the canonical injection
\overline{M}\to\overline{N}
, which is a
f_{2}
-complete module and by assumption has
\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C[f_{2}^{n}]=0.

To show the claim, since both objects are derived
f_{1}
-complete, it suffices to check the injectivity of their mod
f_{1}
-reduction, namely the map

(\overline{M}\otimes^{L}_{\overline{R}}\overline{S})^{\wedge}_{f_{2}}\longrightarrow(\overline{N}\otimes^{L}_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{f_{2}},

which by the long exact sequence of the tensor product is equivalent to the vanishing of
\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(C[f_{2}^{n}]\otimes^{L}_{\overline{R}}\overline{S})
.
Notice that by the assumption, the reduction
\overline{S}
is the
f_{2}
-completion of a projective module
P
over
\overline{R}
, where we can embed
P
into a free module
\bigoplus_{i}\overline{R}
.
So we get

\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(C[f_{2}^{n}]\otimes^{L}_{\overline{R}}\overline{S})=\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(C[f_{2}^{n}]\otimes_{\overline{R}}P)\hookrightarrow\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\bigoplus_{i}C[f_{2}^{n}])\subset\lim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i}C[f_{2}^{n}].

So by switching the order of limits in the last term above, we see it is zero, which finishes the proof.
∎

Here we note that same arguments also show the following simpler case.

Lemma 4.10

(Injectivity of complete base change, one variable)
.

Let
R
be a
f
-complete
f
-torsionfree ring for an element
f\in R
, and let
R\to S
be a completely injective map of
f
-complete rings.
Let
M\to N
be a map of
f
-complete and
f
-torsionfree modules.

(1)

Assume either
M
is completely flat, or
S
is completely faithfully flat over
R
. The complete linearization map
M\to(M\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{f}
is injective.

(2)

Assume
S
is completely projective over
R
and
M\to N
is injective. The complete base change
(M\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{f}\to(N\otimes_{R}S)^{\wedge}_{f}
is injective.

It is tempting to hope that the injectivity can be preserved by an arbitrary completely faithfully flat base change, and even in the one-variable case we give a counterexample below.
Roughly speaking, it is related to the fact that the complete localization functor is not exact in the condensed world (cf.
[
25
, Exm. A.8, Rmk. A.9]
.)

Example 4.11
.

Let
R=\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle x\rangle
, let
f=p
, and let
S
be the ring
R\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle x^{\pm 1}\rangle
.
Consider the map of
R
-modules
M_{1}=(\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{N}}R)^{\wedge}_{p}\to M_{2}=(\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{N}}R)^{\wedge}_{p}
that sends
(a_{i})
onto
(pa_{i}-xa_{i+1})
.
Then the kernel of the complete base change consists of all the elements
(0,(a_{i}))
such that
a_{i}=(\frac{p}{x})^{i}a_{0}
and
a_{0}\in S
.

We now give a projection formula on the continuous group invariant, for the complete tensor product with a flat module.

Theorem 4.12

(Projection formula)
.

Let
R
be a
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree ring, let
G
be a pro-finite group that acts on
R
trivially.
Let
M,N
be two
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree continuous
R[G]
-modules such that the
G
-action on
M
is trivial.
Assume either of the following conditions:

(a)

M
is
p
-completely flat over
R
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion; or

(b)

M
is completely projective over
R
.

Then we have natural isomorphisms

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{p})\simeq\bigl(M\otimes^{L}_{R}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\lim_{n}\bigl(M\otimes_{R}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)\bigr)/p^{n}.

Here we mention that when
G
is profinite, the statement and the proof can be adapted to the higher cohomology, once formulated in an appropriate derived category of continuous
G
-representations.

Proof.

We first notice that the second isomorphism in the statement follows from
Lemma

4.6
, since
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)
is a derived
p
-complete submodule of
N
, where the latter is
p
-torsionfree.
It then remains to check that there is a natural isomorphism between the first and the last term. By assumptions and
Lemma

4.6
, the complete derived tensor product
(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{p}
is the inverse limit of the discrete module
(M\otimes_{R}N)/p^{n}
.
So by the fact that the inverse limit commutes with the group invariant, we get

(35)

\begin{multlined}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\bigl(G,\lim_{n}(M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}N/p^{n})\bigr)\\
\simeq\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}N/p^{n}),\end{multlined}

where the latter naturally admits a map from
\lim_{n}\bigl(M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})\bigr)
.
We then claim that the map is an isomorphism: to see this, it suffices to check that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the canonical map below is an isomorphism

(36)

M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}N/p^{n}).

Since
M
is
p
-completely flat over
R
in both cases, the reduction
M/p^{n}M
is flat over
R/p^{n}R
.
In particular, by Lazard’s theorem
[
43
,
Tag 058G
]
, we know
M/p^{n}M
is isomorphic to a filtered colimit
\colim_{k}F_{k}
, where each
F_{k}
is a finite free
R/p^{n}R
-module.
So thanks to the fact that the filtered colimit commutes with the group invariant, we get natural isomorphisms that are compatible with (
36
)

\displaystyle M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\colim_{k}F_{k}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\colim_{k}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,F_{k}\otimes N/p^{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}\bigl(G,\colim_{k}(F_{k}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}N/p^{n})\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}N/p^{n}),

where the second isomorphism uses the fact that the group invariant commutes with finite direct sums.
So combine (
35
) and the limit of (
36
), we get

(37)

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,(M\otimes^{L}_{R}N)^{\wedge}_{p})\simeq\lim_{n}\bigl(M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})\bigr).

To obtain the formulae in the statement that involves
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)/p^{n}
, we recall that by the universal coefficient theorem and the flatness of
M/p^{n}
over
R/p^{n}
, we have a short exact sequence of inverse systems

(38)

0\longrightarrow\{M/p^{n}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)/p^{n}\}_{n}\longrightarrow\{M/p^{n}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})\}_{n}\longrightarrow\{M/p^{n}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}]\}_{n}\longrightarrow 0.

Now we discuss the two scenarios as in the statement.
If
M
and
N
satisfy the assumptions in
(a)
, then the fourth term of (
38
) is equal to
\{M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{m}]\}_{n}
for a fixed integer
m
.
Since the transition morphism of
\{M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{m}]\}_{n}
is the multiplication-by-
p
map, the inverse system is pro-zero.
Hence we get the isomorphism

\lim_{n}\bigl(M/p^{n}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)/p^{n}\bigr)\simeq\lim_{n}\bigl(M/p^{n}\otimes_{R/p^{n}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N/p^{n})\bigr),

which combining with (
37
) finishes the proof.

Next, we assume that
M
is the
p
-completion of a projective module
M_{0}
as in
(b)
.
We let
\bigoplus_{i}R
be a free module that admits a split injection from
M_{0}
.
Then there are natural injections of inverse systems

\displaystyle\{M/p^{n}M\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}]\}_{n}

\displaystyle=\{M_{0}/p^{n}M_{0}\otimes_{R/p^{n}R}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}])\}_{n}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\{\bigoplus_{i}R/p^{n}R\otimes_{R/p^{n}R}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}])\}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\{\prod_{i}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}]\},

On the other hand, since the inverse limit commutes with the product, we have

(39)

\lim_{n}\prod_{i}(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}])\simeq\prod_{i}\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}]),

where
\lim_{n}(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}])=0
thanks to the assumption that
N
is
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree and
Lemma

4.7
.
Hence the limit of
\{M/p^{n}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,N)[p^{n}]\}_{n}
, which is contained in the first limit of (
39
), also vanishes.
∎

Remark 4.13

(Analogue for pro-étale cohomology)
.

By translating pro-étale cohomology into continuous group cohomology, we naturally obtain an analogue of
Theorem

4.12
but for the global section of a
p
-complete pro-étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on a rigid space
X_{\eta}
.
This is similar to the classical relationship between étale cohomology and group cohomology, and we will assume this fact without additional explanations.

4.2.
Finiteness of arithmetic pro-étale cohomology

In this subsection, we recollect the finiteness of rational arithmetic pro-étale cohomology using the decompletion result of Liu–Zhu
[
34
]
, and prove the finiteness of the integral arithmetic pro-étale cohomology, up to bounded torsions.

Recall from
[
40
, §6]
and
[
34
, §2.1]
that for a smooth rigid space
X_{\eta}
, there is a period sheaf
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}={\mathrm{gr}}^{0}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
We let
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}
be the analytic cotangent complex of the rational complete structure sheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}
, which is defined by inverting
p
at the
p
-complete cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}}
(
[
26
, Rmk. 4.5]
); similarly for
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/K}
.
Then by
[
40
, Def. 6.8.(iv)]
and
[
26
, Thm. 4.21]
, there is a natural formula

(40)

\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}\simeq\colim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\bigl(\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}(-i)[-i]\bigr),

where the transition morphism
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}(-1)[-1]
and its wedge higher powers are induced by the Faltings extension, or more conceptually the canonical map

\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/K}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}.

In particular, the pro-étale sheaf
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}(-i)[-i]
is naturally contained in
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}
.

Theorem 4.14

(Liu–Zhu)
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be an affine topologically of finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and assume the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
is smooth over
K
of dimension
d
.
Let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
X_{\eta}
.
The cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(j))
lives in
D^{[0,d+1]}_{{\mathrm{coh}}}(R[1/p])
, and vanishes if
|j|\gg 0
.

Proof.

The statement can be proved using the decompletion result in
[
34
]
, as we shall explain.
We first notice that the statement is Zariski local with respect to
X_{\eta}
.
So as in the setup of
[
34
, §2.3]
, we may assume
X_{\eta}
is small and thus admits a toric chart, and let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}\rtimes{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
be the Galois group associated to the induced pro-finite Galois cover of
X_{\eta}
, where
K_{\infty}/K
is the (
p
-completed) cyclotomic cover.
Then by
[
34
, Prop. 2.8 (and the paragraph above for notations), Lem. 3.10]
, we know that there is a large enough subextension
K_{m}/K
in
K_{\infty}
, together with a finite projective
(R_{K_{m}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}R\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K_{m})
-module
M
that is equipped with a continuous
\Gamma
-action, such that

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Gamma,M(j))\simeq R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(j)),

where
M(j)=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(j)
is the
j
-th Tate twist of
M
.
Notice that by construction, since
\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}
(resp.
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K_{m})
) is a
p
-completed free abelian group of
d
topological generators (resp.
1
generator), the continuous group cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Gamma_{m},M(j))
is computed by the Koszul complexes, namely

R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Gamma_{m},M(j))\simeq\bigl[\Kos_{M(j)}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}})\xrightarrow{\gamma-\id}\Kos_{M(j)}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}})\bigr],

where
\gamma
is a topological generator of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K_{m})
and
\Gamma_{m}
is the preimage of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K_{m})\subset{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
in
\Gamma
.
In addition, by the finiteness of
M
, each term in the complex
\Kos_{M(j)}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}})
above is a finite
R[1/p]
-module, and the arrows in the complex are
R[1/p]
-linear.
Hence
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Gamma_{m},M(j))
lives in the category
D^{[0,d+1]}_{{\mathrm{coh}}}(R[1/p])
.
Finally, by the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence together with the fact that the
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{m}/K)
-invariant functor is exact on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector spaces, we see
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(j))
lives in the category
D^{[0,d+1]}_{{\mathrm{coh}}}(R[1/p])
as well.

To continue, as the geometric Galois cohomology commutes with the Tate twist, we have
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Kos_{M(j)}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))=\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Kos_{M}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))(j)
, as continuous representations of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K_{m})
.
For each element
x\in\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Kos_{M}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))
, we let
x^{j}
be the associated element in the
j
-th Tate twist.
Then we have
\gamma(x^{j})=p^{jn}\cdot\gamma(x)^{j}
, where
n
is a constant positive integer that depends on
K_{m}
.
In particular, since
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Kos_{M}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))
is finitely generated over
R[1/p]
, the action of
\gamma-\id
on
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Kos_{M}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))(j)
is invertible when
|j|
is sufficiently large.
As a consequence, the arithmetic Galois cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{cont}}\bigl({\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K_{m}),\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Kos_{M}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))(j)\bigr)
vanishes when
|j|
is sufficiently large.
Hence by the boundedness of
\Kos_{M}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{geom}}))(j)
, we see the entire cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(j))
vanishes for
|j|\gg 0
.
∎

Proposition 4.15

(Rational finiteness)
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be an affine topologically of finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and assume the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
is smooth over
K
of dimension
d
.
Let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
.

(i)

For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, the cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}[-i])
lives in
D^{[0,d+1]}_{{\mathrm{coh}}}(R[1/p])
.

(ii)

The zero-th cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}[-i])
is contained in
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(i))
and vanishes when
i\gg 0
.
If
T
is a constant local system, then the zero-th cohomology vanishes for
i>0
.

Proof.

Part
(i)
is a consequence of
Theorem

4.14
and the Faltings extension (
[
26
, Thm. 4.9]
): namely the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}
fits into the fiber sequence

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(1)[1]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{R[1/p]}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta}/K}[1],

where
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta}/K}
is a finite projective
R[1/p]
-module.
So the statement follows by taking the associated long exact sequence of the wedge power and
Theorem

4.14
.

For
(ii)
, we notice that by (
40
), the pro-étale sheaf
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}[-i]
is naturally contained in
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(i)
.
In addition, by
[
34
, Lem. 2.9]
(see
[
34
, Prop. 2.8]
for the notations), we know

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(i))\simeq\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(i)).

Thus the vanishing of the zero-th cohomology of
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}[-i]
follows from the aforementioned inclusion and
Theorem

4.14
.
For the constant local system, as mentioned above, the vanishing of the zero-th cohomology of
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}/X_{\eta}}[-i]
for
i>0
follows from that of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(i)
.
The latter is a consequence of Tate’s calculation of Galois cohomology (
[
44
]
) together with the fact that
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{K_{\infty}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}})=R_{K_{\infty}}
(a consequence of for example the Hodge–Tate decomposition
[
40
]
).
∎

Now we turn to the integral arithmetic pro-étale cohomology.
Different from the rational case, the integral pro-étale cohomology is not finitely generated due to many torsion classes.
However, we prove below that the integral cohomology has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion and the torsionfree quotient is finitely generated.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
R
be a
p
-torsionfree and
p
-complete noetherian ring, and let
\mathrm{H}
be a derived
p
-complete
R
-module.
Assume
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
is finitely generated over
R[1/p]
.

(i)

The submodule
\mathrm{H}[p^{\infty}]
is bounded, namely
\mathrm{H}[p^{\infty}]=\mathrm{H}[p^{N}]
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

(ii)

The torsionfree quotient
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}/\mathrm{H}[p^{\infty}]
is a finitely presented
R
-module.

Proof.

First we notice that the image of
\mathrm{H}
in
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
is naturally isomorphic to the torsionfree quotient
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}
.
Since
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
is a finitely generated
R[1/p]
-module, we let
\{\overline{m}_{1},\ldots,\overline{m}_{n}\}
be a finite set of elements in
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}
whose image in
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
generates the latter.
We let
\{m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\}
be a set of lifts of the aforementioned elements in
\mathrm{H}
.
Then the choice of elements induces a map
f:R^{\oplus n}\to\mathrm{H}
of
R
-modules such that its image in
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
generates the entire
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
.
We let
M
(resp.
\overline{M}
) be the image of
R^{\oplus n}
in
\mathrm{H}
(resp. in
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}
), and let
g:M\to\mathrm{H}
and
\overline{g}:\overline{M}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}
be the induced injections.
Then we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(41)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\oplus n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}[1/p].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{g}}

Notice that since
M
is finitely generated over
R
, we know
M
is derived
p
-complete (
[
43
,
Tag 0EEU
]
).
So by the derived
p
-completeness of
\mathrm{H}
, we know
\mathrm{cofib}(g)=\coker(g)
is also derived
p
-complete.
On the other hand, by the construction of
M
above, we know
M[1/p]=\mathrm{H}[1/p]
which implies that
\mathrm{cofib}(g)
is
p^{\infty}
-torsion.
So by Bhatt’s boundedness result of torsion complete modules (
[
43
,
Tag 0CQY
]
), we know
\mathrm{cofib}(g)
is bounded.

To continue, we consider the map of short exact sequences induced from (
41
)

(42)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cofib}(g)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cofib}(\overline{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{g}}

From the diagram, we know
\ker(\mathrm{H}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}})
, which is equal to
\mathrm{H}[p^{\infty}]
, is sandwiched by
\ker(M\to\overline{M})
and
{\mathrm{cofib}}(g)
.
By the noetherian assumption of
R
and the finiteness of
M
, we know
\ker(M\to\overline{M})=M[p^{\infty}]
is bounded.
Hence by combining the latter with the boundedness of
{\mathrm{cofib}}(g)
, we know
\mathrm{H}[p^{\infty}]
is also bounded, which finishes
(i)
.

To prove
(ii)
, we notice that (
42
) implies that the map
{\mathrm{cofib}}(g)\to{\mathrm{cofib}}(\overline{g})
is surjective.
In particular
{\mathrm{cofib}}(\overline{g})
is bounded and is killed by
p^{N}
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
So as submodules inside
\mathrm{H}[1/p]
, we have

\overline{M}\subseteq\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}\subseteq\frac{1}{p^{N}}\overline{M}.

Hence by the noetherian assumption of
R
and the finiteness of
M
, we see
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{tf}}
is finitely presented over
R
as well.
∎

Corollary 4.17

(Integral finiteness)
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be an affine topologically of finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and assume the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
is smooth over
K
of dimension
d
.
Let
T
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
T
over
X_{\eta}
, let
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
\mathrm{H}^{j}
be the
j
-th cohomology of
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\bigl(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}[-i]\bigr)
.

(i)

The submodule
\mathrm{H}^{j}[p^{\infty}]
is bounded.

(ii)

The torsionfree quotient
\mathrm{H}^{j}_{\mathrm{tf}}
is a finitely presented
R
-module, and vanishes if
j\notin[0,d+1]
.

(iii)

The module
\mathrm{H}^{0}
vanishes for
i\gg 0
.

Finally, we give the calculation of the global section for the integral pro-étale structure sheaf.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be a normal topologically of finite type
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
X_{\eta}
be its generic fiber.
The canonical map below is an isomorphism

R\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}).

Proof.

Note that there are natural maps of ringed sites
(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+})\to(X_{\eta,\mathrm{an}},\mathcal{O}^{+})\to(X_{\mathrm{Zar}},\mathcal{O}_{X})
, inducing maps of rings

(43)

\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)=R\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{+}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}(X_{\eta}).

It is left to show that the canonical maps above are all equalities.
By assumption,
X_{\eta}
is the affinoid rigid space
{\mathrm{Spa}}(R[1/p],R)
, with
R
an integrally closed subring in
R[1/p]
.
On the other hand, by
[
39
, Prop. 10.2.3]
and the normality of
R[1/p]
, we know
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(X_{\eta})=\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}(X_{\eta})=R[1/p]
.
So the maps in (
43
) are all inclusions, and it is left to show that
R\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}(X_{\eta})
is surjective.
Note that by
[
40
, Lem. 4.2.(ii)]
, the subring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}(X_{\eta})
is contained in the ring of power-bounded elements in
R[1/p]
.
So the claim follows from
[
13
, Thm. 7.4.1]
, which shows that
R
is the subring of power-bounded elements in
R[1/p]
.
∎

5.
Prismatic family of period sheaves

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
X_{\eta}
be its generic fiber.
In this section, we introduce a prismatic family of period sheaves: a family of period sheaves over the rigid space
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
that are parametrized by the prisms in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
We then calculate their global sections.

5.1.
Period presheaves

We start with the construction of the period presheaves on the pro-étale site of the rigid space
X_{\eta}
.

To prepare, we recall the construction of the infinitesimal period sheaf as below.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
S
be a
p
-torsionfree perfectoid ring.

(1)

We let
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
be the ring
W(S^{\flat})
, where
S^{\flat}=\lim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}S
is the tilt of
S
.
It admits an automorphism
\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
induced by the Witt vector Frobenius of the perfect ring.
Moreover, there is a natural surjection
\widetilde{\theta}:{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to S
, sending a Teichmüller lift
[(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots)]
onto
x_{1}
.

(2)

Let
X
be a topologically finite type
p
-adic formal scheme.
We define a sheaf of complete rings
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
by sending an affinoid perfectoid space
{\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
onto the
(p,\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
-complete ring
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.

8
8

8

Here we mention that the sheaf condition of (the zero-th cohomology of)
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
follows from that of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
in
[
41
, Prop. 8.5]
.

We let
\varphi_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}
be the induced automorphism on
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
, and let
\widetilde{\theta}:{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
be the induced surjection.

Now we introduce the period presheaves that are parametrized by prisms, using the derived relative prismatic cohomology as in
[
7
, Const. 7.6]
.

Definition 5.2

(Prismatic period presheaf)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a bounded prism.
The
prismatic period presheaf
over
(B,J)
, denoted as
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, is a presheaf of complexes that sends an affinoid perfectoid space
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
in the pro-étale site
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
onto the derived relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\in D_{(p,I)\text{-comp}}(B)\subset D(B)
.
The presheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
is equipped with a Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
that is compatible with
\varphi_{B}
.

Analogously, we can define the Frobenius twisted prismatic period presheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1),{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varphi_{B}^{*}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
and its Nygaard (filtered) completion
\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{(1),{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.

Remark 5.3
.

By
[
40
, Thm. 6.5]
, the prismatic period presheaf is naturally isomorphic to the presheaf of complete rings
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{\overline{B}}/B}
for affinoid perfectoid objects over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
To lighten the notations, we use
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
instead, and similarly for the related constructions later.

Remark 5.4

(Base change property)
.

We recall from
[
7
]
that there is a natural base change formula for the relative prismatic cohomology.
Namely given a map of bounded prisms
(B_{1},J_{1})\to(B_{2},J_{2})
in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, there is a natural isomorphism of presheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

(44)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B_{1}}}/B_{1}}\otimes^{L}_{B_{1}}B_{2}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{(p,J_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B_{2}}}/B_{2}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Remark 5.5

(Relation with
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
)
.

For a perfectoid ring
S
, the relative prismatic site
(S_{\overline{B}}/(B,J))_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
admits a forgetful functor to the absolute prismatic site
S_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
So by the initiality of the perfect prism
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
, we get a natural Frobenius equivariant map of presheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

(45)

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\longrightarrow{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B},

which is compatible with the base prisms
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

Thanks to the analysis on the coproduct in
Corollary

2.25
, the prismatic period presheaf becomes a sheaf and admits simpler and more explicit presentations in favorable situations.
To see this, we first note the following observation on the reduced perfection of a framed regular prism.

Lemma 5.6

(Perfection and pro-étale cover)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a framed regular prism with the framing
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
, and let
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},I_{\mathrm{perf}})
be its perfection.
The generic fiber
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}
is an affinoid perfectoid space and is a pro-finite-étale cover of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})_{\eta}

Proof.

As
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},I_{\mathrm{perf}})
is a perfect prism, by
[
7
, Thm. 3.10]
, the reduction
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
is a perfectoid ring.
Thus the generic fiber
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}
is an affinoid perfectoid space that admits a map to
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})_{\eta}
.
So it remains to prove that the latter map of adic spaces is pro-finite-étale.

We let the
\delta
-pair
(A_{0},I_{0})
be the framing of the prism
(A,I)
, so
A_{0}
is
p
-completely étale over
W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
and
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0}/I_{0})={\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})
.
By construction, the Frobenius morphism
\varphi_{A_{0}}
is a finite flat cover that is compatible with
\varphi_{W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle}:W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle\to W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
,
t_{i}\mapsto t_{i}^{p}
.
Moreover, since the map
\varphi_{W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle}
induces a finite étale cover on the generic fiber and since the map of rigid spaces
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle)_{\eta}
is étale, the compatibility of Frobenii implies that the map
\varphi_{A_{0}}
induces a finite étale endomorphism
(\varphi_{A_{0}})_{\eta}:{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}
on the smooth affinoid rigid space
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}
.
Hence by taking the limit of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}
with respect to a countable amount of
(\varphi_{A_{0}})_{\eta}
and by
[
40
, Lem. 4.5]
, we see
{\mathrm{Spf}}\bigl((\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}\bigr)_{\eta}
is a pro-finite-étale cover of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}
.
Finally, by the last paragraph of the proof for
Proposition

2.8
, the reduced perfection
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
is the mod
I_{0}
reduction of the ring
(\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}
.
Hence the claim follows from the following pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spf}}\bigl((\colim_{\varphi_{A_{0}}}A_{0})^{\wedge}_{p}\bigr)_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Spf}}(A_{0})_{\eta}.}
pro-finite-étale
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

∎

Recall from
[
40
, §2]
that for a perfectoid ring
S
and a pseudo-uniformizer
\pi^{\flat}\in S^{\flat}
, one can define the notion of the almost zero module over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
with respect to the ideal
([\pi^{\flat}]^{1/p^{n}},n>0)\subset{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.
Namely an
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
-module
M
is
almost zero
if
M
is killed by
([\pi^{\flat}]^{1/p^{n}},n>0)
.
The notion is independent of the choice of
\pi^{\flat}
, and we use
M\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny a}}}}{{=}}}0
to denote the condition that
M
is almost zero.

Theorem 5.7

(Sheaf property)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, let
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a framed regular prism for a framing
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
, with
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
the reduction of its perfection.
Assume
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is a flat prism or a flat crystalline prism.

(i)

The restriction of the presheaf
\mathcal{H}^{0}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
onto
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
is a Frobenius equivariant
B
-linear sheaf of complete modules and is
(p,J)
-completely flat over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
.

(ii)

For any affinoid perfectoid space
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
, one has

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B};\quad\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U,{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny a}}}}{{=}}}0,~\forall i>0.

Proof.

We let
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)
be an affinoid perfectoid space over
X
that admits a map from
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}
.
By
Corollary

2.25
.
(i)
, we know the relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\in D_{(p,I)\text{-comp}}(B)
lives in cohomological degree zero and is completely flat over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.
So it is left to check the sheaf property and calculate the higher cohomology.

We let
U^{0}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S^{0}[1/p],S^{0})
be any pro-étale cover of
U
, and let
U^{n}={\mathrm{Spa}}(S^{n}[1/p],S^{n})
be the
(n+1)
-th self product of
U^{0}
over
U
.
So the cosimplicial object
U^{\bullet}
forms the Čech nerve of the pro-étale covering map
U^{0}\to U
.
To check the sheaf condition, it is equivalent to showing that the sequence below is left exact

(46)

0\longrightarrow{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\longrightarrow{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S^{0}_{\overline{B}}/B}\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S^{1}_{\overline{B}}/B}.

Here we note that by the coproduct presentation in
Corollary

2.25
.
(i)
, the sequence in (
46
) is naturally equivalent to the sequence

(47)

\left({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}\bigl(0\longrightarrow{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S^{0})\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S^{1})\bigr)\right)^{\wedge}_{(p,I)}.

Note that each term in the sequence above is
I
-complete and
I
-torsionfree.
So by the left exactness of the inverse limit functor, it suffices to check the left exactness of (
47
) at their mod
(I,p)
-reduction.
In the latter setting, the claim follows from the sheaf property of
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(-)/(p,I)=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/p
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
(
[
41
, Prop. 8.5]
), together with the flatness of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}/(p,I)
over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(p,I)=S/p
.

We let
\mathfrak{m}
be the ideal
([\pi^{\flat}]^{1/p^{n}},n>0)
in
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.
To show the almost vanishing of the higher cohomology, by the similar reasoning as above, it is equivalent to checking that for any
n>0
, the
n
-th cohomology group of the following complex is almost zero:

(48)

C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes^{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}\bigl({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S^{0})\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S^{1})\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}+p_{2}}\cdots\bigr)\right)^{\wedge}_{(p,I)}.

Note that by the exactness of the mod
(p,I)^{n}
reduction of (
47
) as in the last paragraph above, we have
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)\simeq R\lim_{n}\mathrm{H}^{0}(C\otimes^{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(p,I)^{n})
.
In particular, the canonical truncation of
C
satisfies the isomorphism

\tau^{\geq 1}C\simeq R\lim_{n}\tau^{\geq 1}(C\otimes^{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(p,I)^{n}).

We then claim that each
M_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\tau^{\geq 1}(C\otimes^{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(p,I)^{n})
is almost zero.
Granting the claim, since each reduction
M_{n}
is a complex over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(\mathfrak{m},(p,I)^{n})
, and by taking the limit we see
\tau^{\geq 1}C=R\lim_{n}M_{n}
is a complex over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/\mathfrak{m}^{\wedge}_{(p,I)}
.
Since the surjection
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/\mathfrak{m}^{\wedge}_{(p,I)}
factors through
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/\mathfrak{m}
, we know
\tau^{\geq 1}C
is also a complex over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/\mathfrak{m}
, and hence almost zero with respect to
\mathfrak{m}
.

Now we check that
\tau^{\geq 1}\bigl(C\otimes^{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(p,I)^{n}\bigr)
is almost zero, and by induction we let
n=1
.
In this case, we recall that the ring
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}/(p,I)
is flat over
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)/(p,I)=S/p
.
Since being almost zero is preserved under flat base change, it amounts to show that the
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
-complex below is almost zero with respect to
\mathfrak{m}

\tau^{\geq 1}\left(\bigl({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S^{0})\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S^{1})\xrightarrow{p_{0}-p_{1}+p_{2}}\cdots\bigr)\otimes^{L}_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}S/p\right).

The latter was shown in
[
7
, Prop. 8.5]
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Note that in the special case when
(B,J)=(A,I)
is a framed regular prism, we obtain the following explicit description of the prismatic period sheaf.

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, let
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a framed regular prism with framing
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
, and let
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},I_{\mathrm{perf}})
be the perfection.
There is an isomorphsm of Frobenius equivalent
(p,I)
-complete
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
-linear sheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}

{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\{\frac{\iota(t_{1})\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{1}}{I},\ldots,\frac{\iota(t_{d})\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{d}}{I}\}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}).

In particular,
\mathcal{H}^{0}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A})
is completely free over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
.

Proof.

By assumption, for each affinoid perfectoid space
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
, there is a canonical commutative diagram of rings that is functorial in
U
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{perf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}
perfection
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{mod}~I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{mod}~I}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\theta}}

where the existence of the dashed arrow follows from the equivalence of the categories between perfectoid rings and perfect prisms (
[
7
, Thm. 3.10]
).
In particular, we see the coproduct
({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))\coprod(A,I)
is the base change of
(A,I)\coprod(A,I)
along the map
f:(A,I)\to({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))
as above.
Hence the claim follows from
Corollary

2.25
.
(ii)
.
∎

5.2.
Hodge–Tate and de Rham specializations

Similar to the prismatic cohomology, one of the central features of the prismatic period sheaf is that it admits various realizations, as we shall explain in this subsection.

We start with the Hodge–Tate specialization map.

Definition 5.9

(Hodge–Tate specialization)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a bounded prism.

(i)

The
Hodge–Tate period presheaf
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, denoted as
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, is an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
-linear presheaf of complexes that sends an affinoid perfectoid space
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
onto the derived Hodge–Tate prismatic cohomology
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\in D_{p\text{-comp}}(\overline{B})\subset D(B)
.

(ii)

The
Hodge–Tate specialization map
of presheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}\colon{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\longrightarrow\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B},

is defined by the derived mod
J
reduction on the source.

The conjugate filtration on the relative Hodge–Tate cohomology (
[
7
]
) naturally extends to a filtration on the Hodge–Tate period presheaf.

Corollary 5.10

(Conjugate filtration)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a bounded prism.

(i)

There is an
\mathbb{N}
-indexed ascending and exhaustive
conjugate filtration

\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{\bullet}
on
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, with its
i
-th graded piece being the presheaf of complexes
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\{-i\}[-i]
.

(ii)

Assume
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is a framed regular prism with the framing
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
, and let
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
be the reduction of the perfection of
A
.
In addition, we assume
(B,J)
is a flat prism or a flat crystalline prism.
The restriction of
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
is isomorphic to the presheaf of
p
-complete
\overline{B}
-modules

\left(\Gamma^{i}\mathbb{L}_{A_{0}/W}\{-i\}\otimes^{L}_{A_{0}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B}\right)^{\wedge}_{p}.

In particular, the zero-th cohomology sheaves of both
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}^{{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\{-i\}[-i]|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
and
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
are sheaves of finite projective modules over
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{\overline{B}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
.

Here we emphasize that the isomorphism in
Corollary

5.10
.
(ii)
is
not
