Galois equivariant with respect to the pro-étale cover
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})_{\eta}
.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from the
p
-complete left Kan extension of the usual conjugate filtration as in
[
7
, Thm. 1.14]
.
The identification of the cotangent complex in
(ii)
follows from
Proposition

2.24
.(
(ii)
and
(iii)
).
The sheaf property follows from that of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
(
[
41
, Prop. 8.5]
) together with the flatness.
∎

Another useful construction is the de Rham specialization constructed below.

Definition 5.11

(de Rham specialization)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a bounded prism.

(i)

The
de Rham period presheaf
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, denoted as
{\mathrm{dR}}^{{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
, is an
\overline{B}
-linear presheaf of
p
-complete complexes that sends an affinoid perfectoid space
U={\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
onto the
p
-complete derived de Rham complex
{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\in D_{p\text{-comp}}(\overline{B})\subset D(B)
.

(ii)

The
de Rham specialization map
of presheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
is the canonical composition

\gamma^{\mathrm{dR}}\colon{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\longrightarrow{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1),{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}.

By the relative prismatic–de Rham comparison theorem (
[
7
, Thm. 1.8.(3)]
,
[
4
, Prop. 5.2.3]
), the de Rham realization map is equal to the
p
-complete derived pullback of the source along the composition
B\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B}}B\xrightarrow{\text{mod}~J~}\overline{B}
.

Remark 5.12

(Hodge filtration)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a bounded prism.
There is an
\mathbb{N}
-indexed descending
Hodge filtration
on
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
, with its
i
-th graded piece being the presheaf of the complex
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[-i]
.
The zero-th graded piece then produces a natural map of presheaves of rings

{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\longrightarrow(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}.

Moreover, we can define the Hodge-filtered completed de Rham period presheaf
\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}^{{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}/\overline{B}}}
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.

Analogous to the Hodge–Tate period sheaf, we also obtain the following property of the de Rham period sheaf when the base prism admits a flatness assumption.
Note that this in particular generalizes the construction of
[
26
]
to the regular setting.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, and let
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a framed regular prism with framing
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
, with
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
the reduction of its perfection.
Assume
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is a flat prism or a flat crystalline prism.

(i)

The restriction of the presheaf
\mathcal{H}^{0}({\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}})
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
is a sheaf in
\overline{B}
-modules.

(ii)

Assume
(B,J)=(A,I)
.
Then the restriction of the presheaf
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/\overline{A}}
onto
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
is naturally isomorphic to the complete divided power polynomial ring over
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
as below

{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/\overline{A}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}\{\overline{t}_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{i}\}^{\mathrm{pd}},

where
\overline{t}_{i}
is the image of
t_{i}\in A
along the map
A\to\overline{A}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
, and the base map
\overline{A}\to{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/\overline{A}}
sends
t_{i}
onto
1\otimes t_{i}
.

Proof.

By
Corollary

5.8
, we know the restriction of
\mathcal{H}^{0}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A})
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
is a sheaf in
(p,I)
-complete modules and is completely flat over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
.
On the other hand, by
Proposition

2.8
the map
\varphi_{A}:A\to A
is finite flat.
So the zero-th cohomology sheaf of the Frobenius pullback
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1),{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}=\varphi_{A}^{*}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}
is a sheaf and the composition
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1),{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}
is completely flat as well.
In particular, thanks to the
I
-completeness and
I
-torsionfreeness of
\mathcal{H}^{0}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1),{\mathrm{pre}}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A})
, the mod
I
reduction is a sheaf in
p
-completely flat modules over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I)
.
The latter, combined with the
p
-complete sheaf property of
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I)
, implies the sheaf property of
\mathcal{H}^{0}({\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/\overline{A}})
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
, together with its
p
-complete flatness over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I)
.
Here we note that since
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})\to X
is
p
-completely étale and the map
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})_{\eta}
is pro-finite-étale, for any affinoid perfectoid space
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(S[1/p],S)\in X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
, the structural map
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)\to X
factors through the étale morphism
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})\to X
.
In particular, the restriction
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/\overline{A}}
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
is naturally isomorphic to that of
\bigl({\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)/X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{A}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}
.
As a consequence, the presheaf of
p
-complete modules
\mathrm{H}^{0}({\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)/X})
is a sheaf of
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree modules on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
.

For more general base, if
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is a transversal flat prism, then
Definition

2.20
implies that the map of
p
-adic formal scheme
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to X
is
p
-completely flat.
So the sheaf property of
\mathcal{H}^{0}({\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}})
follows from that of the
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{0}({\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)/X})
, together with the
p
-completely flat base change along
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to X
.
For the second scenario when
(B,J)
is a crystalline flat prism, the
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme
\Spec(\overline{B})
is flat over the reduction
X_{p=0}
by
Definition

2.20
.
So the sheaf property of the
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-linear presheaf
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
follows from that of the mod
p
reduction of the sheaf
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)/X}
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}}
, together with the flat base change of the algebraic derived de Rham complex.
This finishes the proof of Part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, the claim follows from the explicit presentation of the relative prismatic cohomology in
Corollary

5.8
, together with the formula that
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/\overline{A}}=(\varphi_{A}^{*}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A})\otimes_{A}\overline{A}
(
Definition

5.11
). ∎

5.3.
Period sheaves and their global sections

In this subsection, we introduce the integral prismatic period sheaves and calculate their global sections.

Definition 5.14

(Prismatic period sheaves)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism or a flat crystalline prism.
We define the following sheaves of complete complexes over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
:

(1)

The
prismatic period sheaf
over
(B,J)
, denoted as
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, is the
(p,J)
-complete hypercomplete sheafification of the presheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.

(2)

The
Hodge–Tate period sheaf
over
(B,J)
, denoted as
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, is the
p
-complete hypercomplete sheafification of the presheaf
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.

(3)

The
de Rham period sheaf
over
(B,J)
, denoted as
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
, is the
p
-complete hypercomplete sheafification of the presheaf
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
.

(4)

We use
\mathbb{L}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
to denote the
p
-complete hypercomplete sheafification of the presheaf of
p
-complete cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
.

Analogously, we may define the Frobenius twisted version
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
and its Nygaard-filtered completion
\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, together with the Hodge-filtered completion
\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
, which we would not repeat.

By
Lemma

5.6
, given a regular
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and a framed regular prism
(A,I)
of
X
with
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
, the generic fiber of the reduction of the perfection
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})
forms a pro-étale cover of the rigid space
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})_{\eta}
.
Moreover, since each point in
X
admits an étale neighborhood that lifts to a framed regular prism (
Footnote

3
), the above allows us to produce a covering of the pro-étale site of
X_{\eta}
.
Namely, we let
\{(A_{i},I_{i})\}_{i}
be a collection of framed regular prisms in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
such that
\{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{i})\}_{i}
covers
X
in the
p
-completely étale topology, then the affinoid perfectoid space
Y_{\eta}=\coprod_{i}{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{i,{\mathrm{perf}}})_{\eta}
is a covering object in
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
On the other hand, by
Theorem

5.7
.
(i)
(resp.
Corollary

5.10
.
(ii)
, resp.
Proposition

5.13
.
(i)
), we know the zero-th cohomology presheaf of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}|_{Y_{\eta}}
(resp.
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}|_{Y_{\eta}}
, resp.
{\mathrm{dR}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}|_{Y_{\eta}}
) satisfies the sheaf condition over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}|_{Y_{\eta}}
.

Corollary 5.15
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism or a flat crystalline prism, and let
Y_{\eta}
be the affinoid perfectoid space mentioned above.
The restriction of the period sheaf
\mathcal{H}^{0}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
on
Y_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
is naturally isomorphic to the presheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}|_{Y_{\eta}}
.
Similarly for
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
and
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
.

We now calculate the global section of various period sheaves.
We start with the de Rham cohomology.

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be an affine regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

(i)

The structural map below is an isomorphism

R\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{gr}}^{0}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}),

and
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})=0
for
i\geq 1
.

(ii)

Let
\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)/X}
be the Hodge-filtered-completion of
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
.
The canonical map

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)/X})

is an injection of
p
-complete modules.
In particular, we have a natural isomorphism
R\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})
.

Proof.

Notice that each graded piece
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
is naturally isomorphic to the sheaf of
p
-complete Kähler differentials
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}[-i]
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
The latter by
Proposition

2.24
.
(ii)
is an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
-vector bundle and in particular is
p
-torsionfree.
So Part
(i)
follows from
Lemma

4.18
and the vanishing result in

Proposition

4.15
.
(i)
.

The injectivity in Part
(ii)
is equivalent to the separatedness of the Hodge filtration on
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})
.
To prove the latter, we notice that the map
R\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
factors through
R\to(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}\otimes_{W}R)^{\wedge}_{p}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
, where the first arrow is relatively perfect and the second arrow is a surjection.
In particular, the maps induce a natural filtered isomorphism

{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\simeq{\mathrm{dR}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/(\mathbb{A}_{\inf}\otimes_{W}R)^{\wedge}_{p}}.

To analyze the above de Rham cohomology, we may assume
X
(hence
X_{\eta}
) is connected.
By shrinking
X
in étale topology if necessary, we let
(A,I=(d))
be a framed regular prism with a framing
A_{0}=W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
and assume
\overline{A}=R
.
Assume
S
is the perfectoid ring
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
, so that there is an induced map of prisms
f:(A,I)\to({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})
, which by the noetherian property of
A
and
Proposition

2.8
is a faithfully flat cover.
Here we notice that by
Lemma

5.6
the generic fiber of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)
is a pro-finite-étale cover of
X_{\eta}
, and by the sheaf property in
Proposition

5.13
it suffices to show that the Hodge filtration is separated on
{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/R}
.

Now we consider the following cartesian diagram of rings

(49)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1,\Delta)\simeq R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{W}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S))^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\otimes_{W}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S))^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1,\Delta)\simeq(R\otimes_{W}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S))^{\wedge}_{p}/(\Delta)\simeq S,}

where
\Delta=(t_{1}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\otimes 1-t_{n}\otimes 1)
is the diagonal ideal of
(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{p}
, and the vertical maps are faithfully flat.
Notice that since the sequence of elements
\{t_{1}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\otimes 1-t_{n}\otimes 1,d\otimes 1\}
is regular (and thus Koszul-regular) in the noetherian ring
(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{p}
, by
[
43
,
Tag 09CC
]
we can switch the positions without affecting the regularity.
In particular, the elements
\{t_{1}\otimes 1-1\otimes t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\otimes 1-t_{n}\otimes 1\}
is a regular sequence in the ring
(A\otimes_{W}A)^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1)
, and hence also in the ring
(A\otimes_{W}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S))^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1)
by the faithfully flatness.
As a consequence, the kernel of the canonical surjection
(R\otimes_{W}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S))^{\wedge}_{p}/(I\otimes 1,\Delta)\to S
is generated by a regular sequence.
Hence the claim follows from the more general observation on the relative de Rham cohomology as in
Lemma

5.17
. ∎

The following general result on the separatedness of the relative de Rham cohomology was used in the proof, and we thank Shizhang Li for the related discussions.

Lemma 5.17

(Separatedness of the Hodge filtration)
.

Let
A\to B
be a surjection of
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree rings with the kernel generated by a regular sequence.
The Hodge filtration on the
p
-complete de Rham cohomology
{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
is separated.

Proof.

We let
J
be the kernel ideal of the surjection
A\to B
.
We first notice that by
[
9
]
, the relative de Rham cohomology
{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
is naturally filtered isomorphic to the
p
-complete divided power envelope
D_{A}(I)
, where the Hodge filtrations are sent onto the (
p
-complete) divided power filtrations.
As each
\Fil_{H}^{j}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}=\Fil^{j}D_{A}(I)
is a derived
p
-complete submodule of
D_{A}(I)
, the intersection
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\cap_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\Fil^{j}D_{A}(I)
is also
p
-complete and
p
-torsionfree.
Thus by the derived Nakayama lemma, to show that
M=0
, it suffices to check it after the derived mod
p
reduction.
So from now on we assume
A\to B
is a regular surjection of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras.
Moreover, by the Künneth formula in
[
9
, Prop. 2.7, Lem. 3.37]
and the aforementioned description of the Hodge filtration, we may assume the kernel is generated by one regular element
f\in A
.
Under the latter assumption, the ring
D_{A}(I)
is naturally isomorphic to the quotient of the divided power polynomial ring, namely

D_{A}(I)\simeq A\{x\}^{{\mathrm{pd}}}/(x-f)=(\bigoplus_{j}A\cdot\gamma_{j}(x))/(f-x),

where
\gamma_{j}(x)
is the
j
-th divided power structure of the element
x
.
In addition, the
j
-th Hodge filtration of
D_{A}(I)
is the submodule generated by
\{\gamma_{i}(x)~|~i\geq j\}
.

To continue, we notice that since
A
is
f
-torsionfree, its
f
-adic completion is flat over
\mathbb{F}_{p}\llbracket x\rrbracket
.
In particular, there is an affine open neighborhood
\Spec(A^{\prime})
of
\Spec(B)=V(f)
in
\Spec(A)
such that
A^{\prime}
is flat over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[x]
.
Note that the étaleness of
A\to A^{\prime}
implies that
{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}={\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A^{\prime}}
.
So by replacing
A
with
A^{\prime}
if necessary, we may assume
A
is flat over
\mathbb{F}_{p}[x]
.

Furthermore, we recall that the ring
{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
naturally admits an ascending and exhaustive conjugate filtration
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
.
In particular, each element
x\in M\subseteq{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
is contained in
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
We then claim that for each
n
, there is an integer
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}\cap\Fil^{m}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}=0
.
Granting the claim, if the intersection
M\neq 0
, we may pick a nonzero element
x\in M
, which is contained in
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}
for some
n
.
The latter however is impossible, since
x\notin\Fil^{m}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{B/A}\supseteq M
.

Finally we check the claim.
By the flat base change formulae of the conjugate and the Hodge filtrations, it suffices to assume
A=\mathbb{F}_{p}[x]
and
f=x
.
Then the divided power envelope
D_{A}(I)
is equal to the divided power polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}_{p}\{x\}^{{\mathrm{pd}}}
, where
\Fil^{j}_{H}D_{A}(I)
is the submodule
\bigoplus_{i\geq j}\mathbb{F}_{p}\cdot\gamma_{i}(x)
.
On the other hand, by
[
9
, Proof of Lem. 3.42]
, we know the conjugate filtration
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{n}D_{A}(I)
is the
\mathbb{F}_{p}[x]/x^{p}
-submodule
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}\mathbb{F}_{p}[x]/x^{p}\cdot\gamma_{ip}(x)
.
In particular, by comparing the descriptions of the Hodge filtration with that of the conjugate filtration, we notice that for a fixed integer
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the intersection of
\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{n}D_{A}(I)
and
\Fil^{m}_{H}D_{A}(I)
is zero if
m>pn+p-1
.
∎

Corollary 5.18
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism, and let
S
be the perfectoid ring over
X
given by the reduction of the perfection of a framed regular prism.
Then the Nygaard filtration on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
is separated.

Proof.

Notice that the Nygaard filtration is finer than the
J
-adic filtration on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, where the latter is classically
J
-adically complete and is in particular
J
-adically separated.
So it suffices to check that the mod
J
-reduction of the Nygaard filtration, which by
[
7
]
is the Hodge filtration on
{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
, is separated.
Hence the claim follows from
Proposition

2.8
, the flatness assumption of the prism
(B,J)
in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, and
Lemma

5.17
.
∎

Below for a bounded prism
(B,J)
and a
p
-adic formal scheme
Y
over
\overline{B}
, we call the tautological map
f:B\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{Y/B}
the structure map, and call its Frobenius pullback
\varphi_{B}^{*}(f):B\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{Y/B}
the twisted structure map.

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism.

(i)

The structural map induces an isomorphism

\overline{B}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{gr}}^{\mathrm{conj}}_{0}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}),

and
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{gr}}^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})=0
for
i\geq 1
.

(ii)

The twisted structural map induces an isomorphism

B\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}).

(iii)

Assume either
(B,J)
is a coproduct of framed regular prisms, or the map
\varphi_{B}
is completely faithfully flat.
Then the structural map induces an isomorphism

B\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}).

Proof.

For Part
(i)
, we notice that each graded piece
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
is naturally isomorphic to the sheaf of
p
-complete Kähler differentials
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{-i\}[-i]
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
By the flat base change property of the cotangent complex, we further have

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i}\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\simeq\left(\bigl(\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}[-i]\bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{-i\}\right)^{\wedge}_{p}.

By
Proposition

2.24
.
(ii)
, we know
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}[-i]
is an
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{+}
-vector bundle and in particular is
p
-torsionfree.
So Part
(i)
follows from
Lemma

4.18
(for
i=0
) and the vanishing result in

Proposition

4.15
.
(ii)
(for
T=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
i>0
), together with the projection formula
Theorem

4.12
.
(a)
(which is applicable thanks to the flatness of
\overline{B}
over
X
and
Corollary

4.17
).

For Part
(ii)
, we notice that
Proposition

5.16
and the filtered version of the prismatic-de Rham comparison theorem (
[
7
]
) implies the natural map below is an isomorphism

B\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}),

where
\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
is the sheaf associated to the Nygaard-completed twisted relative prismatic cohomology.
The above map can be naturally factored as

(50)

B\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\widehat{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}),

which implies that the first arrow is an injection.
So to prove
(ii)
, it suffices to check that the second arrow above is also injective; or in other words the Nyggard filtration on
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
is separated.

As the statement is
p
-completely étale local with respect to
X
, by shrinking
X
in étale topology if necessary, we assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine and admits an auxiliary framed regular prism
(A,I=(d))
with a framing
A_{0}=W\langle t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{n}^{\pm 1}\rangle
and the equality
\overline{A}=R
.
Assume
S
is the perfectoid ring
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
.
Then by
Lemma

5.6
, we know the generic fiber of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)
is a pro-finite-étale cover of
X_{\eta}
, and by
Theorem

5.7
it suffices to show that the Nygaard filtration is separated on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, which was verified in
Corollary

5.18
.

Finally, we consider Part
(iii)
, where we denote the tautological arrow
B\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
by
g
.
Let
S
be the perfectoid ring considered above, and we first assume
\varphi_{B}
is completely faithfully flat.
There is a natural injection of derived
(p,J)
-complete modules
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, such that
(J,p)
forms a regular sequence for each of them.
Now we notice that by applying
Lemma

4.9
at the homomorphism
\varphi_{B}:B\to B
and the map
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\hookrightarrow N\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, we know the completed base change below is injective:

(51)

\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\otimes_{B,\varphi_{B}}B\bigr)^{\wedge}_{(p,J)}\longrightarrow{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}.

In addition, the map (
51
) naturally factors through the subring
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
.
So by combining with (
50
), we obtain the natural
B
-linear injective arrows as below

B\hookrightarrow\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\otimes_{B,\varphi_{B}}B\bigr)^{\wedge}_{(p,J)}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\hookrightarrow{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},

where the first arrow is the base change of the map
g
by
\varphi_{B}
, and the composition of the first two arrows by Part
(ii)
is an isomorphism.
Thus the canonical map
B\to\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\otimes_{B,\varphi_{B}}B\bigr)^{\wedge}_{(p,J)}
is isomorphic.
As a consequence, the complete faithfully flatness of the map
\varphi_{B}
now implies that the derived complete complex
{\mathrm{cofib}}(g)
vanishes itself, and thus
g
is an isomorphism.

If
(B,J)
is a coproduct of framed regular prisms, then we can consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where the vertical arrows are the linearization maps along
\varphi_{B}
, and all the arrows are injective (
Proposition

2.27
).
Moreover, the bottom left horizontal arrow by
(ii)
is an isomorphism.
Hence the surjectivity of the map
g
follows from the fact that the outer square is cartesian, as in
Proposition

2.27
.
(iii)
.
∎

5.4.
Rational period sheaves

Similarly to the various period rings in the classical
p
-adic Hodge theory, the prismatic period sheaves defined in
Definition

5.2
admit rational analogues.
However, to yield an integral Galois invariant, we consider the localization by inverting the element
\mu\in{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}
, instead of the element
p
in the classical situations.

We start by recalling the
F
-gauge structure on the Breuil–Kisin twist.

Construction 5.20

(Breuil–Kisin twist as an
F
-gauge)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a transversal prism.
The first Breuil–Kisin twist over
A
is the invertible
A
-module

A\{1\}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lim_{\cdot\frac{1}{p}}I_{r}/I_{r}^{2},

where
I_{r}
is the ideal
I\cdot\varphi_{A}(I)\cdots\varphi_{A}^{r-1}(I)
in
A
(
[
4
, Const. 2.2.11]
).
It satisfies the natural base change formula with respect to maps of prisms, making it an invertible prismatic crystal
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\{1\}
over
(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
Moreover, as in
[
4
, Const. 2.2.14]
, Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi_{A}
induces a natural isomorphism

(52)

\varphi_{A\{1\}}:\varphi_{A}^{*}(A\{1\})\longrightarrow\frac{1}{I}\cdot A\{1\}.

The latter allows us to enhance
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\{1\}
with a canonical filtration by taking the Frobenius preimage as in
[
27
, Thm. 1.2]
.
Concretely, assume
(A,I)=({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I)
for a quasiregular semiperfectoid ring
S
.
we then equip
\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\{1\}
with the filtration such that its value at
(A,I)
is

\Fil^{i}(A\{1\})=\{x\in A\{1\}~|~\widetilde{\varphi}_{A\{1\}}(x)\in I^{i}A\{1\}\subset\frac{1}{I}\cdot A\{1\}\},

where
\widetilde{\varphi}_{A}
is the precomposition of
\varphi_{A}
with the Frobenius-linearization
A\{1\}\to\varphi_{A}^{*}(A\{1\})
.
So for
i\leq-1
, we have
\Fil^{i}(A\{1\})=A\{1\}
.
For example, in the special case when
S
is perfectoid, by (
52
) the submodule
\Fil^{i}A\{1\}
is isomorphic to
\varphi_{A}^{-1}(I)^{i+1}A\{1\}
through the Frobenius structure for
i\geq-1
.
As shown in
[
27
]
, each filtered module
\Fil^{\bullet}(A\{1\})
satisfies the filtered base change formula among prisms arising from the syntomic site
(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
, making
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\{1\}
an invertible
F
-gauge.
In addition, the similar construction extends to
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\{n\}=(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\{1\})^{\otimes n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

To analyze the rational prismatic period sheaf, the following construction plays an important role.

Construction 5.21

(The twist by
\mu
)
.

We let
K_{\infty}
be a perfectoid Galois extension of
K
that contains all the
p
-power roots of unity
\zeta_{p^{n}}
.
Then by choosing a compatible system of
\zeta_{p^{n}}
, we can define the element
\mu=[\epsilon]-1\in{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
, which satisfies the formula

(53)

\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})}(\mu)=\mu\cdot\frac{[\epsilon]^{p}-1}{[\epsilon]-1}.

The latter implies that the invertible ideal
\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\subset{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
is naturally a Frobenius submodule of height
[1,1]
.
In particular, we can define a natural filtration using the Frobenius structure, namely

\Fil^{i}\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{x\in\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})~|~\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}(x)\in I^{i}\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\}.

Here we note that the submodule
\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\subset{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
and its filtrations are preserved under the natural action of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
, and the inclusion map is filtered.

Conceptually, as explained in
[
5
, Ex. 4.24]
(cf.
[
4
, §2.6]
), the submodule
\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
admits a canonical (Galois and Frobenius) equivariant isomorphism with the double twist

{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\{-1\}(1)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\{-1\}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1),

where
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\{-1\}
is the Breuil–Kisin twist and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
is the Tate twist.
Moreover, the
i
-th filtration on
\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
is naturally isomorphic to
\Fil^{i}({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\{-1\})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)
, which we abbreviate as
\Fil^{i}\bigl({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\{-1\}(1)\bigr)
.
As before, the similar constructions naturally extend to
\mu^{n}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The following statement describes the difference among the different twists
\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
.

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
n\geq m
be two integers, and let
\iota_{n,m}
be the canonical filtered inclusion
\mu^{n}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\to\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
.
We have the following descriptions of Galois modules:

(i)

As submodules in
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})[\frac{1}{\mu}]
, we have

\Fil^{i}\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})=\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(I)^{\max\{i,m\}}\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(\mu)^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}).

(ii)

The associated graded of
\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
is given by

{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})=\begin{cases}(\zeta_{p}-1)^{m}\cdot\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(I)^{i}/\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(I)^{i+1},~i\geq m;\\
0,~i<m,\end{cases}

which is functorial in
i
and
m
.
In particular, the entire associated graded
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
is an invertible module over
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}}={\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(I)}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
, and the induced map of graded pieces
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\iota_{n,m})
is injective.

(iii)

The
i
-th graded module of the cokernel of
\iota_{n,m}
is given by

{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\coker(\iota_{n,m})=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\bigl(\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\bigr)/(\zeta_{p}-1)^{n-m}\simeq(\zeta_{p}-1)^{m}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}\{i\}\cdot(\zeta_{p}-1)^{m}/(\zeta_{p}-1)^{n},~i\geq n;\\
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\bigl(\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\bigr)\simeq(\zeta_{p}-1)^{m}\varphi^{-1}(I)^{i}/\varphi^{-1}(I)^{i+1}=(\zeta_{p}-1)^{m}\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}\{i\},~m\leq i<n;\\
0,~i<m.\end{cases}

Here we note that the above descriptions extend to
p
-torsionfree perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}
-algebras
S
, and we use
\iota_{n,m,S}
to denote the induced map.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from (
53
) together with the fact that the map
\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}:{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\to{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
is an isomorphism.
The description and the invertibility of the graded piece in Part
(ii)
are direct consequences of Part
(i)
, together with the observation that the image of
\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(\mu)
in
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})/\varphi_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}}^{-1}(I)
is
\zeta_{p}-1
.
For the injectivity of the map
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\iota_{n,m})
, it suffices to notice that by
5.21
we have

\Fil^{i}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})=\bigl(\mu^{n}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\bigr)\bigcap\Fil^{i}\mu^{m}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}),~\forall i.

Part
(iii)
follows from Part
(ii)
.
∎

Another preparation is a description of the canonical map between the absolute and the relative prismatic cohomology, as analyzed by Bhatt–Lurie in
[
4
]
.

Theorem 5.23

(Bhatt–Lurie)
.

Let
S
be a ring, and let
(A,I)
be a bounded prism.

(i)

There is a canonical commutative diagram of filtered
\mathrm{E}_{\infty}
-algebras

(54)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\overline{A}})^{\wedge}_{p}/A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{A}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}

where the horizontal arrows are the absolute and the relative prismatic–de Rham realization morphisms respectively, as in
[
4
, Const. 5.5.3, Cor. 5.2.8]
,
and the right vertical arrow is the tensor product of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\overline{A}
.

(ii)

Assume
S
is quasiregular semiperfectoid.
For the given
i\in\mathbb{N}
, the induced map

(55)

{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}(\gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{dR}})\colon{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S}

is an inclusion of
S
-modules with the cokernel killed by a bounded power of
p
.

Proof.

For Part
(i)
, we recall from
[
4
, Const. 5.5.3, Cor. 5.6.3]
that the Nygaard filtered absolute prismatic cohomology can be defined using the pullback diagram

(56)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{(B,J)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{(B,J)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\bigl(\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{dR}}}

where the right vertical map is the limit of the tensor product maps, and the bottom arrow is the relative prismatic–de Rham comparison.
So Part
(i)
follows by combining (
56
) with the tautological projection diagram as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{(B,J)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{(B,J)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\bigl(\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{A}}/A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{A}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}.}

To see Part
(ii)
, we recall from the proof of
[
4
, Prop. 5.5.12]
that the fiber of the morphism
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}(\gamma_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{dR}})
is isomorphic to the complex

C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl(\Fil^{\mathrm{conj}}_{i-1}\widehat{\Omega}_{S}^{\not}{D}\bigr)^{\Theta=-i},

where
\widehat{\Omega}_{S}^{\not}{D}
is the diffracted Hodge complex as in
[
4
, §4.7]
.
By
[
4
, Not. 4.7.2]
, the complex
C
admits a finite filtration with its non-zero graded pieces given by

C_{j}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\wedge^{j}\mathbb{L}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[-j]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\xrightarrow{\cdot(j-i)}\mathbb{Z}_{p})

for
j\in\{0,1,\ldots,i-1\}
.
On the other hand, since
S
is quasiregular semiperfectoid (
[
7
, Not. 7.1]
), we know each
\wedge^{j}\mathbb{L}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[-j]
is a
p
-torsionfree module in cohomological degree zero.
Hence the complex
C_{j}
lives in cohomological degree
1
and is killed by the integer
(j-i)
.
As a consequence, the map (
55
) is injective with the cokernel killed by a power of
p
.
∎

We then extend the analysis of
Proposition

5.22
and
Theorem

5.23
and consider the base change of the morphism
\iota_{n,m}
to the relative prismatic cohomology of a perfectoid algebra.

Proposition 5.24
.

Let
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
(B,J)
be a flat prism, and let
S
be a perfectoid ring over
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}}
.
For
n\geq m
, let
\widetilde{\iota}_{n,m}
be the
(p,J)
-complete filtered base change of
\iota_{n,m,S}
along the morphism

\Fil^{\bullet}_{\varphi^{-1}(I)}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)=\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\to\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
There is a natural graded isomorphism of graded abelian groups (in cohomological degree zero)

(57)

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{cofib}}(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})[1/p]\simeq M_{S_{\overline{B}}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{S}S(n),

where
M_{S_{\overline{B}}}^{\bullet}
is a graded and functorial
S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
-module of degree
\geq n
, such that each
M_{S_{\overline{B}}}^{i}
admits a natural finite filtration whose graded piece is of the form
\bigl(\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{R/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\{j\}\bigr)\otimes_{\overline{B}}S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
.

Before the proof, we remind the reader the convention that
S_{\overline{B}}
is defined as
(S\otimes_{R}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}
.

Proof.

By switching the order of taking the associated graded and the filtered base change, we obtain an identification of the graded maps

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})\simeq({\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\iota_{n+1,n,S})\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},

and similarly their cofibers.
Here we notice that by
Proposition

5.22
.
(ii)
, the cofiber (which coincides with the cokernel) of the map
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\iota_{n,m,S})
is a perfect graded complex over
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.
So by
Proposition

5.22
.
(iii)
and by killing the
p
-torsions, we get

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})[1/p]\simeq\bigl(S\{n\}\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr)[1/p],

where the action of
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
on
S\{n\}
factors through the graded surjection
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to{\mathrm{gr}}^{0}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\simeq S
and the module
S\{n\}
lives in the graded degree
n
.
In particular, the right hand side above is naturally isomorphic to

\bigl(S\{n\}\otimes_{S}S\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr)[1/p].

So to prove the statement, it amounts to decomposing the graded ring
S\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]
, which we denote by
Q
.

Next, we notice that the graded ring
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]
lives in cohomological degree zero: by Nygaard–conjugate comparison (
[
7
, Thm. 15.2]
), each
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{i}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]
admits a finite filtration whose formation is
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{-i\}[-i][1/p]
, which, thanks to the Faltings extension and the smoothness of the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
, is a finite projective
S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
-module.
In addition, as
\Fil^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
is the
\varphi^{-1}(I)
-adic filtration for an invertible ideal
\varphi^{-1}(I)
, the kernel of
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to S
is a principal ideal generated by
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.
Thus the ring
Q
is naturally isomorphic to the quotient ring of
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]
by the ideal generated by the image of
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
.

To understand the aforementioned image, we let
C
be the
S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
-submodule generated by the image of
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)[1/p]
in
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]
.
Consider the natural commutative diagram as below:

(58)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)={\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S}=\mathbb{L}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}/B}=\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}=\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/R}\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[-1],}

where the left column factorizes the morphism
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)\to{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, the horizontal arrows are absolute and relative prismatic–de Rham realizations, and the right column follows from the functoriality of de Rham cohomology.
By
Theorem

5.23
.
(ii)
, the top horizontal arrow becomes an isomorphism after inverting
p
.
On the other hand, by the proof of
[
7
, Cor. 15.4]
, the bottom horizontal arrow in (
58
) fits naturally into a fiber sequences

(59)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{1\}={\mathrm{gr}}^{0}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}=\mathbb{L}_{S/R}\otimes_{S}S_{\overline{B}}[-1],}

which becomes a short exact sequence of finite projective
S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
-modules after inverting
p
.
Hence by combining (
58
), (
59
) and the aforementioned isomorphism, we see the image of
C
in
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[1/p]
coincides with the image of
\mathbb{L}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{S}S_{\overline{B}}[1/p][-1]
in
\mathbb{L}_{S/R}\otimes_{S}S_{\overline{B}}[1/p][-1]
.
As a consequence, the quotient
{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]/C
functorially fits into a canonical short exact sequence

S_{\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{1\}[1/p]={\mathrm{gr}}^{0}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{1\}[1/p]\longrightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]/C\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{R/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{R}S_{\overline{B}}[1/p].

This finishes the proof for the lowest graded piece.

To extend the above to the higher graded pieces of the ring
Q
, we recall that the Nygaard graded pieces admit a canonical fiber sequence that is compatible with the prismatic–de Rham comparison (
[
4
, Cor. 5.2.8]
)

{\mathrm{gr}}^{n-1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{1\}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{n}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{n}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}.

On the other hand, by the multiplicativity, the graded pieces of the quotient ring
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[1/p]/(\mathbb{L}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}[-1])
is of the form
\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{R/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{R}S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
.
Hence the structure of the general graded piece of the ring
Q
follows by the above short exact sequence and induction. ∎

Now we are ready to define the rational prismatic period sheaf.

Construction 5.25

(Rational prismatic period sheaves)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(B,J)
be a prism over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

(1)

Consider the following tensor product over
(X_{\eta,K_{\infty}})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})[\frac{1}{\mu}],

and similarly for the twisted version
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]=(\varphi_{B}^{*}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})[\frac{1}{\mu}]
.
As the invertible ideal
\mu{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})\subset{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
is invariant under the action of
{\mathrm{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
, the sheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]
then naturally descends to a sheaf onto
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, which we call the
rational prismatic period sheaf
over
(B,J)
.
Note that by
5.21
, the rational period sheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]
admits a canonical presentation

(60)

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]\simeq\colim_{n\geq 0}\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)\bigr),

which in particular does not require the choice of
\mu
.

(2)

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the twisted period sheaf
\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\mathbb{A}_{\inf}}\mathbb{A}_{\inf}\{-n\}(n)
naturally admits a weak Frobenius structure (after inverting the ideal
J
)

\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\longrightarrow\varphi_{B}^{*}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\longrightarrow\mu^{n}J^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}.

In addition, the Frobenius-twisted object
\varphi_{B}^{*}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
admits a canonical filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}\varphi_{B}^{*}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})=\Fil^{\bullet}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n)
that is determined (up to
(p,J)
-complete sheafification) via the preimage of the ideals
J^{\bullet}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
along the Frobenius morphism.
Both the Frobenius structures and the filtrations are compatible with respect to different
n
. By taking the union of
\Fil^{i}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n)
with respect to
n
, we then obtain a filtration on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]
.

The following intersection formula will be used later.

Lemma 5.26
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and let
(B,J)
be a flat prism over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\Fil^{i}\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]\bigr)\bigcap\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}=\Fil^{i}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr).

Proof.

It suffices to check the equality for the sections at a large enough perfectoid pro-étale objects
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta}
over
X_{\eta}
, namely

\Fil^{i}\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]\bigr)\bigcap\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}=\Fil^{i}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr),

which follows from construction.
∎

Assembling various analyses, we obtain the following structural result on the rational prismatic period sheaf, which plays an essential role in the calculation of its global section.

Theorem 5.27
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism, and let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(i)

There are natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}/\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p])

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(n),

where
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}
is a graded sheaf of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{B}}
-vector bundles in degrees
\geq n
on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
, such that each
\mathcal{F}^{i}
admits a finite filtration whose graded piece is an analytic vector bundle of the form
\bigl(\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\overline{B}\{j\}\bigr)\otimes_{\overline{B}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{B}}
.

(ii)

The filtered completion map below is an injection

\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]\longrightarrow\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}.

(iii)

Assume
(B,J)
is a finite coproduct of framed regular prisms.
The cokernel of the map
\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n}\colon\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\to\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
naturally admits injections

\displaystyle\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})\hookrightarrow\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})[1/p]\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\prod_{a\geq 1}\prod_{1\leq b\leq a}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-b}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]\bigr)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\prod_{\text{countable}}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]\bigr)

where the last arrow (on the individual factor) is induced by the
(b-1)
-th power of the absolute Frobenius morphism on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.

Proof.

For
(i)
, as
\mu{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}
is divisible by
\varphi^{-1}_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})
, there is a map of graded modules

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\mu{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}})[1/p]\longrightarrow{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]),

which by
Proposition

5.22
.
(iii)
is a graded isomorphism.
In particular, by taking the base change, we get an induced isomorphism of graded objects

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\mu{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/p]\bigr)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]\bigr)={\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})[1/p].

Thus
(i)
follows from the canonical structure in
Proposition

5.24
.

To show the injectivity in
(ii)
and
(iii)
, since the maps are all canonical, it suffices to do so after evaluating at objects in
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
that are sufficiently large.
So we may assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine and
S
is a
p
-torsionfree integrally closed perfectoid algebra over
R_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}}
such that
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta}
is a pro-étale object
\lim_{i}{\mathrm{Spa}}(R_{i}[1/p],R_{i})
for topologically finite type affinoid adic spaces
{\mathrm{Spa}}(R_{i}[1/p],R_{i})
in
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
By enlarging
S
if necessary, we also assume that
S
is a perfectoid algebra over
\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
for the perfection of some framed regular prism
A
.
We let
d_{S}
be a generator of the ideal
I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
.
Then we make a claim on the intersection formula fo the ideal
\mu{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
, extending that of
[
5
, Lem. 3.23]
.

Claim 5.28
.

Let
S
be the perfectoid
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}
-algebra be as above.
There is an equality of ideals

\mu{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}=\bigcap_{a>0}\frac{\mu}{\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-a}(\mu)}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}.

Proof of
5.28
.

We first notice that by the assumption of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta}
and by
[
20
, Lem. 2.13]
together with the tilting correspondence, we know
S^{\flat}=(S^{\flat}[1/t])^{\circ}
, where
S^{\flat}
is the tilt of
S
and
t
is a pseudo-uniformizer of
S^{\flat}
.
In addition, by the arguments of
[
20
, Lem. 2.11]
, we know
S^{\flat}/tS^{\flat}
has no almost zero elements with respect to
\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}
, where
\mathfrak{m}^{\flat}
is the completion of the radical ideal of
tS^{\flat}
.
Now to prove the equality, it suffices to check the inclusion for their the mod
p
reductions is an equality:

(\epsilon-1)S^{\flat}\subseteq\bigcap_{a>0}\frac{\epsilon-1}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{p^{a}}}-1}S^{\flat}.

The latter follows since the quotient
\bigl(\bigcap_{a>0}\frac{\epsilon-1}{\epsilon^{\frac{1}{p^{a}}}-1}S^{\flat}\bigr)/(\epsilon-1)S^{\flat}
is an almost zero submodule in
S^{\flat}/(\epsilon-1)S^{\flat}
, which by the aforementioned inputs is trivial.
∎

To show
(ii)
, it suffices to show that each map in the factorization below is injective

\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\longrightarrow\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}\longrightarrow\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}.

The injectivity of the first map follows from the separatedness of the Nygaard filtration, as shown in the proof of
Theorem

5.19
.
For the second map, since both the source and the target are filtered complete, it suffice to check the injectivity on individual graded pieces, which amounts to showing that the graded pieces of
\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})
are
p
-torsionfree.
We notice that the quotient ring
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})
is naturally isomorphic to the zero-th graded piece of
\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
.
So we get

{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\bigr)\simeq{\mathrm{gr}}^{0}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S})\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}).

By
Proposition

5.22
.
(ii)
, we know
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S})
is naturally isomorphic to
({\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S})\otimes_{S}S\{n\}
.
Thus we reduce to checking that the graded module
S\otimes_{{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B})\simeq{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}/B}}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi^{-1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\bigr)
is
p
-torsionfree, as verified in the next claim.

Claim 5.29
.

The graded piece of the filtered base change
\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{\mathrm{gr}}^{0}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
is
p
-torsionfree.

Proof of
5.29
.

Since the property of being
p
-torsionfree can be checked before/after a
p
-completely faithfully flat cover, by the base change formula of the Nyggard-filtered relative prismatic cohomology (
[
7
, Thm. 1.8.(5)]
), we may assume
(B,J)=(A,I)
is a framed regular prism (as in the proof of
Theorem

2.21
and Equation (
9
)).
In addition, since the Frobenius map
\varphi_{A}
is faithfully flat (
Proposition

2.8
), we may further replace
(B,J)
by the perfection
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
, where we let
T=\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
be the corresponding perfectoid ring.
Then by applying
Corollary

2.25
twice, we obtain natural isomorphisms of prisms in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
:

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\simeq({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\coprod(B,J)=({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\coprod({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T}})\simeq{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T_{S}/{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}.

To continue, we notice that since the Frobenius morphisms
\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
and
\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T}}
are both isomorphic, by the definition of the relative Nyggard filtration, we obtain the following filtered base change isomorphisms

(61)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T_{S}/{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{{T_{S}}/{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the filtration on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\simeq{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{T_{S}/{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
is defined by the preimage of the
I
-adic filtration along its absolute Frobenius morphism.
Moreover, notice that the Frobenius morphism
\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
sends the absolute Nyggard filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}=\varphi^{-1}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})^{\bullet}
onto the
I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}
-adic filtration on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
.
Hence by taking the filtered reduction and by (
61
), we obtain a filtered isomorphism

\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{\mathrm{gr}}^{0}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\simeq\Fil^{\bullet}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{T_{S}/{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}\otimes_{I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{\bullet}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}},

which by the relative prismatic–de Rham comparison is further isomorphic to the Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology
\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{{T_{S}}/S}\simeq\bigl(\Fil^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{T/\mathcal{O}_{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}S\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}
.
As a consequence, by the locally freeness of the shifted cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{T/\mathcal{O}_{X}}[-1]=\mathbb{L}_{\overline{A}_{{\mathrm{perf}}}/\mathcal{O}_{X}}[-1]
as in
Proposition

2.8
and the flatness of
S
over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
, we see the graded pieces of the Hodge filtration is
p
-torsionfree.
∎

To show
(iii)
, we notice that
by the assumption of the complete faithful flatness of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
(cf.
Proposition

2.27
), we know the sequence
(\mu,p)
is a regular sequence in
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
Hence
\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})
is contained in its
p
-inverted localization
\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})[1/p]
.
In addition, by
5.28
and the complete projectivity of the prism
(B,J)
(cf.
Item

(ii)
.
(iii)
), we know the base change of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
along the reduction
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/(p,\mu){{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
is a projective module over
S^{\flat}/(\epsilon-1)S^{\flat}
and in particular has no non-trivial almost zero elements.
Thus as in the proof of
5.28
we get

\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}=\bigcap_{a>0}\mu^{n}\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\cdots\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-a}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}){{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}.

Notice that the ideals
\varphi^{-j}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})
are disjoint after inverting
p
.
Thus we get

\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n})({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})[1/p]\subset\prod_{a>0}\prod_{1\leq b\leq a}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-b}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})[1/p]\bigr).

The final injection in
(iii)
is given by the injectivity of (finite compositions of) the absolute Frobenius morphisms
\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-b}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})[1/p]\to\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}/\varphi_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}^{-1}(I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})[1/p]
, which was proved in
Proposition

2.27
.
(ii)
.
∎

Now we are ready to calculate the global section of the rational prismatic period sheaf.

Theorem 5.30
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism.
Assume either
(B,J)
is a finite coproduct of framed regular prisms or
\varphi_{B}
is completely faithfully flat.
For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the structure map induces an isomorphism

B\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\mu^{-n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}).

Proof.

In the following, we use
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-m\}(m)
to denote the double twisted sheaf
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{\mathbb{A}_{\inf}}\mathbb{A}_{\inf}\{-m\}(m)\simeq\mu^{m}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
We first notice that by applying the complete base change along
\varphi_{B}
, the canonical map
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)
naturally induces a map
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)
, which by construction is equivariant under the relative Frobenii and filtrations.
In addition, as in the proof of
Theorem

5.27
, we choose a large enough perfectoid algebra
S
that is
p
-completely flat over
X
and is over

\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
for a framed regular prism
(A,I)
, such that the generic fiber covers
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
by
Lemma

5.6
).
Under the assumption, we notice that
(\mu,p)
is a regular sequence in
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
, thanks to the complete flatness of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
.
To see the latter, the case when
(B,J)
is a finite coproduct of framed regular prisms follows from
Proposition

2.27
.
In general, the flatness of
(B,J)
and
Theorem

2.21
.
(ii)
implies that
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
is completely flat over
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
, and thus the other case when
\varphi_{B}
is completely flat follows by taking the base change.
Thus the induced map
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)
is injective.
Consider the induced diagram for the filtered completion of the
p
-localized objects:

(62)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}.}

Here we note that the other three arrows are injective as well: for the left vertical arrow, it follows from the separatedness of the Nygaard filtration (cf. proof of
Theorem

5.19
); for the right vertical arrow, it is the consequence of
Theorem

5.27
.
(ii)
; and for the bottom horizontal arrow, it follows from
Proposition

5.24
.

We then notice that the bottom arrow in (
62
) induces an isomorphism on the global sections.
Indeed, by induction and the filtered completeness, it suffices to show that the graded pieces of the cokernel of the map
\iota\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{i\}(-i)[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}\to\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{i+1\}(-i-1)[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}
have vanishing global sections for
i>0
.
In addition, by
Theorem

5.27
.
(i)
, we know
{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\coker(\iota))
is of the form
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(-i)
where
\mathcal{F}^{\bullet}
is a base change of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\overline{B}},\eta}
-modules.
So the vanishing of
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{\mathrm{gr}}^{\bullet}(\coker(\iota)))
for
i>0
follows from the vanishing of the pro-étale cohomology of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(<0)
in
Proposition

4.15
, together with the projection formula in
Theorem

4.12
.

Now, by
Theorem

5.19
.
(ii)
, the twisted structure map induces an isomorphism

(B[1/p])^{\wedge}_{J}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}),

which is further isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil})
by (
62
).
As a consequence, by the injectivity of the right vertical arrow in (
62
), we see

(63)

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n))\subseteq(B[1/p])^{\wedge}_{J}\cap\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)),

where the right hand side by
Lemma

2.26
is equal to
B
.
On the other hand, we know the left hand side in (
63
) naturally contains
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})
, which under the twisted structure map is identified with
B
itself (
Theorem

5.19
.
(ii)
).
Hence the twisted structure map
B\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n))
is an isomorphism.
Finally, by the same argument for
Theorem

5.19
.
(iii)
, we see the structure map
B\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n))
is an isomorphism as well, which finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.31
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a flat prism such that either
(B,J)
is a finite coproduct of framed regular prisms or
\varphi_{B}
is completely faithfully flat.
The structure map below is an isomorphism

B\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]).

6.
Canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
X_{\eta}
be its generic fiber.
In this section, we show that there is a canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal over the flat prismatic site of
X
, for any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
.

Below we consider a subcategory of flat prisms that will serve as the parametrizing space for our construction.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme.
The
special prismatic site of
X
, denoted as
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, is the full subcategory of
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
consisting of transversal prisms
(B,J)
that are finite coproduct of framed regular prisms.
We call such
(B,J)

special
.

It is easy to see that a special prism is in particular a flat prism.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme.
A
weak prismatic (
F
-)crystal
is a presheaf
\mathcal{E}
of (resp. weak Frobenius) modules on
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
such that

•

For each
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}(B,J)
is
(J,p)
-regular.

•

For each noetherian prism
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}(B,J)
is finitely presented.

•

for each map of framed regular prisms
(B_{1},J_{1})\to(B_{2},J_{2})
such that
\overline{B}_{1}/p\overline{B}_{1}\to\overline{B}_{/}p\overline{B}_{2}
is flat, the induced linearized map of torsionfree modules

\bigl(B_{2}\otimes^{L}_{B_{1}}\mathcal{E}(B_{1},J_{1})\bigr)^{\wedge}_{(p,J_{2})}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}(B_{2},J_{2})

is an injection.

The category of weak prismatic (
F
-)crystals is denoted as
{\mathrm{wCrys}}^{(\varphi)}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}})
.

We also remind the reader the following terminology for prismatic
F
-crystals.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme.
A prismatic (
F
-)crystal in complexes
\mathcal{E}
on
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is called
reflexive
if for each framed regular prism
(A,I)
, the
A
-complex

\mathcal{E}(A,I)
is represented by a reflexive
A
-module in the sense of
Definition

3.3
.

We denote the category of reflexive prismatic (
F
-)crystals over
X
as
\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
.

Remark 6.4
.

When
X
is smooth, the category of reflexive prismatic (
F
-)crystals is equivalent to the category of analytic prismatic (
F
-)crystals, as shown in
[
28
, Thm. 5.10]
.

The following observation describes the relationship between prismatic
F
-crystals and weak prismatic
F
-crystals, and gives a formula of calculating the sections of a prismatic
F
-crystal through the subcategory
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme.

(i)

There is a canonical fully faithful functor
\Coh^{(\varphi)}_{{\mathrm{refl}}}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to{\mathrm{wCrys}}^{(\varphi)}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}})
.

(ii)

A weak prismatic (
F
-)crystal
\mathcal{E}
is a (reflexive) prismatic (
F
-)crystal if and only if there is an étale cover
\{U_{i}\to X\}
such that each restriction
\mathcal{E}|_{U_{i}}
is so.

(iii)

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a prismatic crystal in prefect complexes, let
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be a bounded prism, and let
(B,J)
be a covering object in
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.
Then the canonical map belows are isomorphisms

\mathcal{E}(A,I)\longrightarrow\underset{(C,K)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}}{R\lim}\mathcal{E}((A,I)\coprod(C,K))\longrightarrow R\lim_{[n]\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{E}((A,I)\coprod(B^{n},IB^{n})).

The same holds for Laurent
F
-crystals in vector bundles/perfect complexes.

Proof.

For Part
(i)
, as framed regular prisms (which are weak initial in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
) and their Čech nerves are contained in the subsite
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, we have a natural identification
\Coh_{\mathrm{refl}}^{(\varphi)}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\simeq\Crys_{\mathrm{refl}}^{(\varphi)}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}})
.
Moreover, the canonical functor
\Crys^{(\varphi)}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}})\to{\mathrm{wCrys}}^{(\varphi)}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}})
is fully faithful, which follows directly from the definition and
Remark

3.2
.
For Part
(ii)
, it suffices to check that for a given completely flat map of prisms
(B_{1},J_{1})\to(B_{2},J_{2})
in
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the induced map of
B_{2}
-modules
(\mathcal{E}(B_{1},J_{1})\otimes_{B_{1}}B_{2})^{\wedge}_{(p,J_{2})}\to\mathcal{E}(B_{2},J_{2})
is an isomorphism.
The latter can be checked
p
-completely étale locally, hence follows from the assumption.
Part
(iii)
follows from the complete faithfully flat descent of perfect complexes together with the flatness of the coproducts in
Theorem

2.21
.
(ii)
.
The analogues for Laurent
F
-crystals follow from Drinfeld–Mathew’s descent result
[
35
, Thm. 1.6, Thm. 1.7]
.
∎

In the rest of the section, we will construct the canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal associated to any
p
-adic local system
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
and prove
A
.
Specifically, we give the construction and prove its finiteness in
Section

6.1
, analyze its Nygaard filtration in
Section

6.2
.
We also study its integral de Rham realizations in
Section

6.4
, its crystalline realization in
Section

6.5
, and its compatibility with pullbacks and higher direct images in
Section

6.6
.
In the special case when the local system is pointwise crystalline, we prove in
Section

6.3
that the equivalent descriptions in
D
hold true, together with an equivalence of categories between pointwise crystalline local systems and reflexive prismatic
F
-crystals.

6.1.
Construction and the finiteness

We start with the formula of the canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal together with its finiteness.
