Throughout the section, we let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.

Construction 6.6
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

(i)

We define the
B
-modules as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])=\colim_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J),

where
\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{n\}(-n)
is the
\mu^{n}
-twisted prismatic period sheaf as in
5.25
.

(ii)

The Frobenius structure of the relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
induces a natural map
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu J]
,
where we use the equalities of ideals
\varphi_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}(\mu{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}})=\mu{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\cdot I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}
and
I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}=J{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
By tensoring it with
T
and then taking the cohomology, we obtain a
\varphi_{B}
-semi-linear morphism
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/J]
.
We let
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
be the Frobenius twist
\varphi_{\mathcal{O_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes^{L}_{B,\varphi_{B}}B)^{\wedge}_{(p,J)}
.
Then inverting
J
at the source, we obtain a canonical
B
-linear map

\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)}\colon\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/J]\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/J].

Putting the above together, we obtain a functor that sends a local system
T
onto a module with weak Frobenius structure
(\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J),\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)})
over
B
,
which is also functorial in the prism
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

To analyze
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
, the following reduction plays an important role.
Namely, the localization at
\mu
in the construction of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
can be replaced by a finite twist.
Note that the result uses the full strength of the structural results of the rational prismatic period sheaves.

Proposition 6.7

(Rational-to-integral reduction)
.

There is an integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that for each
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the inclusion map below is an equality

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J).

Proof.

We first notice that the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{B}
induces the following commutative diagram of injections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}.}

Since
(B,J)
is a finite coproduct of framed regular prisms, by the arguments of
Proposition

2.27
, the diagram is cartesian.
So after tensoring it with the local system
T
and then taking the global sections, it suffices to show that for small enough
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the global section
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\coker(\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n}))
vanishes, where
\widetilde{\iota}_{n+1,n}
is the map
\mu^{n+1}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\to\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
Moreover, by
Theorem

5.27
.(
(iii)
and
(ii)
), it suffices to show that for small enough
n
, the cohomology below vanishes

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}/\varphi^{-1}(I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}})[1/p]\bigr)^{\wedge}_{\Fil}),

which by the filtered completeness further reduces to the vanishing of its graded pieces.
Then the claim follows from the analysis of the graded pieces in
Theorem

5.27
.
(i)
, together with Liu–Zhu’s result in
Theorem

4.14
and the projection formula in
Theorem

4.12
.
(b)
.
∎

Definition 6.8
.

The largest integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}
that satisfies
Proposition

6.7
, if exists, is defined as the
bottom height
of
T
.

Remark 6.9
.

It is still possible that
T
does not have the bottom height.
As we shall see in
Proposition

6.20
, this is equivalent to
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}=0
.

We now consider the structure of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
as a
B
-module.
The first observation is the regularity of
(J,p)
.

Lemma 6.10

(
(J,p)
-regularity)
.

Assume
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.
The sequence of invertible ideals
(J,p)
is a regular sequence for the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
.

In particular, when
(B,J)
is a two-dimensional complete localization of a regular prsim (for example, the classical Breuil–Kisin prism, and more generally the localization of transversal regular prisms in
Section

2.2
), the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is locally free (cf.
Lemma

3.4
and
Corollary

3.7
).

Proof.

As the construction of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is Zariski local with respect to
X
, we may assume
X
is affine.
In addition, by
Lemma

5.6
, we let
S
be perfection of a framed regular prism over
X
, so that
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)\to X
is a faithfully flat cover and the generic fiber
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta}
is a pro-étale cover of
X_{\eta}
.
So by construction and
Proposition

6.7
,
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n))
is a submodule of
T({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n)
, where the latter by
Corollary

2.25
is
J
-torsionfree.
In addition, by the universal coefficient theorem, the mod
J
reduction
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)/J\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is contained in the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n)\otimes_{B}\overline{B})=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{-n\})
, where the latter is contained in
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{-n\}
.
By
Corollary

5.10
.
(ii)
, the graded pieces of the conjugate filtration of
\overline{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\overline{B}\{-n\}
is locally free over
S_{\overline{B}}
and in particular are
p
-torsionfree.
Hence the mod
J
reduction of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is
p
-torsionfree as well.
∎

For later applications, we notice that the linearization map, or more generally a complete base change along a completely injective and flat morphism is injective.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, let
M
be a
(J,p)
-complete and
(J,p)
-regular module over
B
, and let
C
be a
B
-algebra that is
(J,p)
-completely projective.

(1)

The
C
-complex
(M\otimes^{L}_{B}C)^{\wedge}_{(p,J)}
lives in cohomological degree zero and is equal to
\lim_{m}(M\otimes_{B}C)/(p,J)^{m}
.

(2)

The linearization map
M\to(M\otimes^{L}_{B}C)^{\wedge}_{(p,J)}
is injective.

Proof.

The claim on the cohomological degree follows from
Lemma

4.6
.
For the injectivity, we notice that the complete projectivity of the map
B\to C
implies that the reduction
\overline{B}/p\overline{B}\to\overline{C}/p\overline{C}
is faithfully flat and thus injective.
So the claim follows from
Lemma

4.9
.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.
The linearization
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\to\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
along
\varphi_{B}
is injective.

Proof.

The linearization map can be fitted into a composition

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]),

so it suffices to show that the composition is injective: this follows from the injectivity of the Frobenius twist map for the relative prismatic cohomology, as in
Proposition

2.27
.
(ii)
.
∎

Continue with the injectivity above, we also have the injectivity of the Frobenius structure.

Lemma 6.13

(Frobenius is injective)
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
be a framed regular prism.
The weak Frobenius structure
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/J]\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/J]
is injective.

Proof.

By construction, the map
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)}
can be factored as below

\varphi_{B}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])[1/J],

where the first map is the linearlization of the morphism
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])
, and the second map is induced from the Frobenius structure on the relative prismatic period sheaf.
When
\varphi_{B}
is finite flat, the first arrow is an isomorphism.
The injectivity of the second arrow for a special
(B,J)
follows from
Proposition

2.27
.
(ii)
.
∎

Now we prove the finiteness
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
for a framed regular prism
(B,J)
.

Theorem 6.14

(Finiteness)
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
be a framed regular (thus noetherian) prism.
The
B
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\bigr)
is finitely presented.

Proof.

Recall from
Proposition

2.8
that the map
\varphi_{B}:B\to B
is finite faithfully flat.
So by the fact that the perfectness can be checked flat locally, it suffices to prove that
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is a finitely presented
B
-module.
In addition, as explained in the proof of
Lemma

6.13
, the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is naturally contained in the
(J,p)
-completely regular module
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n))
.
Thus by the universal coefficient theorem and the noetherian assumption, it suffices to show that the
p
-complete
p
-torsionfree module
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n)\otimes_{B}\overline{B})=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}})
is finitely generated over
\overline{B}
, where
n\in\mathbb{Z}
is chosen as in
Proposition

6.7
.
Moreover, since the Hodge filtration on the de Rham cohomology is separated (
Proposition

5.16
.
(ii)
), we may replace the de Rham period sheaf by its filtered completion.

As the cohomology group is local with respect to the Zariski topology of
X
, we may assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine.
Now by the rational finiteness in
Proposition

4.15
.
(i)
and by the integral finiteness
Proposition

4.16
, we know each cohomology group
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})=\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(-)/X}[-i])
has bounded
p^{\infty}
torsions and has finitely presented torsionfree quotient over
R
.
So the projection formula
Theorem

4.12
.
(a)
and the flatness assumption of the prism
(B,J)
imply the isomorphism

(64)

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\simeq\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}).

In addition, by the vanishing result in
Proposition

4.15
.
(ii)
, only finitely many graded pieces contribute to the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}})
.
Hence the finitenss of
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}})
follows from the finiteness of the
R
-module
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})
mentioned above (which itself is
p
-torsionfree), together with the tensor product formula (
64
).
∎

We also observe that the despite the construction of the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
being global, it only depends on the image of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to X
under the Zariski topology.

Proposition 6.15

(The local nature of cohomology)
.

Let
(B,J)
be a bounded prism over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, and let
U\subseteq X
be an open sub formal scheme.
Assume the structural map
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to X
factors through
U
.
Then for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the natural map below is an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{0}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}).

Proof.

By taking the Čech cohomology with respect to an open affine covering, it suffices to assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
and
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{1})
are both affine and connected.
As in
4.5
, we let
\widetilde{X}={\mathrm{Spf}}(S)\to X
be the integral closed perfectoid scheme whose generic fiber is a fixed maximal connected pro-finite-étale cover of
X_{\eta}
.
Under the setup,
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is a Galois cover with Galois group
G\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}G_{X_{\eta}}
, and the restriction of the local system
T|_{\widetilde{X}_{\eta}}
is trivial.
We also let
\widetilde{X}_{U}
be the base change
\widetilde{X}\times_{X}U={\mathrm{Spf}}(S_{R_{1}})
, so that
\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta}\to U_{\eta}
is also a Galois cover of group
G
.
Then the localization map of the cohomology is identified with a map of continuous group cohomology as below

(65)

\bigl(T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(S\otimes_{R}{\overline{B}})^{\wedge}_{p}/B}\bigr)^{G}\longrightarrow\bigl(T(\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta})}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(S_{R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}/B}\bigr)^{G},

where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})
(resp.
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta})
) is the evaluation of the
p
-complete constant sheaf
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
at the adic spaces
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}
(resp.
\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta}
).
By assumption, the
p
-complete tensor product
(S_{R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}
is naturally equal to
(S\otimes_{R}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}
, which induces an identification of their relative prismatic cohomology over
(B,J)
.
Moreover, we have a natural factorization of equivariant maps of rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(S\otimes_{R}{\overline{B}})^{\wedge}_{p}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(S_{R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{B})^{\wedge}_{p}/B}.}

In addition, since the local system
T|_{\widetilde{X}_{\eta}}
is trivial on
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}
, the pullback along the left vertical map above induces an equality of representations of
G
:

T(\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta})=T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})}\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{U,\eta}).

As a consequence, by taking the base change of the representation
T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})
(which is finitely generated over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})
) along the factorization diagram, the target Galois cohomology of (
65
) is naturally identified with the source, which finishes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Nygaard filtration on the Frobenius twist

We then consider the filtration on the Frobenius twist
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
, evaluated at framed regular prisms.
As before, we assume
T
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
X_{\eta}
, where
X
is a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
For simplicity, we also assume
X
is connected.

Definition 6.16
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
be either a framed regular prism or a perfect prism.
For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define
the twisted (Nygaard) filtration
on
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
by

\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\bigl(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Fil^{i}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\bigr),

where the filtration of the twisted prismatic period sheaf is defined in
5.25
.

Here we note that we implicitly identifies the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
with the global section
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])
, which is clear if
(B,J)
is perfect and follows from the proof of
Lemma

6.13
if
(B,J)
is framed regular.
By construction, the filtration is functorial with respect to the local system and the prism
(B,J)
.

To analyze the filtration, we first notice that the localization by
\mu
in the definition above can often be replaced by a finite
\mu
-twist, similar to
Proposition

6.7
.

Lemma 6.17
.

Let
(B,J)
be a framed regular prism, and let
n
be an integer that satisfies
Proposition

6.7
.
For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the natural inclusion map below is an equality

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Fil^{i}(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}))\xrightarrow{=}\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J).

In particular,
\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)=\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
for
i\leq n
.

Proof.

We note from
Lemma

5.26
that the following diagram of injections is cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{i}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{i}\bigl({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[\frac{1}{\mu}]\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].}

So the claim follows by taking the global section at the above diagram (after tensoring it with
T
), together with
Proposition

6.7
.
The second claim on the eventual constancy of the filtration follows from the construction that
\Fil^{i}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr)=\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
for
i\leq n
.
∎

Recall from
Lemma

6.13
that the weak Frobenius structure on
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is injective when
(B,J)
is framed regular.
Under the assumption, the twisted filtration is in fact determined by the weak Frobenius structure; or in other words it is saturated.

Lemma 6.18

(Filtration and Frobenius)
.

Let
(B,J)
be a framed regular prism. For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)=\{x\in\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)~|~\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)}(x)\in J^{i}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\}.

In particular, the filtration on
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is determined by the weak Frobenius structure.

Proof.

We let
n
be any integer as in
Lemma

6.17
and assume
i\geq n
.
Notice that for large perfectoid objects
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
, the filtration on the
\mu
-twisted relative prismatic cohomology
\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
is defined through the Frobenius preimage (
[
7
]
).
In particular, by ranging over those perfectoid objects, we see that for
i\geq n
, the following diagram of injections among period sheaves is in fact cartesian (cf.
Proposition

2.27
.
(ii)
for the injectivity)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{i}\bigl(\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J^{i}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J^{n}\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}.}

So the claim follows by taking the global section and by
Proposition

6.7
and
Lemma

6.17
.
For general
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, it suffices to notice that the integer
n
can be as small as needed.
∎

Another observation is that the canonical filtration is separated.

Lemma 6.19

(Separatedness)
.

Let
(B,J)
be either a flat perfect prism or a framed regular prism.
The twisted Nygaard filtration on
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is separated.

Proof.

As the statement is local with respect to the étale topology of
X
, we may assume
X
is affine and admits a framed regular prism
(A,I)
with
\Sigma\subset A^{\times}
.
We let
S
be the perfectoid ring given by the reduction of the perfection
(A_{\mathrm{perf}},IA_{\mathrm{perf}})
.
Under the assumption, by
Lemma

5.6
, the filtered
B
-module
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}(n))
is contained in the Nygaard filtered relative prismatic cohomology
T(n)({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\}
, and it suffices to check that the latter is separated, which was proved in
Corollary

5.18
.
∎

Assembling the previous ingredients, we obtain the following observations on the graded pieces of the filtration on
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
(B,J)
be a framed regular prism, and let
n
be an integer that satisfies
Proposition

6.7
.

(i)

For
i<n
, we have
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)=0
.

(ii)

For
i\geq n
, there is a natural injection of
\overline{B}
-modules

{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\bigl(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\bigr)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\bigl(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{N}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\{-n\})\bigr),

(iii)

For each
i\geq n
, there exists a natural injection of
\overline{B}
-modules

\coker\bigl(\Fil^{i-1}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}J\hookrightarrow\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\bigr)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i-n}\mathbb{L}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[n-i]).

(iv)

If
T
does not admit the bottom height, then both
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
and thus
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
vanish.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows from
Lemma

6.18
.
For
(ii)
, it follows from
Lemma

6.17
together with the left exact sequence of the global sections of the exact sequence of sheaves below

0\longrightarrow T(n)\otimes\Fil^{i+1}_{N}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\})\longrightarrow T(n)\otimes\Fil^{i}_{N}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\})\longrightarrow T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}_{N}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\})\longrightarrow 0.

Similarly, Part
(iii)
follows from
Lemma

6.17
and the following short exact sequence of sheaves

0\longrightarrow T(n)\otimes\Fil^{i-1}_{N}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\})\otimes_{B}J\longrightarrow T(n)\otimes\Fil^{i}_{N}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\{-n\})\longrightarrow T(n)\otimes{\mathrm{gr}}^{i-n}_{H}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\longrightarrow 0,

where the latter is a direct consequence of
[
4
, Cor. 5.2.8]
.
Finally, if
T
does not admit the bottom height, then it means by definition that any integer
n
satisfies
Proposition

6.7
.
Hence the vanishing of
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
in Part
(iv)
follows from Part
(i)
together with the separatedness of the twisted Nygaard filtration (
Lemma

6.19
).
The vanishing of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
, on the other hand, is a consequence of its injection into
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
(
Lemma

6.12
).
∎

In particular, the filtration on the Frobenius twist
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is eventually
J
-adic.

Corollary 6.21
.

Let
n
be an integer that satisfies
Proposition

6.7
.
There is an integer
n^{\prime}\geq n
, such that for each framed regular prism
(B,J)
, the filtered submodule
\Fil^{\geq n^{\prime}}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is the
J
-adic filtration
J^{\geq n^{\prime}}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the inclusion map
\Fil^{i-1}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}J\hookrightarrow\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is an isomorphism for
i\gg 0
, which follows from
Proposition

6.20
.
(iii)
, Liu–Zhu’s finiteness and vanishing result in
Proposition

4.15
, together with the projection formula in
Theorem

4.12
.
∎

Recall as in
Section

4.2
that the pro-étale sheaf
\wedge^{i-n}\mathbb{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}/\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}}(n)[n-i]
is contained in the period sheaf
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{C}(i)={\mathrm{gr}}^{i}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}
.
So the
\overline{B}[1/p]
-module
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\wedge^{i-n}\mathbb{L}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[n-i])[1/p]
is a submodule in
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{HT}}(T)|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})_{\eta}}
.
Moreover, recall from the work of Shimizu
[
42
]
that there is a finite set of elements
{\mathrm{wt}}(T)\subset\overline{K}
, called
generalized Hodge–Tate weights
, associated to the local system
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
By the locally freeness of the generalized eigenspaces of Sen operator in
[
42
, Proof of Prop. 5.3]
, we know
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{HT}}(T)|_{{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})_{\eta}}=0
if and only if
(-i)\notin{\mathrm{wt}}(T)
.
In particular, we get the following vanishing result.

Corollary 6.22
.

Assume
{\mathrm{wt}}(T)\cap\mathbb{Z}=\emptyset
.
The
B
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
vanishes for any framed regular prism
(B,J)
over
X
.

6.3.
Crystalline local systems

In this section, we consider the special case when
T
is a
(pointwise) crystalline
local system.
We prove that under the assumption, the associated canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal is in fact a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal whose étale realization is
T
and in addition satisfies the purity result.
In particular, the notion of crystallinity with respect to a regular integral model is as good as that for smooth integral models.

We start by recalling the Strong Étale Comparison Theorem of
[
28
, Thm. 1.21]
, which gives an enhanced association between prismatic
F
-crystals and étale local systems.

Theorem 6.23

(Guo–Reinecke)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
\mathcal{E}
be a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, and let
T
be the associated étale local system over
X_{\eta}
.
There is a canonical Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves over
X_{\eta}

T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})[1/\mu],

satisfying the following properties:

•

The tautological map induces a natural short exact sequence of pro-étale sheaves

0\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})[1/\mu]\xrightarrow{\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})[1/\varphi(\mu)]\longrightarrow 0.

•

The base change of the isomorphism along
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]\to{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\langle 1/I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}\rangle=W(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\flat})
recovers the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert equivalence of Laurent
F
-crystals (
[
8
, Cor. 3.7]
).

•

It is compatible with proper smooth direct image:
g:X\to Z
be a proper smooth morphism of regular
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
There is a canonical Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of pro-étale complexes over
Z_{\eta}

(Rg_{\eta,*}T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(Rg_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})[1/\mu].

Here we recall from
[
28
, § 9.1]
that
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})[1/\mu]
is the canonical
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]
-linear pro-étale sheaf associated to the prismatic crystal
\mathcal{E}
, defined by sending each affinoid perfectoid object
{\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in(X_{\eta,K_{\infty}})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
to the module
\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),I_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)})[1/\mu]
.

Proof.

For the first two properties,
it suffices to show that for a covering family of affinoid perfectoid spaces
{\mathrm{Spa}}(S[1/p],S)\in(X_{\eta,K_{\infty}})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, there is a natural isomorphism
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)[1/\mu]\simeq\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),I_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)})[1/\mu]
that satisfies the short exact sequence and recovers the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert equivalence of Bhatt–Scholze.
In our case, we check those affinoid perfectoid spaces that admit a map from
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}})_{\eta}
, where
(A,I)
is some framed regular prism (the topos of such affinoid perfectoid spaces coincide with that of
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
by
Lemma

5.6
.)
Under the assumption, each
{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
-module
\mathcal{E}({\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S),I_{{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)})
, which is isomorphic to the tensor product
\mathcal{E}(A,I)\otimes_{A}{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)
, is finitely presented, perfect, and becomes finite projective after inverting
p
.
The rest of arguments is then identical to that of
[
28
, Thm. 9.1]
: by applying the arc descent at perfectoid rings, it suffices to produce a canonical isomorphism after replacing
S
with a product of perfectoid valuation rings
\prod V_{j}
over
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{K}}
.
Then the claim follows from
[
28
, Prop. 9.12]
.

For the last property, it suffices to notice that the argument of
[
28
, Thm. 9.1]
applies to the current setting as well: in
loc. cit.
, the only inputs that made use of the smoothness assumption of
Z
are the claims that
R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E}
is a prismatic
F
-crystal in perfect complexes on
Z_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, and
R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E}[1/p]
is a vector bundle over
(Z_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}[1/p])
.
To see the former, the perfectness was proved in
[
28
, Cor. 5.16]
, and the Frobenius isogeny follows from either
[
28
, Rmk. 8.11]
, or the identical arguments of
[
28
, Thm. 8.1]
with the Breuil–Kisin prism replaced by the framed regular prism.
To see the latter when
Z
is a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, since
Z_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is covered by framed regular prisms
(A,I)
, it suffices to show that for
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})(A,I)
, the localization
M[1/p]
is finite projective over
A[1/p]
.
By
Theorem

3.9
, it suffices to check that for any given surjection
(A,I)\to(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})
onto a two dimensional transversal regular prism, the base change
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}[1/p]
is finite projective over
A^{\prime}[1/p]
.
We let
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}=\overline{A^{\prime}}
be the reduction, which under the assumption is a finite extension of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
z:{\mathrm{Spf}}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})\to Z
be the structural morphism.
Then the base change
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}
is naturally isomorphic to the evaluation of
\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}z_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E}=R^{i}(g_{z})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}(\mathcal{E}|_{X_{z}})
at the prism
(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})\in(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, where
g_{z}:X_{z}\to\{z\}
is the fiber of
g
at
z\in Z
and is proper smooth.
By
[
28
, Thm. 1.18]
,
\mathcal{F}
is a coherent prismatic
F
-crystal over
(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
In addition, by
6.26
, we know
\mathcal{F}[1/p]
is locally free over
((\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[1/p])
.
Hence the
A^{\prime}[1/p]
-module
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}[1/p]=\mathcal{F}(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})[1/p]
is finite projective, which finishes the proof.
∎

The next statement analyzes the weak prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
assuming that the local system
T
arises from a prismatic
F
-crystal.

Proposition 6.24
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, and let
\mathcal{E}
be a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal whose étale realization
\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{E})
admits an isomorphism
\alpha:\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\sim}T
(
[
8
, §3]
).

(i)

There is an isomorphism of (weak) prismatic
F
-crystals
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}
induced from
\alpha
.

(ii)

The tautological linearization map
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\to T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
is an isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves.

Proof.

By
Theorem

6.23
, the map
\alpha
induces an isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves
\alpha^{\prime}:T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})[1/\mu]
.
In particular, for each prism
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, by taking the base change along the pro-étale sheaves
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}
, we obtain an induced Frobenius equivariant isomorphism

\beta_{1}\colon T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

Moreover, for each perfectoid ring
S
over
X
, by applying the crystal structure of
\mathcal{E}
at the following diagram of prisms

({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S),I_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)})\longrightarrow({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},J{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B})\longleftarrow(B,J),

we obtain a natural isomorphism to the constant pro-étale sheaf

\beta_{2}\colon{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(\mathcal{E})\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\simeq\mathcal{E}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

So by combining
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
, we get an isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves

\gamma\colon T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\simeq\mathcal{E}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

Now for each prism
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, by
6.6
, the isomorphism
\gamma
induces the formula

(66)

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\xrightarrow[\sim]{\mathrm{H}^{0}(\gamma)}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{E}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]).

We then claim that the right hand side above is equal to the
B
-module
\mathcal{E}(B,J)
through the tautological map
\mathcal{E}(B,J)\to\mathcal{E}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
: since
\mathcal{E}
is reflextive, we know
\mathcal{E}
is locally free away from the locus
V(p,J)
and
\mathcal{E}(B,J)=\mathcal{E}(B,J)[1/p]\cap\mathcal{E}(B,J)[1/J]
.
In particular, by using the finite projectivity of
\mathcal{E}(B,J)[1/p]
and
\mathcal{E}(B,J)[1/J]
, the map
\gamma
induces isomorphisms

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/p]\simeq\mathcal{E}(B,J)[1/p],\quad\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)[1/J]\simeq\mathcal{E}(B,J)[1/J].

Hence the claim follows from
Lemma

6.10
and
Corollary

3.5
.
As a consequence, the composition of the maps in (
66
) and (the inverse of) the tautological map is a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}(B,J)
.
In addition, the canonical linearization map
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\to T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
is in fact an isomorphism: this follows from the commutative diagram induced from
\gamma
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(\gamma)\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

∎

By combining the Primitive Purity in
Theorem

3.29
with the properties of prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor, we now prove that the crystallinity of the local system
T
implies that
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal and is associated with
T
.

Theorem 6.25
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Assume either of the following three equivalent conditions:

(a)

There is a dense open smooth subscheme
U\subseteq X
such that
T|_{U_{\eta}}
is a crystalline local system.

(b)

The restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
is a crystalline representation for each Shilov point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
of
X_{\eta}
.

(c)

There is a dense open smooth subscheme
U\subseteq X
such that the restriction
T|_{x}
is a crystalline representation for each classical point
x\in U_{\eta}
.

Then we have the following:

(i)

The canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal.

(ii)

For each
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the tautological map
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\to T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
induces an isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\xrightarrow{\sim}T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

(iii)

The étale realization of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is canonically isomorphic to
T
.

In particular, the local system
T
is
crystalline
with respect to
X
and is associated with the crystalline realization of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
.

Here the crystallinity of the local system is defined in the style of Faltings, as we introduced in
[
29
, Def. 4.4]
.
For the convenience of discussions, we use
\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
to denote the full subcategory of local systems that satisfies the conditions in
Theorem

6.25
.

Proof.

We first remind the reader of equivalences among the three assumptions: by taking the pullback, condition
(a)
implies both
(b)
and
(c)
(
[
29
, Thm. 5.3]
).
The implication from
(b)
to
(a)
is the purity result of Moon
[
36
, Thm. 1.2]
.
The implication from
(c)
to
(a)
is the pointwise criterion of Guo–Yang in
[
29
, Thm. 1.1]
.

Now we prove that the canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal is a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal in
(i)
.
As the statement is étale local on
X
(
Lemma

6.5
.
(ii)
), by
Proposition

6.24
, it suffices to show that for each closed point
x\in X
, there is a
p
-complete étale neighborhood
U
together with a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{U}
over
U_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
whose étale realization is
T|_{U_{\eta}}
.
To see the latter, by
Footnote

3
, we may assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine and there is a framed regular prism
(A,I)
such that
\overline{A}=R
.
Under the assumption, each Shilov point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
of
X_{\eta}
corresponds to the complete localization
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, where
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the
p
-complete discrete valuation ring
(R_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\wedge}_{p}
for an associated ideal
\mathfrak{p}\in{\mathrm{Ass}}(R/pR)
.
Moreover, since
(A,I)
covers the prismatic site (
Corollary

2.9
), it suffices to show that there is a reflexive Frobenius module over
A
together with a descent data over the Čech nerve
(A^{n},IA^{n})
.
Now by the main results of
[
28
, Thm. A]
or
[
15
, Thm. 1.3]
, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in{\mathrm{Ass}}(R/pR)
, the crystalline assumption of
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L_{\mathfrak{p}})}
implies that there is locally free prismatic
F
-crystal over
\mathcal{O}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
associated to
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
More concretely, this prismatic
F
-crystal is given by a reflexive Frobenius module
M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
together with a descent isomorphism as below

\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}:M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}A^{1}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}A^{1}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}},

where
A^{\bullet}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the Čech nerve and the isomorphism satisfies the cocycle condition over
A^{\geq 2}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
On the other hand, the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert equivalence of
[
8
, Cor. 3.7]
implies that the local system
T
corresponds to a locally free Frobenius module
M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}
over
A\langle 1/I\rangle
together with a descent isomorphism

\gamma_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}:M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}A^{1}\langle 1/I\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}A^{1}\langle 1/I\rangle\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}.

Notice that since the local system
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is produced by restricting
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
onto
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L_{\eta})
,
the isomorphisms
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\gamma_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
are identical after their common base changes to
A^{1}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\langle 1/I\rangle
.

Next, by the Primitive Purity
Theorem

3.29
, the intersection
M\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}})\cap M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}
is a saturated Frobenius module over
A
(and in particular is finitely presented).
In addition, the tensor product
M\otimes_{A,p_{j}}A^{n}
for each structure map
p_{j}:A\to A^{n}
is naturally isomorphic to the intersection

\bigl(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}A^{n}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bigr)\bigcap\bigl(M_{A{\langle 1/I\rangle}}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}A^{n}\langle 1/I\rangle\bigr).

To see the latter, since each morphism
p_{j}:A\to A^{n}
is completely free (
Theorem

2.21
.
(iii)
), by choosing a basis
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
A^{n}
over
A
, we may write the elements in
M\otimes_{A}A^{n}
(which is complete thanks to the finiteness of
M
) uniquely as a countable sum
\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}e_{i}
with
m_{i}\in M
converging to zero.
Similarly, elements in
M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}A^{n}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}
are of the form
\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}e_{i}
with
m_{i}\in M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
, and elements in
M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}A^{n}\langle 1/I\rangle
are of the form
\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}e_{i}
with
m_{i}\in M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}
.
In particular, by looking at the coefficients in fron of each
e_{i}
and by
Theorem

3.29
, an element
m=\sum_{i\in I}m_{i}e_{i}
belongs to the intersection if and only if
m_{i}\in M
.
As a consequence, by restricing the isomorphisms
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\gamma_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
onto the intersection, we obtain the following isomorphism through the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\otimes_{A}A^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{1}\otimes_{A}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}A^{1}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\bigr)\bigcap\bigl(M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}A^{1}\langle 1/I\rangle\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}A^{1}_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}\otimes_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}M_{A_{L_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\bigr)\bigcap\bigl(A^{1}\langle 1/I\rangle\otimes_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}M_{A\langle 1/I\rangle}\bigr).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The cocycle condition of this isomorphism can be checked through intersection.
Hence we obtain a saturated prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}
over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, whose étale realization coincides with the local system
T
through the base change
A^{n}\to A^{n}\langle 1/I\rangle
and
Theorem

3.29
.

To continue, we want to make use of
Proposition

6.24
, and it remains to check that the prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}
is in fact reflexive.
The latter amounts to showing that the Frobenius module
M=\mathcal{E}(A,I)
is analytically locally free.
By
Theorem

3.9
, it suffices to check that for any given surjection
(A,I)\to(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})
onto a two dimensional transversal regular prism, the base change
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}[1/p]
is finite projective over
A^{\prime}[1/p]
.
We let
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}=\overline{A^{\prime}}
be the reduction, which under the assumption is a finite extension of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
f:R\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
be the structural morphism.
Then the base change
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}
is naturally isomorphic to the evaluation of the coherent prismatic
F
-crystal
f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{E}
at the prism
(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})\in(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
Now we make the following claim.

Claim 6.26
.

For a coherent prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{F}
over the
p
-adic discrete valuation ring
(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, the
p
-inverted localization
\mathcal{F}[1/p]
is locally free.

Proof of
6.26
.

We let
(\mathfrak{S},I_{\mathfrak{S}})
be a Breuil–Kisin prism of
\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}}
, which covers the prismatic site
(\mathcal{O}_{K^{\prime}})_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
It then suffices to check that the coherent Frobenius module
\mathcal{F}(\mathfrak{S},I_{\mathfrak{S}})
over
\mathfrak{S}
, or in other words, the Breuil–Kisin module, is locally free after inverting by
p
.
The latter was verified for example in
[
5
, Prop. 4.3]
.
∎

By applying
6.26
at
\mathcal{F}=f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{E}
, we know the localization
f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{E}[1/p]
, and in particular the
A^{\prime}[1/p]
-module
(f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{E})(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})[1/p]
is finite projective.
Hence the
A^{\prime}
-module
M\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}[1/p]\simeq(f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{E})(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})[1/p]
is finite projective over
A^{\prime}[1/p]
, which finishes the proof that
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal, namely Part
(i)
.

Now, to check that the tautological map in
(ii)
is an isomorphism, it suffices to do so étale locally on
X
.
We then notice that the above arguments have shown that étale locally on
X
, the étale realization of the prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is isomorphic to
T
.
So
(ii)
follows from
Proposition

6.24
.

Finally, we prove that the étale realization of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is canonically isomorphic to
T
.
We let
\mathcal{F}\in\Vect^{\varphi}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\langle 1/I\rangle)
be the Laurent
F
-crystal associated to the local system
T
.
The claim then amounts to constructing a canonical equivariant isomorphism from
\mathcal{F}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})
to
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\langle 1/I\rangle
for large enough perfectoid algebras
S
over
X
.
We note that for each
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the maps of prisms
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\to({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}})
and
(B,J)\to({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}})
induce the maps of modules

(67)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\langle 1/I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}\rangle}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\langle 1/I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}\rangle

(68)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\langle 1/J\rangle)({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})

(69)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\langle 1/J\rangle\otimes_{B\langle 1/J\rangle}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\langle 1/J\rangle\longleftarrow\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\langle 1/J\rangle,

which are functorial in
T
,
S
and
(B,J)
.
We also recall that the relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
is naturally isomorphic to the coproduct
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\coprod(B,J)
by
Corollary

2.25
.
So by taking the limit with respect to
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
and by the formula in
Lemma

6.5
.
(iii)
for Laurent
F
-crystals,
the above maps can be enhanced into canonical isomorphisms

(70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}}{R\lim}\bigl(\mathcal{F}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\langle 1/I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}\rangle}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\langle 1/I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}}\rangle\bigr),

(71)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}})

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}}{R\lim}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\langle 1/J\rangle\otimes_{B\langle 1/J\rangle}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\langle 1/J\rangle\bigr).

Hence by combining the maps in (
67
) and (
69
), we obtain a canonical isomorphisms of Laurent
F
-crystals

\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}\langle 1/I_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\rangle,

which from construction is functorial in
T
.
∎

Corollary 6.27

(Crystalline local systems are prismatic)
.

Let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
The prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor induces an equivalence of categories

\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})\longrightarrow\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{refl}}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}),

whose inverse is the étale realization.

Proof.

Theorem

6.25
.
(i)
shows that the canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal of an object
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
is a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal, hence inducing the functor as in the statement.
In addition, by
Theorem

6.25
.
(iii)
we know the composition of the above functor with the étale realization is an equivalence.
To finish the proof, it then suffices to show that the étale realization functor of
\Coh^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{refl}}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
is faithful, which follows from the reflexivity and the fact that for a regular transversal prism
(A,I)
that covers
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
(étale locally), the complete localization map
A\to A\langle 1/I\rangle
is an injection (cf. (
5
)).
∎

Corollary 6.28

(Various notions of the crystallinity)
.

Let
X_{\eta}
be a smooth rigid space over
K
and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Assume
X
is a regular
p
-adic formal model of
X_{\eta}
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
The following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

The canonical weak prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is a prismatic
F
-crystal and is associated to
T
.

(b)

There exists (hence unique by
Corollary

6.27
) a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal over
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
whose étale realization is
T
.

(c)

The local system
T
is crystalline with respect to the integral model
X
(in the sense of
[
29
, Def. 4.4]
).

(d)

There is a dense open smooth subscheme
U\subseteq X
such that
T|_{U_{\eta}}
is a crystalline local system.

(e)

The restriction
T|_{{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)}
is a crystalline representation for each Shilov point
{\mathrm{Spa}}(L)
of
X_{\eta}
.

(f)

There is a dense open smooth subscheme
U\subseteq X
such that the restriction
T|_{x}
is a crystalline representation for each classical point
x\in U_{\eta}
.

In particular, if above conditions hold true with respect to
X
, it then holds true for every regular model of the rigid space
X_{\eta}
.

Remark 6.29

(Enhancement into
F
-gauges)
.

When
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, it worth mentioning that the prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
can be canonically enhanced into a coherent prismatic
F
-gauge over
X
, or in other words a coherent sheaf over the syntomification
X^{\mathrm{syn}}
, as introduced by Drinfeld
[
14
]
and Bhatt–Lurie (
[
10
]
).
Indeed, when
X
is smooth, it was proved in our joint work with Li
[
27
, Thm. 3.32]
that there is a natural fully faithful functor

\Coh_{{\mathrm{refl}}}^{\varphi}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\longrightarrow\Coh(X^{\mathrm{syn}}),

defined through the saturated filtration for the Frobenius structure.
Specifically, the twisted Nygaard filtrations on
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
for Breuil–Kisin prisms
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, as introduced in
Definition

6.16
, generate (in the filtered sense) the Nygaard filtration on the associated
F
-gauge.
The latter was explained in
[
27
, Conv. 3.34, Lem. 3.37, and Prop. 3.38]
.
For general regular
p
-adic formal schemes
X
, the statement follows by applying the same arguments at the framed regular prisms
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.

6.4.
Integral de Rham realizations

In this subsection, we construct an integral enhancement of Liu–Zhu’s Riemann–Hilbert functor and relates it with the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor, for a regular
p
-adic formal scheme
X
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

As before, we let
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}
be the ring of integers of the cyclotomic extension of
K
, and let
S
be any perfectoid algebra over
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}}
.
Recall that the absolute prismatic–de Rham realization map induces a canonical composition of injections

{\mathrm{A_{inf}}}(S)={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/X},

which is compatible with the filtrations.
So by twisting a power of the element
\mu\in\Fil^{1}_{N}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}
, for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we obtain a filtered module
\Fil^{\bullet}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/X}
together with natural injections
\Fil^{\bullet}\mu^{n+1}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/X}\to\Fil^{\bullet}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/X}
.
As in
Section

5.4
, the construction naturally extends to a sequence of
p
-complete sheaves
\mu^{\bullet}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
over
X_{\eta}
, which we call

the twisted integral de Rham period sheaves
.

Construction 6.30
.

Let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, and let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(1)
We define a presheaf of
p
-complete
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules by sending an affine subscheme
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subseteq X
onto

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(U)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}).

(2)
For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define a canonical filtration of submodules on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
by

\Fil^{i}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(U)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Fil^{i}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}).

(3)
For each pair of integers
n\geq m
, the injection of twisted de Rham period sheaves induces an injection of presheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},m,T},

and we let
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
be the union
\colim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
.

(4)
Consider the derived connection on the de Rham period sheaf introduced in
[
26
, Lem. 3.15]

{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}/W},

which satisfies a derived Poincaré lemma as in
loc. cit.
.

Assume
X
is smooth over
W
.
We define an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-linear continuous connection
\nabla_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
through the formula

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathbb{L}_{X/W})=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})\otimes_{R}\Omega^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}_{R/W},

where
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subseteq X
is an affine open subscheme and
\Omega^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}_{R/W}=\mathbb{L}_{R/W}
is the continuous differential (which is finite projective under the smoothness assumption).
By the Griffiths transversality of the connection on the integral period sheaf, the connection
\nabla_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
satisfies the Griffiths transversality as well.

For a general regular
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, we define a
K
-linear continuous connection
\nabla_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}:\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}(U)[1/p]\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}[1/p]\otimes_{R[1/p]}\Omega^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}_{R[1/p]/K}
to be the map of global sections

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}[1/\mu])[1/p]\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}[1/\mu]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathbb{L}_{X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}})[1/p],

where the latter is naturally isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}[1/\mu])\otimes_{R[1/p]}\Omega^{1,{\mathrm{an}}}_{R[1/p]/K}
: thanks to the smoothness of the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
over
K
, the analytic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}_{X_{\eta}/K}=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}/W}[1/p]
is a vector bundle over the rigid space
X_{\eta}
(cf.
[
30
, Prop. 5.2.12]
).

Proposition 6.31
.

Let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, and let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(i)

Each
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
is a presheaf of finitely presented
p
-torsionfree modules over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

(ii)

For each affine open subscheme
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subseteq X
, there is a canonical filtered injection of
R
-modules with continuous flat connections

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(U)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(U_{\eta}),

where
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)
is the
p
-adic Riemann–Hilbert functor of Liu–Zhu
[
34
]
.

(iii)

For
n\gg 0
, the
p
-inverted localization of the inclusion
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n+1,T}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
is an equality with both terms equal to
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Proposition

5.16
, the filtered completion map
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\hookrightarrow T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)/X}
is an injection.
Moreover, since the graded piece of
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
is pro-étale locally free (
Corollary

5.10
) and is in particular
p
-torsionfree, we have a further injection
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{(-)/X}\hookrightarrow T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}({\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}[1/p])^{\wedge}_{\Fil}
.
Notice that by
[
26
, Thm. 1.1]
, the period sheaf
({\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}[1/p])^{\wedge}_{\Fil}
is naturally isomorphic to the de Rham period sheaf
{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
over
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
.
Hence the composition of the above maps of pro-étale sheaves induce an injection

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(U)=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(U_{\eta},T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}})=\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(U_{\eta}),

which finishes the proof of
(ii)
.

To continue, we note that the pro-étale sheaf
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
is
p
-torsionfree and
p
-complete, its global section is also a
p
-torsionfree
p
-complete module.
On the other hand, by
[
34
, Thm. 3.9]
, we know
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{dR}}(T)(U_{\eta})
is a finitely generated
R[1/p]
-module, which implies the same for the submodule
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(U)[1/p]
.
Hence the finiteness in
(i)
follows from
Proposition

4.16
.

Finally, the equalities of
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n+1,T}[1/p]=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}[1/p]=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}[1/p]
follows from the noetherian property of the ring
R[1/p]
and
(ii)
.
To finish the proof of
(iii)
, it suffices to check that
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
is automatically
p
-inverted (and thus
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}[1/p]=\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
): by the functoriality of the derived de Rham complex, the period sheaf
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
locally on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
admits a map from
{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}}/W}
, where the latter by Beilinson and Bhatt (cf.
[
26
, Thm. 1.4]
) is naturally isomorphic to the ring
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
.
So the claim follows from a known fact that the element
p
is inverted in
{\mathrm{A_{crys}}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})[1/\mu]=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{\infty}})
. ∎

To relate the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor with the integral de Rham functor, we have the following natural observation.

Proposition 6.32

(de Rham realization)
.

Let
(B,J)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, let
n
be an integer, and let
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)\subseteq X
be the image of the morphism
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to X
.

(i)

Assume
(B,J)
is a framed regular prism or its perfection.
There is a canonical injection of filtered
\overline{B}
-modules that are compatible with the continuous connections (either when
X
is smooth or after inverting
p
)

(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J))\otimes_{B}\overline{B}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}\otimes_{R}\overline{B},

(ii)

Assume
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
and
(B,J)
is special.
Then
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
is a sheaf of finitely presented modules over
\mathcal{O}_{X}[1/p]
and the above map is an isomorphism after inverting
p
.
Moreover, the canonical injection
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}\to\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(T)
in
Proposition

6.31
.
(ii)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We first notice that it suffices to replace
X
by the open subscheme
U
, thanks to the localization property in
Proposition

6.15
(where the same arguments apply verbatim to the period sheaves
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}
and
{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}
).
By the proof of
Lemma

6.13
, we know the Frobenius twist
\varphi_{B}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\cdot\mu^{n})
is naturally equal to
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{(1)}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\cdot\mu^{n})
.
In addition, since the ideal
J
is an invertible
B
-module, the universal coefficient theorem implies an injection

\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}\overline{B}=\bigl(\varphi_{B}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\cdot\mu^{n})\bigr)\otimes_{B}\overline{B}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\cdot\mu^{n}).

On the other hand, by the complete projectivity assumption of
\overline{B}
over
R
, the finiteness of
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\cdot\mu^{n})
over
R
(
Proposition

6.31
.
(i)
), and the projection formula (
Theorem

4.12
.
(b)
), we see the following linearization map is an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\cdot\mu^{n})\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}\cdot\mu^{n}).

Hence by combining the above two equations, we obtain a canonical injection as in the statement.

Now we assume
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, and for simplicity we assume the rank of the local system
T
is
m
.
We apply the base change of the linearization isomorphism in
Theorem

6.25
.
(ii)
along
B\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B}}B\to\overline{B}
, to get an isomorphism of period sheaves on
X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
:

(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J))\otimes_{B}\overline{B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[1/\mu]\simeq T\otimes{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/\overline{B}}[1/\mu].

So by taking the global section and by the projection formula as above (
Theorem

4.12
.
(b)
), we get the expected isomorphism

(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J))\otimes_{B}\overline{B}[1/p]\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}\otimes_{R[1/p]}\overline{B}[1/p].

The last isomorphism in particular implies that
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
is a flat connection of rank
m
.
As a consequence, since
\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(T)
has rank
m
as well (
[
34
]
) and since the category of flat connections is abelian, we know the injection
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}\to\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(T)
is an isomorphism of flat connections.
∎

6.5.
Crystalline realization

In this section, we consider the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor for a collection of crystalline prisms in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
As before, we let
X
be a regular
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

Consider the following collection of crystalline prisms:

Definition 6.33
.

We define a crystalline prism
(D,(p))\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
to be
special
if either of the following is true:

(a)

The prism
(D,(p))
is of the form
(D_{B}(J),(p))
, where
(B,J)
is a prism
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
and
D_{B}(J)
is the
p
-complete
p
-adic divided power envelope for the surjection
B\to\overline{B}
.

(b)

The prism
(D,(p))
is of the form
(R_{0},(p))
, where the ring
R_{0}
is
p
-completely smooth over
W
, and the structural map
\Spec(R_{0}/pR_{0})\to X_{p=0}
can be lifted to a
p
-completely étale morphism
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0,\mathcal{O}_{K}})\to X
.

9
9

9

Informally, the ring
{\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{0})
is an unramified smooth model of an étale object over (the smooth locus of)
X
, equipped with a Frobenius structure.

Remark 6.34
.

By the identical arguments of
Example

2.10
, a special crystalline prism is in particular a flat crystalline prism in the sense of
Definition

2.20
.

We now calculate the global section of the prismatic period sheaves.

Proposition 6.35
.

Let
(D,(p))
be a special crystalline prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
The tautological maps below are isomorphisms:

D\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{D}}/D}),\quad D[1/p]\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{D}}/D}[\frac{1}{\mu}]).

Proof.

We first consider the case when
D=D_{B}(J)
for a special prism
(B,J)
.
As the statement is étale local on
X
, we may assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
is affine and connected, and following
4.5
we also let
\widetilde{X}_{\eta}={\mathrm{Spf}}(S[1/p],S)\to X_{\eta}
be the maximal connected Galois cover with Galois group
G_{X_{\eta}}
.
It then suffices to show that the tautological map
D\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{D}}/D}
induces an isomorphism onto
G_{X_{\eta}}
-invariant of the target.
By the base change formula of prismatic cohomology, the target
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{D}}/D}
is isomorphic to the base change of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
along the map of
prisms

(B,J)\to(D,(p))
.
Here we notice that the underlying map of rings
B\to D
is naturally factored as
B\xrightarrow{\text{incl}}D\xrightarrow{\varphi_{D}}D
,
where the first arrow is the canonical inclusion for the divided power envelope
B\to D=D_{B}(J)
.
So by interpreting
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{D}}/D}\simeq({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{p}
as the coproduct (
Corollary

2.25
), and by further identifying this coproduct as the divided power envelope (
[
7
, Cor. 2.39]
), we see
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{D}}/D}
is naturally isomorphic to the
p
-complete divided power envelope
D(S)
of the l.c.i surjection

({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)\otimes_{W}B)^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow S_{\overline{B}}.

Here the ring
D(S)
is naturally equipped with the divided power filtration, which is compatible with that of
D=D_{B}(J)
and is separated by the proof of
Lemma

5.17
.
So it suffices to show that the canonical map
D\to D(S)
induces an isomorphism
{\mathrm{gr}}^{i}D\simeq({\mathrm{gr}}^{i}D(S))^{G}_{X_{\eta}}
.

To continue, we notice that by the structure of the divided power envelope with respect to l.c.i surjections, the higher graded pieces are the divided powers of the first graded pieces, where the latter is the shifted (
p
-complete) cotangent complexes.
So we need to prove that

\overline{B}\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{\overline{B}}^{G_{X_{\eta}}},~\text{and}~J/J^{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)\otimes_{W}B)^{\wedge}_{p}}[-1]\bigr)^{G_{X_{\eta}}}.

The first equality follows from
Lemma

4.18
and the projection formula in
Theorem

4.12
.
(b)
, since
\overline{B}
is completely projective.
For the second map, we note that by the relateive perfectness of
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)
over
W
, the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(S)\otimes_{W}B)^{\wedge}_{p}}
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}
.
In addition, the factorization
B\to\overline{B}\to S_{\overline{B}}
naturally induces a distinguished triangle of finite projective
S_{\overline{B}}
-modules

\mathbb{L}_{\overline{B}/B}[-1]\otimes_{\overline{B}}S_{\overline{B}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[-1]\longrightarrow\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/R}\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[-1],

where by the functoriality
J/J^{2}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\overline{B}/B}[-1]
identifies the first term in the triangle as the base extension.
In particular, by the same reasoning above, the Galois invariant of
\mathbb{L}_{\overline{B}/B}[-1]\otimes_{\overline{B}}S_{\overline{B}}
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathbb{L}_{\overline{B}/B}[-1]
through the functoriality map.
So to finish the calculation, it suffices to notice that
\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/R}\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[-1]
has no
G_{X_{\eta}}
-invariant, thanks to vanishing result of
Proposition

4.15
.
(ii)
and the projection formula.

To calculate the global section for the
p
-inverted period sheaf, we first notice that the ring
D(S)[1/\mu]
, being an algebra over
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}}(S)[1/\mu]
, is
p
-inverted.
So to prove the equality
D[1/p]\simeq\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{D}}/D}[1/p])
, it suffices to check that map

\mu^{n+1}D(S)[1/p]\longrightarrow\mu^{n}D(S)[1/p]

induces an isomorphism on Galois invariants for each
n<0
.
In addition, by the separatedness of the divided power filtrations, we may do so by considering the Galois invariant of the graded pieces.
To continue, we use the calculation from
Proposition

5.22
to get that the
i
-th graded pieces of the cokernel of the above map is isomorphic to
S(n)\otimes_{S}{\mathrm{gr}}^{i-n}D(S)[1/p]\simeq S(n)\otimes_{S}\wedge^{i-n}\mathbb{L}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}[n-i][1/p]
(or zero if
i<n
).
Now we claim that the above module has no Galois invariant for each
n<0
.
By the calculation of the cotangent complex, the above
S_{\overline{B}}[1/p]
-module admits a finite filtration whose graded pieces are
\Gamma^{j}(\mathbb{L}_{\overline{B}/B}[-1])\otimes_{\overline{B}}\Gamma^{i-n-j}\bigl(\mathbb{L}_{S/R}\otimes_{R}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}[-1]\bigr)\otimes_{S}S(n)[1/p]
for
0\leq j\leq i-n
.
Note that the tensor product with
\Gamma^{\bullet}\mathbb{L}_{\overline{B}/B}[-1]
and
\overline{B}
have no influence on the vanishing of the Galois invariant, since both are completely projective
R
-modules.
So to check the vanishing, it suffices to recall that as in the proof of
Proposition

4.15
.
(ii)
, the Galois invariant of each each
\Gamma^{i-n-j}(\mathbb{L}_{S/R}[-1])\otimes_{S}S(n)[1/p]
is contained in
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},\widehat{\mathcal{O}}(n))
, which is zero for
n<0
.

Next, we consider the case when
D=R_{0}
is a smooth model of
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R)
over
W
, where the Frobenius structure
\varphi_{R_{0}}
is finite flat.
Under the assuption, to show that the canonical injection
D\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{R}_{0}}/R_{0}}^{G_{X_{\eta}}}
is isomorphic, it suffices to show it after a pullback along
\varphi_{R_{0}}
.
In particular, by the prismatic–crystalline comparison (
[
7
, Thm. 5.2]
), it suffices to consider the map
R_{0}\to R\Gamma_{\mathrm{crys}}(S_{\overline{R}_{0}}/R_{0})^{G_{X_{\eta}}}
.
Note that by
[
26
, Thm. 1.4]
and the crystalline–de Rham comparison, the crystalline cohomology
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{crys}}(S_{\overline{R}_{0}}/R_{0})
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)
.
In addition, the rational analogue
R_{0}[1/p]\to\bigl(\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)[1/\mu]\bigr)^{G_{X_{\eta}}}
is an isomorphism (
[
29
, Thm. 3.21]
).
As a consequence, since
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{A}_{{\mathrm{crys}},R_{0}}(S[1/p],S)^{G_{X_{\eta}}}
is a compact subalgebra of
R_{0}[1/p]
that contains
R_{0}
, the Galois invariant has to be
R_{0}
itself.
∎

We then notice that the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor satisfies the base change formula when the base prisms are special crystalline.

Lemma 6.36
.

Let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, and let
(D_{1},(p))\to(D_{2},(p))
be a completely projective map of crystalline prisms.
The linearization map
\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(D_{1},(p))\otimes_{D_{1}}D_{2}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(D_{2},(p))
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows immerdiately from the projection formula in
Theorem

4.12
.
(b)
.
∎

Corollary 6.37

(Compatibility with the crystalline Riemann–Hilbert functor)
.

Let
U\subset X
be an affine open subscheme, and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.

(i)

By ranging over all the special crystalline prisms
(D,(p))
such that
\overline{D}
is projective over
U_{p=0}
, the
D[1/p]
-modules
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(D,(p))
naturally glue to a crystal
\mathcal{F}_{n,U}
with a weak Frobenius structure over
U_{p=0}
.

(ii)

Assume
X
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Then the crystal
\mathcal{F}_{n,U}
is coherent, the inclusion map
\mathcal{F}_{n,U}\to\mathcal{F}_{n-1,U}
is an isogeny for
n\ll 0
, and the isocrystal
\colim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{F}_{n,U}
with weak Frobenius structure is naturally equivalent to
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{crys}},T}(U)
of Guo–Yang
[
29
, Thm. 1.10]
.

We expect the individual crystal
\mathcal{F}_{n,U}
to be finitely generated in the first case as well, though we do not pursue it in this article due to the mixed characteristic theme and the length of the article.

Proof.

The claim follows from the base change formula in
Lemma

6.36
and the prismatic–crystalline equivalence for coefficients in
[
28
, Thm. 6.4]
.
∎

Our next result relates the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor for transversal and crystalline prisms.

Proposition 6.38
.

Let
(D,(p))
be a special crystalline prism in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.

(i)

Assume
D=D_{B}(J)
for a framed regular prism
(B,J)
.
The linearization map below is injective

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}D\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(D,(p)).

(ii)

Assume
D=R_{0}
is a smooth model of
U\in X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
over
W
, and let
