\mathfrak{S}=R_{0}\llbracket u\rrbracket
be the associated Breuil–Kisin prism.
For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the mod
u
reduction map
\mathfrak{S}\to R_{0}
induces an injection

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(\mathfrak{S},(E(u)))\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}R_{0}\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(R_{0},(p)).

(iii)

Assume
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Then the above maps become isomorphic after taking the unions with respect to
n
and inverting
p
.
In particular, the
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{F}_{U}
in
Corollary

6.37
is canonically isomorphic to the crystalline realization of the prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
.

Proof.

For
(i)
, we let
\widehat{D}
be the filered completion of
D
with respect to the divided power filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}D=J^{[\geq\bullet]}
.
There is then a commutative diagram enlarging the linearization map

(72)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(D,(p))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}\widehat{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{1\otimes\Fil^{\bullet}D}),}

where the period sheaf
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{1\otimes\Fil^{\bullet}D}
is the filtered completion of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D
with respect to the divided power filtration on
D
.
So to show the injectivity of the claimed arrow, it suffices to show the injectivity of the rest of the arrows in (
72
).
Notice that since the map of ring
B\to D
is given by the composition
B\xrightarrow{\varphi_{B}}B\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}}D
(cf. proof of
Proposition

6.35
), the left column of (
72
) can be identified with the base change of
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
along
B\xrightarrow{\mathrm{incl}}D
.
So the injectivity of the left vertical arrow in (
72
) follows from the finiteness of the filtered module
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
over
J^{\bullet}B
(as in
Theorem

6.14
and
Corollary

6.21
), together with the separatedness of the filterer ring
\Fil^{\bullet}D
(
Lemma

5.17
).
For the right vertical arrow of (
72
), we notice that it is given by the global section of the map of sheaves
T\otimes\mu^{n}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{p}\to T\otimes\mu^{n}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{1\otimes\Fil^{\bullet}D}
.
We let
S=\overline{A}_{\mathrm{perf}}
be the reduction of the perfection of a framed regular prism
(A,I)
as in
Lemma

5.6
.
Then to show the injectivity of the right vertical arrow in (
72
), it suffices to show that the map of rings
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{p}\to({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}D)^{\wedge}_{1\otimes\Fil^{\bullet}D}
is injective: for the latter, by
Theorem

2.21
.
(iii)
, both source and target are completely free over
D
and
\widehat{D}
respectively, so it follows by the injectivity of
D\to\widehat{D}
in
Lemma

5.17
.
Finally, to prove that the bottom arrow in (
72
) is injective, by the filtered completeness, it suffices to check it for each graded piece.
The
i
-th graded piece of the bottom arrow is

\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}\overline{B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\Gamma^{i}(J/J^{2})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\otimes_{B}\overline{B}\otimes_{\overline{B}}\Gamma^{i}(J/J^{2})).

So by factoring out the invertible
\overline{B}
-module
\Gamma^{i}(J/J^{2})
, its injectivity follows from that of the de Rham realization functor in
Proposition

6.32
.
(i)
.

Part
(ii)
follows from the universal coefficient theorem, since the element
u
is regular in
\mathfrak{S}
and hence in
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}/\mathfrak{S}}
.
For
(iii)
, we notice that by
Theorem

6.25
, the pro-étale sheaf
T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
, where
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)
is a reflexive module over
B
.
By taking the base change along
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{D}}/D}
, the above map induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}D\otimes_{D}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{D}}/D}[1/\mu]\xrightarrow{\sim}T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{D}}/D}[1/\mu].

Hence the claim follows by taking the global section, thanks to
Proposition

6.35
and the finite projectivity of
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}D[1/p]
over
D[1/p]
.
∎

6.6.
Functoriality

In this subsection, we analyze how the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor and the integral de Rham functor interact with the pullback.
For crystalline local systems, we also consider its higher direct image along proper smooth morphisms.

We first consider the pullback for the flat connection
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},T}
.

Proposition 6.39

(Pullback and de Rham functor)
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.

(i)

The canonical filtered map
f^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}
is injective after inverting
p
.

(ii)

Assume
f^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
is
p
-torsionfree.
Then the above filtered map of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules is injective integrally.

Proof.

We let
U={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{2})\to V={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{1})
be an induced map of open affine subschemes in
Y
and
X
respectively, and consider the associated map of filtered
R_{2}[1/p]
-modules with connections (cf.
6.30
)

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(V)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}[1/p]\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(U)[1/p].

By the comparison with Liu–Zhu’s Riemann–Hilbert functor (
Proposition

6.31
.
(ii)
), the above map fits into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(V)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}[1/p]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(U)[1/p]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(T)(V)\otimes_{R_{1}[1/p]}R_{2}[1/p]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{dR}},Y_{\eta}}(f_{\eta}^{-1}T)(U),}

where the right vertical map is injective by
loc. cit.
and the bottom map is an isomorphism by
[
34
, Thm. 3.9.(ii)]
.
In addition, since
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(V)[1/p]\to\mathrm{D}_{{\mathrm{dR}},X_{\eta}}(V)
is an injection of flat connections over the smooth rigid space
X_{\eta}
, its pullback is also injective.
Hence by the commutative diagram above, we get the injectivity of the top horizontal map.

Now assume that
f^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
has no
p
-torsion,
By definition, the map
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(V)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(V)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}[1/p]
is injective.
On the other hand, the
R_{2}
-module
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(U)
by
Proposition

6.31
.
(ii)
has no
p
-torsion.
Hence the injectivity of
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(V)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(U)
follows from the injectivity after inverting
p
.
∎

Below we give some examples where the torsionfree assumption in
Proposition

6.39
.
(ii)
is satisfied.
We recall that a l.c.i morphism has
embedded codimension

r
if locally it can be factored as a composition of a smooth morphism followed by a regular closed immersion of codimension
r
.
We refer the reader to
[
26
, Def. 5.6]
for the precise definition and discussion.

Corollary 6.40
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a l.c.i. morphism of regular
p
-adic formal schemes satisfying either of the following conditions:

•

the map
f
is flat;

•

the embedded codimension of
f
is one.

Then for each
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
, the pullback
f^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
is
p
-torsionfree.
In particular, the canonical filtered map
f^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}
is injective.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}
has no
p
-torsions, it suffices to consider the case when
f:Y={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{2})\to X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{1})
is a regular closed immersion of codimension one: namely
R_{2}=R_{1}/gR_{1}
for a regular element
g\in R_{1}
.
Note that by our assumption, the quotient
R_{1}/gR_{1}
is
p
-torsionfree.
By shrinking
X
if necessary, we let
S
be a perfectoid faithfully flat
R_{1}
-algebra such that the generic fiber
{\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta}
covers
X_{\eta}
(
Lemma

5.6
).
Then one has
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(X)\subset T({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})\otimes\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/R_{1}}
, where the latter by the separatedness of the Hodge filtration (
Lemma

5.17
) is contained in
T({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}({\mathrm{Spf}}(S)_{\eta})}\mu^{n}\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{S/R_{1}}
.
Since each graded pieces of
\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{S/R_{1}}
is a finite projective
S
-module (
Proposition

2.24
) and since
S
is faithfully flat over
R_{1}
, the graded pieces of
\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{S/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{1}/gR_{1}
is flat over
R_{1}/gR_{1}
and has no
p
-torsion.
Hence by taking the limit, the entire filtered complete ring
\widehat{{\mathrm{dR}}}_{S/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{1}/gR_{1}
has no
p
-torsion, and so is its subring
{\mathrm{dR}}_{S/R_{1}}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{1}/gR_{1}
.
As a consequence, by the universal coefficient theorem, we obtain an injection

\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(X)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{1}/gR_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\eta},T\otimes\mu^{n}{\mathrm{dR}}_{(-)/X}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{1}/gR_{1}),

where the latter has no
p
-torsion.
Thus
\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(X)\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{1}/gR_{1}
is
p
-torsionfree.
∎

We now discuss the injectivity for Frobenius modules over a compatible map of prisms.
Below for given prisms
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
and
(B,J)\in Y_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
, we say a map of prisms
(A,I)\to(B,J)
is
over
a map of
p
-adic formal schemes
f:Y\to X
if the map of the reductions
\overline{A}\to\overline{B}
is compatible with
f
.

Theorem 6.41

(Pullback and prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor)
.

Let
(A,I)\to(B,J)
be a map of prisms over a l.c.i. map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes
f:Y\to X
and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Let
g:\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}B\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(B,J)
be the induced map of
B
-modules, and let
g^{(1)}=\varphi_{B}^{*}(g)
be the completed Frobenius twist.

Assume for now that
(A,I)\in X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is framed regular, that
\overline{B}
is completely projective over the image of
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})
in
Y
, and
\varphi_{B}
is completely projective:

(i)

The complete base change of
g^{(1)}
along
B\to B[1/p]^{\wedge}_{J}
is injective.

(ii)

The maps
g^{(1)}
and
g
are injective if the map
f
is flat.

Assume for now that
(A,I)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, and either
(B,J)\in Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
or
\varphi_{B}
is completely projective:

(iii)

The maps
g^{(1)}
and
g
are isomorphisms if either
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})
or
A\to B
is finite étale.

Here we recall that the assumptions for
(B,J)
in
Theorem

6.41
.(
(i)
and
(ii)
) are satisfied for example when
(B,J)
is a framed regular prism or its perfection over
Y
.
We also note that under the assumption of
Theorem

6.41
.
(iii)
, the map
f:Y\to X
is étale itself.

Proof.

As the claims are Zariski local on
Y
and
X
, we may assume
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{1})
and
Y={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{2})
. We first consider the Frobenius twist
g^{(1)}
.
By the
J
-completeness of the map
g^{(1)}
and
(g^{(1)}[1/p])^{\wedge}_{J}
, to show their injectivity, it suffices to show it for their mod
J
reductions.
We consider the following commutative diagram that relates the reduction of
g^{(1)}
with the base change of the integral de Rham functors

(73)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,f^{-1}T}(B,J)\otimes_{B}\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(X)\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,f^{-1}T}(Y)\otimes_{R_{2}}\overline{B}\bigr)^{\wedge}_{p},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{(1)}}

where we use the finiteness of
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(A,I)
to ensure the completeness of the left top object.
In the diagram, the right vertical arrow is injective thanks to the complete projectivity assumptions of
(B,J)
and
Proposition

6.32
.
For the bottom arrow, by
Lemma

4.9
,
Corollary

6.40
, and the complete projectivity of
\overline{B}
, we know it is injective if
f
is flat and is always injective after inverting
p
.
So to prove both
(i)
and
(ii)
, it suffices to prove the injectivity of the left vertical arrow in the two situations.

We now analyze the left vertical arrow.
By construction, the map is identical to the base change of the map of
\overline{A}
-modules
h\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}\overline{A}\to\mathcal{E}_{{\mathrm{dR}},n,T}(X)\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{A}
along
\overline{A}\to\overline{B}
, where
\overline{A}
is
p
-completely étale over
R_{1}
and is in particular topologically of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
Note that by
Proposition

6.32
, the map
h
is injective and is compatible with their flat connections after inverting
p
.
So if the map
f:Y\to X
is flat, then the base change
h\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}
is an injection of finite presented modules over
\overline{A}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}
.
For general
f
, since the injectivity of a map of flat connections over a rigid space is preserved under base changes, the map
h\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}[1/p]
is an injection of flat connections over the smooth rigid space
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2})_{\eta}
.
Hence by the complete projective assumption of
\overline{B}
over
R_{2}
and by taking the base change of
h\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}
along
\overline{A}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}\to\overline{B}
, we see the left vertical arrow in (
73
) is injective either when
f
is flat, or in general after inverting
p
.

To get the injectivity of
g
, we consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(B,J)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},n,f_{\eta}^{-1}T}(B,J),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{(1)}}

where the vertical maps are linearlization for
\varphi_{B}:B\to B
.
So the injectivity of
g
follows from that of
g^{(1)}
together with
Lemma

4.9
.

Finally, we assume
A\to B
is finite étale and prove that
g
is an isomorphism.
We first assume
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})
.
By
Theorem

6.25
. (
(i)
and
(ii)
), the weak
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}
is in fact a reflexive prismatic
F
-crystal and there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}[1/\mu]\xrightarrow{\sim}T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}[1/\mu]
.
In particular, we get a canonical isomorphism of sheaves of
B
-modules over
Y_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
through the pullback along the map of ringed sites
(Y_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu])\to(X_{\eta,{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}},{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{A}}/A}[1/\mu])
:

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}B\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\xrightarrow{\sim}f^{-1}T\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

So by taking the global section functor
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{0}(Y_{\eta},-)
and by
Corollary

5.31
, we get an isomorphism

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(A,I)\otimes_{A}B\simeq\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},f^{-1}T}(B,J).

Assume from now that
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
and
A\to B
is finite étale.
Under the assumption, both
(A,I)
and
(B,J)
are framed regular prisms over
X
, so
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})
is étale over
X
and
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})
is étale over
Y
.
In particular, we know the map
f
is étale itself.
Notice that by the localization property in
Proposition

6.15
, we may assume the reduction
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})
is surjective onto
X={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{1})
and similarly for
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to Y={\mathrm{Spf}}(R_{2})
.
Thus by taking the composition, it suffices to discuss two separate cases: in the first case
\overline{B}
is the base change of
\overline{A}
along a finite étale map
f
, and in the second case
X=Y
.
Moreover, by the injectivity of
g
in
(ii)
for flat maps, we may assume
f
is finite Galois.

As in
4.5
, we let
\widetilde{X}={\mathrm{Spf}}(S)\to X
be the integral model of a maximal connected Galois cover of the generic fiber, let
G=G_{X_{\eta}}
be the Galois group, and let
H
be the kernel of
G\to{\mathrm{Gal}}(Y/X)
.
So the canonical map
g
can be translated to the following map of Galois invariants:

(74)

(T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{1}}{\overline{A}}/A})^{G}\otimes_{A}B\longrightarrow(T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{2}}{\overline{B}}/B})^{H}.

In the first case when
\overline{B}=\overline{A}\otimes_{R_{1}}R_{2}
, the tensor product
S\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{A}
is equal to
S\otimes_{R_{2}}\overline{B}
.
Moreover, by
[
28
, Lem. 7.12]
, the étaleness implies that the relative prismatic cohomology
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{2}}\overline{B}/B}={{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{A}/B}
is naturally isomorphic to
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{1}}\overline{A}/A}
.

10
10

10

Though the statement in
loc. cit.
assume the smoothness, the arguments apply verbatimally in general.

We let
M
be the
A
-module
\bigl(T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{1}}{\overline{A}}/A}\bigr)^{H}
with the induced
G/H
-action.
Then (
74
) can be rewritten as a map of
B
-modules

M^{G/H}\otimes_{A}B\to M.

Note that by
[
7
, Lem. 2.18]
, the map
{\mathrm{Spf}}(B)\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(A)
is also a finite Galois
G/H
-cover that is compatible with
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B})\to{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{A})
.
Thus the claim follows from the usual Galois descent.

We then consider the second case when
X=Y
and
A\to B
is finite étale.
Note that since
B
is finite projective over
A
and has the trivial
G
-action, by the projection formula for finite projective modules, we may replace the source of (
74
) as below

(75)

(T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{1}}{\overline{A}}/A}\otimes_{A}B)^{G}\longrightarrow(T(\widetilde{X}_{\eta})\otimes{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S\otimes_{R_{2}}\overline{B}/B})^{H}.

So the map in (
75
) is an isomorphism, thanks to the base change property of relative prismatic cohomology.
∎

In the special case when
Y=X
, we yield a change of prisms property.

Corollary 6.42
.

Let
(B_{1},J_{1})\to(B_{2},J_{2})
be a map of two prisms over
X
, and let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Assume
(B_{1},J_{1})
is framed regular, the map
\varphi_{B_{2}}
is completely projective, and
{\mathrm{Spf}}(\overline{B}_{2})
is completely projective over its image in
X
.
The induced map of weak Frobenius modules
\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B_{1},J_{1})\otimes_{B_{1}}B_{2}\to\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}(B_{2},J_{2})
is injective.

Note that one can construct a natural pullback functor for presheaves of weak Frobenius modules.

Construction 6.43
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.
For a weak prismatic (
F
-)crystal
\mathcal{E}\in{\mathrm{wCrys}}^{(\varphi)}(X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
, we define the
pullback of
\mathcal{E}
to be the functor on
Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
such that

Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}\ni(B,J)\longmapsto(f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{F})(B,J)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lim(\mathcal{E}(A,I)\otimes_{A}B^{\prime})^{\wedge}_{(p,I)},

where the limit is ranging over all the diagrams
\bigl((B,J)\to(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})\leftarrow(A,I)\bigr)
such that
(B,J)\to(B^{\prime},J^{\prime})
is completely flat and
(A,I)
is completely flat over
X
.
Here we note that the index category of the limit is in fact filtered thanks to
Theorem

2.21
.

So applying
Theorem

6.41
and the limit formula in
Lemma

6.5
, we obtain the following pullback compatibility.

Corollary 6.44
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, let
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Assume either
T\in\Loc_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{\eta})
or
f
is finite étale.
There is a canonical isomorphism of weak prismatic
F
-crystals on
Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}

f_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},T}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},f^{-1}T}.

Finally, we show that the prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor is naturally compatible with the derived direct image for
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
Before the statement, we recall that
(-)_{\mathrm{tf}}
is defined as the
p
-torsionfree quotient.
For an analytically locally free coherent prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{F}
over a regular
p
-adic formal scheme, we define its
saturation

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
to be the intersection of
\mathcal{F}[1/p]\cap\mathcal{F}[1/\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}]
inside of
\mathcal{F}[1/p\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}]
.
By
Corollary

3.6
and the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is analytically locally free, the saturation is also equal to the reflexive hull of
\mathcal{F}
.
In particular, the cokernel of
\mathcal{F}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is supported at the non-analytic locus
V(p,\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
in
X_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
and is killed by a power of
p
.

Proposition 6.45

(Pushforward and prismatic Riemann–Hilbert functor)
.

Let
g:X\to Z
be a smooth proper map of regular
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, and let
T\in\Loc^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{\eta})
.
For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a canonical injection of coherent prismatic
F
-crystal over
Z_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
:

(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},(R^{i}g_{\eta,*}T)_{\mathrm{tf}}},

which identifies the target as the saturation of the source.

Proof.

By the Strong Étale Comparison of Guo–Reinecke in
Theorem

6.23
, we know there is a canonical Frobenius equivariant isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves over
Z_{\eta}

(76)

(Rg_{\eta,*}T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(Rg_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})[1/\mu].

So for each prism
(B,J)\in Z_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, the base change along
{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]
induces a Frobenius equivariant isomorphism

(Rg_{\eta,*}T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\simeq{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}(Rg_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})[1/\mu]\otimes_{{\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}}[1/\mu]}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

Moreover, by applying the crystal property of
Rg_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E}
along the maps of prisms
({\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}},\ker(\widetilde{\theta}))\to({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B},I_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}})\leftarrow(B,J)
, the right hand side above is naturally isomorphic to

(Rg_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

So by combining the above two identifications, we obtain a natural isomorphism

(Rg_{\eta,*}T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\simeq(Rg_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

The
i
-th cohomology yields a canonical isomorphism of pro-étale sheaves over
Z_{\eta}

(77)

(R^{i}g_{\eta,*}T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\simeq(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu].

Now, by the proper smooth assumption of
g:X\to Z
, the derived direct image of
\mathcal{E}
is a prismatic
F
-crystal in perfect complexes (
[
28
, Cor. 5.16, Rmk. 8.11]
) and each
R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E}
is a coherent prismatic
F
-crystal over
Z_{\eta}
.
Moreover, as in the proof of
Theorem

6.23
, the prismatic
F
-crystal
R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E}
is locally free after inverting
p
.
In addition, by taking the
p
-completion at the
i
-th cohomology of (
76
), we see the
p
-torsionfree quotient
(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}
becomes locally free after base change along
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\langle 1/\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\rangle
.
As a consequence, we know
(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}
is analytically locally free and is contained in its saturation, where the latter is a reflexive coherent prismatic
F
-crystal over
Z
.
In particular, by
Theorem

5.30
, we know for each special prism
(B,J)\in Z_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
, there is an injection

(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}(B,J)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\bigl(Z_{\eta},(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}(B,J)\otimes_{B}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\bigr)

that induces an isomorphism after inverting either
p
or
I
.
Hence by combining this injection with (
77
), we obtain a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{E}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},(R^{i}g_{\eta,*}T)_{\mathrm{tf}}}(B,J)=\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\bigl(Z_{\eta},(R^{i}g_{\eta,*}T)_{\mathrm{tf}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{(-)_{\overline{B}}/B}[1/\mu]\bigr)\simeq(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf},{\mathrm{sat}}}(B,J),

where
(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf},{\mathrm{sat}}}
is the saturation of
(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}
and is equal to the intersection
(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}[1/p]\cap(R^{i}g_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},*}\mathcal{E})_{\mathrm{tf}}[1/\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}]
.
So the statement follows by ranging over
(B,J)\in Z_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{sp}}}
.
∎
